# nirs4all 1.4.0 — Reviewed bug audit (2026-10-05)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Date / review** | 2026-10-05; initial audit reviewed and missing areas supplemented on the same date |
| **Status** | Audit/review complete within the stated evidence limits; findings remain open. No production code or tests changed. |
| **Audited revision** | `49ee7d7bf8a7ab307436a3d4a7d0cf298bbc23b1` on `main`, package 1.4.0; focused probes used this checkout and compatible temporary dependencies. Unrelated uncommitted changes were isolated when attributing test failures. |
| **Owner** | Maintainers; ownership per repair item remains to be assigned |
| **Requested scope** | Validate the initial 265 claims, finish the missing execution/visualization review, update this document; implementation comes later |
| **Maintainer direction** | Legacy is planned for deprecation: retain mentions, fix only major defects or genuinely local 1–2-line corrections |

## 0. How to read this document

There are **279 retained IDs**: the original 265 plus **14 new findings**. The re-audit rejects **2** claims as bugs and groups **6** duplicates under canonical IDs. **271 active review items** remain: 2 critical, 50 high, 128 medium and 91 low. Qualified items include contract/documentation limitations and are not automatic instructions to change behavior.

* **Original-claim verdicts:** 156 freshly reproduced, 57 source-confirmed, 44 qualified, 6 duplicates, 2 rejected.
* **VALIDATED / reproduced** means a fresh observable witness in this review, sometimes at helper level. **VALIDATED / source** means current code/caller evidence without a new execution claim. **QUALIFIED** means the behavior/limitation remains relevant but the initial scope, scientific interpretation or proposed correction was overstated. Its revised description is authoritative.
* Every initial ID retains its historical evidence, explicitly labelled as such. Original throw-away scripts were unavailable; their claimed outputs are **not** inherited as fresh reproduction evidence. The re-audit result and current confidence replace the initial conclusions.
* **Default routing:** general `run()` chooses `native` for supported portable Archive V2 requests and `dag-ml` otherwise before execution. `legacy` is an explicit rollback lane, never an implicit fallback. “Default = dag-ml” in historical repro comments is not a universal contract.
* **Severity** measures impact, not the commitment to fix. Critical/high legacy defects remain eligible when they corrupt scientific results, leak validation data, lose artifacts/data or block a common valid workflow. Legacy minor items are mention-only unless a verified correction actually fits in **1–2 lines**, stays local and requires no refactor. Line count alone does not justify a contract change.
* No fixes are made in this document update. 18 active critical/high items are attributed to the legacy/controller path; 38 legacy minor items carry the mention-only rule and its small-fix exception. Shared operators, storage and config helpers are not automatically exempt merely because a witness used legacy.
* IDs remain stable for issues/PRs. Source paths refer to the library checkout. Areas `EXE` (execution/lifecycle) and `QA` (verification) are new. Fresh inline repros are provided for the new leakage and categorical encoding defects; other trigger/output details are recorded per finding.

### 1.2 Decisions made by the re-audit

* **Rejected:** DGB-04 is intentional refusal of missing physical wavelengths; do not invent nanometer coordinates or add implicit legacy fallback. VIZ-21 is documented preset precedence; `preset=None` enables manual options.
* **Canonical grouping:** API-10 → SYN-19; SYN-02 → API-09; DT2-11 → DT1-06; DT1-23 → CFG-19; DT2-21 → OPO-16; AOM-02 → OPO-01. The EMSC item includes two separate copied implementations; grouping does not remove either repair location.
* **Scope corrections:** axis-0 SNV is batch-dependent/stateless, not proved supervised leakage; relation/repetition_spec joins do execute; not every config key or generate kwarg is lost; positive FastAOM Ridge penalties shrink coefficients; warning filters do not suppress literally every possible warning.
* **Correction constraints:** preserve categorical-list semantics when repairing tuple ranges (CFG-10); retain CV evidence for model selection (DT1-10) rather than ranking on held-out test targets; do not change legitimate outlier/statistical defaults based only on inaccurate docs (OPO-17/20).
* **Priority changes:** VIZ-09 rises medium → high for cross-dataset artifact loss; API-09 inherits high from the merged SYN-02 axis-loss item; STO-16 rises low → medium after a supported JAX export/reload witness. AOM-01 falls high → medium because its initial universal no-shrinkage claim is false.
* **New major evidence:** EXE-01 proves supervised validation leakage in legacy CV; DT2-22 proves silently inconsistent class IDs across train/test CSVs. EXE-02/03 expose legacy failure/lifecycle reporting, CTM-17 exposes ignored stacking validation, VIZ-26/27 expose diagram defects, VIZ-28 exposes an EVR scoring error, EXE-04 through EXE-06 expose shared diagnostic/metadata defects, EXE-07 exposes omitted model topology in legacy refit inputs, and QA-01 records stale public-contract expectations.

### 1.3 Cross-cutting causes retained for triage

Metric direction remains duplicated across replay, prediction maintenance, optimization and stacking. Fit/transform asymmetry affects EMSC/EPO and several contracts. Mutable input/cache state affects DGA-01 and CTM-01. Sample-ID versus position confusion affects CTM-02 and CTD-02/03. Storage refcounts and non-atomic operations affect STO-01/02/06. Public normalization/argument handling affects API-03/04/05, DT2-03 and SYN-19. These are cross-references, not additional findings.

## 3. Audit method, coverage and limits

The initial audit reported 16 areas and extensive full-file reading. This re-audit independently checks **every original ID** against current source, upstream/downstream callers, tests and documented contracts. Fresh deterministic/public-API or focused helper witnesses are distinguished from source confirmation. Three parallel reviews covered controllers/visualization, operators/config and data/storage/synthesis; the lead reviewed DAG/API behavior, routing, the missing execution area and cross-area duplicates. Historical assertions of exhaustive full-file coverage are not repeated as fresh evidence.

The initially missing `pipeline/execution/`, `pipeline/steps/`, top-level runner/predictor/retrainer/resolver/engine paths and their public callers received targeted review. All five pipeline/analysis modules were checked against operator/config semantics and Studio/workspace callers. Stacking validation and PipelineDiagram parsing/rendering bounds received additional review. Native witness/training/lineage, tuning validators, synthesis fitting parameter/RNG paths, robustness rendering/CLI and transfer helpers were checked at affected contracts and callers. DGA-06 and API-02 now have fresh legacy orchestration witnesses. This completes the requested audit supplement, **not a proof that every possible pipeline, numerical regime or source line is bug-free**.

Remaining limits: real TabPFN weights/network/GPU were not invoked; unwired vendored GPU code is source-only; only selected historical TensorFlow architectures were exercised; physical multi-sensor noise conclusions are qualified; diagram QA used parser/coordinate/text witnesses rather than pixel comparison. Reproductions with synthetic fixtures validate the described trigger, not performance on every scientific dataset.

### 3.1 Runtime used for the re-audit

The existing `.venv` was left unchanged. A temporary `pip --target --no-deps` overlay supplied the compatible versions below; source imports were forced by `PYTHONPATH`. Installed distribution metadata still said nirs4all 1.3.3, but the imported library source was 1.4.0. Python 3.11.15, NumPy 2.4.6, SciPy 1.17.1, scikit-learn 1.9.1, pytest 9.1.1 were used.

| Dependency | Initial .venv | Re-audit overlay |
|---|---:|---:|
| dag-ml | 0.3.32 | 0.3.34 |
| dag-ml-data | previous installed version | 0.2.12 |
| nirs4all-io | 0.2.2 | 0.2.4 |
| nirs4all-core | 0.4.0 | 0.4.1 |
| nirs4all-methods / pls4all | 1.2.1 | 1.3.2 |

Commands below use `$AUDIT_RUNTIME` for that temporary dependency directory and `$AUDIT_TMP` for scratch space. These are placeholders to set locally, not dependencies or generated workspaces committed to the repository. `PYTHONPATH="$PWD:$AUDIT_RUNTIME"`, `TMPDIR="$AUDIT_TMP"`, `OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1`, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`, `MPLBACKEND=Agg` were used for the broad tests. The initial upgraded-test attempt stopped on disk exhaustion and is excluded; completed runs used RAM-backed scratch space.

### 3.2 Mechanical checks and failure attribution

| Check | Result | Interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/unit -n 6 --tb=short -o addopts='' --basetemp="$AUDIT_TMP/pytest-unit"` | **10021 passed, 21 failed**, 551.26 s | Every failure traverses the unrelated local methods_multimodal import of missing nirs4all_io.public_content. They are not added as library bugs in this audit. |
| Same four failing unit files against a `git archive HEAD` source/test snapshot, with the same overlay | **101 passed**, 3.48 s | Confirms those failures are attributable to local uncommitted work. |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/integration/pipeline tests/regression tests/e2e -n 4 --tb=short -o addopts='' --basetemp="$AUDIT_TMP/pytest-extra"` | **489 passed, 3 failed**, 521.19 s | All failures are the frozen public-contract expectations. |
| `tests/regression/test_public_api_contract.py` in pristine HEAD snapshot | **25 passed, 3 failed**, 1.15 s | Reproduces QA-01 independently of local work. |
| `.venv/bin/python -m ruff check nirs4all` | **Passed** | Configured repository rules; the initial nonconfigured bug-rule lint dump is not a bug list. |
| `.venv/bin/python -m mypy nirs4all`, working tree | **12 errors in 3 files**, 584 files checked | Only unrelated new api/public_results.py and cli/commands/workflow.py,tuning.py. No changes made to them. |
| Same mypy command on pristine HEAD source | **Passed**, 579 files checked | Committed source has a clean configured type gate in this environment. |
| Existing storage/bundle/trace tests | **651 passed** | Focused baseline run; overlaps the full unit suite. |
| Existing data tests (including synthesis) | **2256 passed** | Focused baseline run; overlaps the full unit suite. |
| Focused stacking/branch/cache/merge/tag tests | **297 passed** | Focused baseline run; overlaps the full unit suite. |
| Focused pipeline/analysis tests | **59 passed**, 1.05 s | Overlaps unit suite; existing train-size expectation is itself wrong (EXE-04). |

The four HEAD-isolated files were `tests/unit/pipeline/dagml/test_methods_multimodal.py`, `tests/unit/pipeline/dagml/test_structural_classification.py`, `test_structural_multimodal.py` and `test_structural_topology.py`. Existing successes do not invalidate the separate bug witnesses: several defects have no matching regression fixture. Overlapping focused runs are not summed into a unique test total. No fixture/golden was regenerated.

Unrelated work present during the audit included changes to package/API exports, CLI, methods_multimodal and its tests, plus new public results/tuning/workflow code and tests. The document update preserves those files. The initial audit’s 99 failures/8 errors from old dependencies are superseded by the correctly provisioned checks above.

## 5. Findings by area

### 5.1 DAG execution paths (dag-ml and named native helpers) — `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/`

#### Run orchestration (DGA)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

The seven DGA claims and their callers were reviewed with the required dependency overlay. Public augmentation, refit flags, sibling parameter handling, checkpoint splits and subprocess quoting were freshly probed. DGA-06 is explicitly legacy and is now reproduced.

##### DGA-01 — dag-ml engine mutates the caller's live `SpectroDataset` (augmentation rows added, partitions rewritten); the next `run()` on that object crashes

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh public dag-ml run grows the live dataset from 60 to 180 rows; subsequent plain CV raises IndexError. Input aliasing is real; path/config inputs materialize fresh objects.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/dataset.py:48-49` (`_materialize_dataset`), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/run_paths.py:2114-2115` (`_run_augmentation`), also `run_paths.py:2029-2035`, `run_paths.py:1734` (`_run_augmentation_full_train`), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/folds.py:211` (`lower_fold_file_holdout`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `_materialize_dataset` returns the user's `SpectroDataset` object itself (no copy), and `api/run.py` does not copy it on the default `dag-ml` path. `_run_augmentation` then calls `_apply_sample_augmentation(aug_step, spectro, ...)` (and `_apply_pre_augmentation_steps`) directly on that object, so the synthetic children are appended to the caller's dataset. `lower_fold_file_holdout` likewise rewrites the caller's train/test partitions via `spectro._indexer.update_by_indices`. The legacy engine leaves the dataset untouched. A `DatasetConfigs` or path input is unaffected because `get_dataset_at(0)` returns a fresh object each call.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
ds = nirs4all.generate.regression(n_samples=60, random_state=1)
pipe = [{"sample_augmentation": {"transformers": [GaussianAdditiveNoise(sigma=0.01)], "count": 2}}, KFold(3), {"model": PLSRegression(3)}]
nirs4all.run(pipe, ds, engine="legacy", ...)  -> ds.num_samples == 60 (unchanged)
nirs4all.run(pipe, ds)                        -> ds.num_samples == 156 ; second run -> 252
plain = [KFold(3), {"model": PLSRegression(3)}]; nirs4all.run(plain, ds)   # ds already augmented
  File ".../pipeline/dagml/folds.py", line 38, in _pool_targets: block = block[order]
  IndexError: index 48 is out of bounds for axis 0 with size 48
Fold-file holdout: SpectroDataset(60 train, 0 test) + {"split": "fold.json"} -> after run: train 40, test 20.
```

- Impact: A notebook/script that reuses one `SpectroDataset` object (very common) after a `sample_augmentation` run gets (a) a crash (`IndexError`) in the next plain CV run, because the CV pool now contains augmented children while `y(..., include_augmented=False)` does not, and (b) silent compounding augmentation on the next augmented run (156 -> 252 rows). `_attach_export_spec` then deep-copies the already-augmented dataset into the export spec. A single-fold fold-file `split` permanently moves the validation IDs of the caller's dataset into the test partition.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): In `run_via_dagml` (or `_materialize_dataset`), `copy.deepcopy` a caller-supplied `SpectroDataset` before any in-place step (augmentation, pre-augmentation transforms, fold-file holdout, chart flags).

##### DGA-02 — `refit=False` + the documented sibling-key sweep (`{"model": PLS(), "n_components": {"_range_": ...}}`) is rejected as "unsupported shape" because the host drops the swept values

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh public CV-only sibling sweep rejects unsupported_shape, and per-variant extraction returns four empty dictionaries. Nested model.params syntax is outside this defect.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/run_backend.py:818-824` (`_native_param_variant_model_params`), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/run_paths.py:287-288` (`_run_native_generation`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `PipelineConfigs` expands a sibling-key sweep into variants of the form `{'model': 'sklearn...PLSRegression', 'n_components': 1}` (the model is a bare string and the swept value is a sibling key). `_native_param_variant_model_params` only reads `model["params"]` / `model.get_params()`, so every variant yields `{}`. `_native_param_config_map_from_catalog` therefore matches nothing, and the CV-only guard fires.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
run_backend.py:822  model_params = dict(model["params"]) if isinstance(model, dict) and ... else (model.get_params() if hasattr(model, "get_params") else {})
run_paths.py:287    if not refit and variant_config_names and len(catalog_map) != len(variant_config_names):
run_paths.py:288        raise DagMlUnsupported("native CV-only parameter sweep did not expose an unambiguous variant-to-parameter mapping")
_native_param_variant_model_params([KFold(3), {"model": PLSRegression(), "n_components": {"_range_": [1,6,1]}}], "") -> [{}, {}, {}, {}, {}, {}]
nirs4all.run([KFold(3), {"model": PLSRegression(), "n_components": {"_range_": [1,4,1]}}], (X, y), refit=False)
  -> RtError [run/unsupported_shape] ... did not expose an unambiguous variant-to-parameter mapping
```

- Impact: The default engine refuses a plain CV-only run of a documented sweep syntax (used in `examples/developer`, parity tests only exercise `refit=True`), with a misleading unsupported-shape error instead of running. With `refit=True` the same lost parameters disable content-based winner labelling (`_native_param_winner_config_name` needs non-empty params), so the winner's rows are labelled with variant 0's `config_name` regardless of which `n_components` won.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Build the per-variant params from the non-reserved sibling keys of the expanded model step (the same keys `_apply_model_params` applies) in addition to `model["params"]`.

##### DGA-03 — `refit=False` is silently ignored for per-branch prediction-aggregation stacking (refit still runs, metadata claims it did not)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: With 60 train +12 held-out rows, refit=False still returns final rows while refit_enabled=False. Without test rows final score rows alone do not prove/refute an actual REFIT.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/run_backend.py:1341-1353` (guard), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/run_backend.py:1610-1617` (`_dispatch_run`, `detected_proba_stacking` call)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: The `refit is False` guard lists every composition that cannot run CV-only (`detected_stacking`, `detected_by_source`, ...) but omits `detected_proba_stacking`. The `_run_stacking_branch(...)` call for that shape also omits `refit=refit` (every sibling call passes it), so the default `refit=True` is used.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
pipe = [KFold(3), {"branch": [[{"model": PLS(2)}], [{"model": Ridge()}]]},
        {"merge": {"predictions": [{"branch": 0, "aggregate": "mean"}, {"branch": 1, "aggregate": "mean"}]}}, {"model": Ridge(alpha=0.5)}]
detect: proba=True seq=False stack=False
run(refit=True)  fold_ids ['0','1','2','avg','final','w_avg']
run(refit=False) fold_ids ['0','1','2','avg','final','w_avg']   per_dataset: {'refit_enabled': False, ...}
```

- Impact: A user who asks for CV-only (`refit=False`) with a structured `{"merge": {"predictions": [...]}}` stack still pays for the full refit and gets `final` rows (including held-out test predictions), while `per_dataset["refit_enabled"]` says `False`. Other shapes raise `DagMlUnsupported` instead.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Add `detected_proba_stacking is not None` to the `refit is False` guard (or pass `refit=refit` and implement CV-only lowering).

##### DGA-04 — `{"split": KFold(...)}` dict splitter crashes with `AttributeError` in the checkpoint-around-duplication-branch paths

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh checkpoint/duplication pipeline with dict splitter raises AttributeError: dict has no split. Scope is the checkpoint paths, not all dict splitters.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/run_paths.py:2498-2501` (`_run_checkpoint_before_duplication_branch`), `run_paths.py:2566-2569` (`_run_checkpoint_inside_duplication_feature_merge`), `run_paths.py:1887-1889` (`_run_interleaved_augmentation_checkpoints`, same pattern, confirmed by reading)
- Category: correctness
- Description: These functions take the splitter as the raw `pipeline[0]` / `splitter_steps[0]` and pass it to `_build_folds`. The detectors accept it through `_is_split_step`, which also admits the dict form `{"split": splitter, ...}`. Every other path first parses the dict with `_split_pipeline` into a `DagMlSplitStep`; here the dict reaches `splitter.split(...)`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
run_paths.py:2498  splitter = pipeline[0]
run_paths.py:2501  folds = _build_folds(splitter, spectro, pool, set())
[KFold(3), {"model": PLS(2)}, {"branch": [[SNV()],[MSC()]]}, {"model": Ridge()}]            -> ok
[{"split": KFold(3)}, {"model": PLS(2)}, {"branch": [[SNV()],[MSC()]]}, {"model": Ridge()}] -> AttributeError: 'dict' object has no attribute 'split'  (folds.py:165)
[{"split": KFold(3)}, {"branch": [[SNV()],[MSC()]]}, {"model": PLS(2)}, {"merge": "features"}, {"model": Ridge()}] -> same (run_paths.py:2569)
```

- Impact: The documented dict split syntax (also the only way to pass `group_by`) works for the equivalent single-model shapes but crashes with an uncaught `AttributeError` (not a `DagMlUnsupported`) on these multi-checkpoint shapes.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Obtain the splitter through `_split_pipeline(pipeline)` (as the sibling paths do) instead of `pipeline[0]` / `splitter_steps[0]`.

##### DGA-05 — Positional sweep-label fallback counts the `w_avg` report's `None` variant id as a CV variant, shifting config names and leaving the last variant unlabelled

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Minimal report set with w_avg variant_id=None maps winner to config1 and None to config2, dropping loser entirely. Positional fallback bug independently reproduced.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/result.py:185` (`_native_variant_config_map`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `cv_variant_ids` is built from validation reports with `fold_id != "avg"`. The winner also emits an untagged `(None, "validation", "w_avg")` report, so `None` enters the list and consumes a config name. `_scores_to_run_result` itself excludes both (`fold_id not in ("avg", "w_avg")`, result.py:463), and nothing ever reads the `None` key.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
result.py:185  ... if report["partition"] == "validation" and report.get("fold_id") != "avg"))
4-variant n_components `_range_` sweep (refit=True):
  map = {'variant:8feeed..': 'config_d97d6105', None: 'config_24cfa01a', 'variant:3fd8..': 'config_02151728', 'variant:2431..': 'config_5948f253'}
  rows: 'config_d97d6105' (winner), 'config_02151728', 'config_5948f253', ''   <- 4th variant has config_name ''
```

- Impact: In the positional fallback (every `_range_`/`_log_range_` sibling sweep, because of DGA-02), N variants get N-1 labels: the last variant's rows have a blank `config_name` and the rest are shifted by one, so grouping/filtering predictions by `config_name` is wrong. The same shift affects the `winner_config_name` branch (`cv_variant_ids[0]` is correct, the remaining ids are shifted).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Use `fold_id not in ("avg", "w_avg")` and drop `None` ids, matching `_scores_to_run_result`.

##### DGA-06 — (legacy lane, outside the assigned area) sibling hyperparameters on a model step are silently ignored by the legacy engine

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh legacy run sibling n_components=5 gives CV RMSE 0.5934747, exactly explicit n_components=2, whereas explicit 5 gives 0.0711698. This closes the initial unverified orchestration claim.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/steps/parser.py:148-170` (`StepParser._parse_dict_step`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: For `{"model": PLSRegression(), "n_components": 5}` the parser selects `model` as the workflow key and keeps only `step.get("params", {})` as metadata; the non-reserved sibling key `n_components` is never applied. The dag-ml engine applies it (`steps._apply_model_params` docstring states "nirs4all applies the non-reserved siblings to the model via set_params").
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
manual pooled-OOF RMSE: n_components=2 -> 9.4329, n_components=5 -> 5.4812
run([KFold(3), {"model": PLSRegression(), "n_components": 5}], (X, y), engine="legacy") -> 9.4329   (default n=2 used)
same with engine="dag-ml"                                                                -> 5.4812
legacy `_range_` sibling sweep [1..6]: all six configs score 9.4329 (identical)
```

- Impact: With `engine="legacy"` (or the dual/compare lane) a sibling-key hyperparameter or sweep silently trains the default model; all sweep variants tie, so "best" is arbitrary. This also makes the dag-ml code treat these sweeps as "degenerate" and label the winner with variant 0's name (see DGA-02/05).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Apply non-reserved sibling keys to the model (clone + `set_params`) in the legacy model path, or reject them loudly.

##### DGA-07 — Subprocess launcher shim does not quote interpreter/adapter paths (fails with exit 127 when a path contains a space)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Launcher with a real executable symlink under my env exits127 at whitespace. Interpreter and adapter paths need shell quoting.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/cli_runner.py:251` and `cli_runner.py:253` (`write_launcher_shim`)
- Category: other
- Description: `venv_python` and the adapter file path are interpolated unquoted into a `#!/bin/sh` script, so a path containing whitespace is word-split. This is the launcher for the dag-ml-cli subprocess lane (used when the in-process extension is absent or `N4A_DAGML_INPROCESS=0`).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
f'  exec {venv_python} {adapter_file} "$@"\n'
f'exec {venv_python} -m nirs4all.pipeline.dagml.process_adapter "$@"\n'
write_launcher_shim(p, ".../my env/bin/python"); subprocess.run([shim, "--describe"]) -> rc 127, "exec: .../my: not found"
```

- Impact: Any run on the subprocess lane from a venv or checkout whose path contains a space (typical on macOS/Windows home directories) fails before the first task.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Wrap both interpolated paths with `shlex.quote`.

#### Node runner, native lane, replay, lowerers (DGB)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

The four DGB claims were checked against spectral-coordinate and runtime contracts. DGB-01/02 have fresh runtime witnesses; DGB-03 is a manifest-helper proof. DGB-04 is rejected as a bug. Fail-closed protocol checks are intentional.

##### DGB-01 — Strict wavelength-requiring operators get a cm-1 axis (legacy: nm): `TemperatureAugmenter` & co. silently become no-ops on the dag-ml engine

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Dataset nm axis is encoded as cm-1 then forwarded to TemperatureAugmenter. Fresh coordinate wrapper gives maximum spectral change0 vs0.561326 with correct nm coordinates.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/envelope.py:83` (`_numeric_feature_axis`), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/node_runner.py:201` (`_CoordinateTransform.fit`), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/node_runner.py:222` (`_coordinate_chain`); same root cause at `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/run_paths.py:1491`
- Category: correctness
- Description: The only feature-axis contract the dag-ml engine carries is `wavelengths_cm1` (`feature_axis_cm1`). `_CoordinateTransform.fit` forwards it verbatim as `wavelengths=` to every operator for which `_needs_wavelength_injection` is true, which includes all operators with `_requires_wavelengths is True` (environmental / scattering / edge-artifact augmenters). The legacy `TransformerMixinController._extract_wavelengths` (`controllers/transforms/transformer.py:166`) gives these operators `dataset.wavelengths_nm(...)`. These operators hard-code nm band ranges (e.g. `TEMPERATURE_REGION_PARAMS` 1400-2000 nm), so with a 10000-4000 axis no region matches and the operator returns its input unchanged. (Resampler/CARS legitimately use cm-1 in legacy too, so only the strict-flag operators diverge.)
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# node_runner.py:201
self.transformer.fit(X, y, wavelengths=np.asarray(self.coordinates, dtype=float))   # coordinates = cm-1 strings
# repro_wl.py (probe op with _requires_wavelengths=True, nm dataset 1000-2500):
legacy OK [('fit', (1000.0, 2500.0, 150))]
dag-ml OK [('fit', (10000.0, 4000.0, 150))]
# repro_temp_e2e.py: [TemperatureAugmenter(temperature_delta=40.0), KFold(3), PLS(3)], cv_best_score
no-aug legacy 0.729202 | no-aug dag-ml 0.729202 | aug legacy 0.727989 | aug dag-ml 0.729202   # dag-ml == no augmentation
# repro_temp.py: max|aug-X| with nm axis 1.34, with cm-1 axis 0.0
```

- Impact: `nirs4all.run([TemperatureAugmenter(...), KFold(3), {"model": PLSRegression(3)}], ds)` (the documented `[aug, PLSRegression(10)]` usage; default engine is dag-ml) silently trains on unaugmented spectra; scores differ from `engine="legacy"` and no warning is emitted. Same for MoistureAugmenter, ParticleSizeAugmenter, EMSC/edge-artifact augmenters used as pipeline steps.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): pass `wavelengths_nm` (converted from the cm-1 axis) to operators with `_requires_wavelengths is True`; keep cm-1 only for Resampler/CARS/MCUVE.

##### DGB-02 — Unequal multi-source SNV differs between dag-ml and legacy; default semantics require a contract decision

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Fresh unequal two-source SNV run gives dag-ml RMSE3.110786 vs legacy2.350258. This is a reproducible semantic/parity difference; choice of default per-source behavior still needs a contract decision because an existing test deliberately uses concatenated SNV.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/node_runner.py:1802-1805` (reconstructed `upstream`), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/node_runner.py:1845-1849` (`source_concat` decision), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/node_runner.py:1902` (`make_pipeline(*upstream, model)`), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/node_runner.py:1994` (`resolve_features` = fused matrix)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Fresh unequal two-source SNV run gives dag-ml RMSE3.110786 vs legacy2.350258. This is a reproducible semantic/parity difference; choice of default per-source behavior still needs a contract decision because an existing test deliberately uses concatenated SNV.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# repro_ms.py / manual_ms.py: 2 sources (40 + 25 cols), [SNV, KFold(3), PLS(3)], cv_best_score (pooled OOF RMSE)
legacy 1.341369   (== manual per-source SNV 1.341369)
dag-ml 1.280904   (== manual concat-then-SNV 1.280904)     default engine (no engine=) -> dag-ml 1.2809
# repro_ms2.py ShapeProbe transform shapes: legacy fit (80,25),(80,40); dag-ml fit (53,65),(54,65),(80,65)
```

- Impact: Scores differ for unequal sources; per-source versus concatenated normalization needs an explicit contract decision.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Decide and document per-source versus concatenated semantics before changing behavior; add a parity fixture with genuinely unequal sources.

##### DGB-03 — Stacking replay manifest inverts `weighted_mean` weights for `f1` (Python metric-direction list is missing `f1`)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh _stacking_replay_manifest with validation f1 .9/.5 produces weights1.111111/2.0 instead of .9/.5. Helper-level reproduction; no claim of full classification archive roundtrip in this pass.

- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/native_results.py:569` (`_stacking_replay_manifest`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: The manifest recomputes the per-member weights that dag-ml used at fit time, but its higher-is-better set is `{"r2","accuracy","balanced_accuracy"}`. dag-ml's `stacking_model_weights` (`dag-ml/crates/dag-ml-core/src/runtime/oof.rs:1682`) derives direction from `RegressionMetricKind::objective()`, where `F1` is Maximize (`metrics.rs:73`), and `f1` is a metric present in every report (`SCORE_METRICS`). `detect.py:1978` and `detect.py:1992` admit `weight_metric`/`metric` "f1". For `f1` the Python code takes the "lower is better" branch (`1/(score+1e-10)`), so the better model gets the smaller weight in the exported `.n4a`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
higher_better = metric in {"r2", "accuracy", "balanced_accuracy"}      # native_results.py:569
# repro_f1.py: members with validation metric 0.9 and 0.5
accuracy [[0.9, 0.5]]
f1       [[1.1111111109876544, 1.9999999996]]     # Rust: f1 -> max(score,0) -> [0.9, 0.5]
```

- Impact: classification branch stacking with `{"aggregate": "weighted_mean", "weight_metric": "f1"}` (or `metric: "f1"`): predictions replayed from the exported native archive (`_DagmlNativeStackingModel`, `api/result.py:1220`) use inverted member weights and no longer reproduce the training/refit predictions. Existing tests only cover rmse.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): add `"f1"` to the set (better: derive from `nirs4all.core.metrics.is_higher_better`, which already lists f1/auc/etc.).

The DAG lane intentionally refuses this input; legacy inventing feature indices is not evidence of valid nm input. #### Remaining dag-ml modules: conformal, tuning, filters, estimators (DGC)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

The five DGC claims were reviewed and freshly probed, including valid metadata, integer class-weight keys, conformal quantiles and archive rejection. Native/structural regression tests were rerun with compatible dependencies.

##### DGC-01 — `exclude` / `tag` with a `MetadataFilter` always crash on the dag-ml engine (metadata never passed to `get_mask`)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh public exclude and tag runs with correctly initialized metadata both fail MetadataFilter requires metadata. Other non-metadata filters are unaffected.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/exclude.py:132-135` (`_excluded_from_pool`), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/exclude.py:265-268` (`_resolve_tags`), `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/exclude.py:67-87` (`_filter_data_for_pool` only builds X/y)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: The dag-ml host re-implements `ExcludeController`/`TagController` filter application but only calls `filter_obj.get_mask(x_pool, y_pool)`. Legacy passes `metadata=dataset.metadata(...)` when the filter is a `MetadataFilter` (`nirs4all/controllers/data/exclude.py:171-173`, `nirs4all/controllers/data/tag.py:171-173`). `MetadataFilter.get_mask` raises `ValueError("MetadataFilter requires metadata ...")` when `metadata is None`, which the dag-ml code re-wraps as "could not be applied". `MetadataFilter` is documented for both keywords (`docs/source/reference/nodes/tag.md:68`, `docs/source/reference/filters.md:128`).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  132  filter_obj.fit(x_pool, y_pool)
  133  masks.append(filter_obj.get_mask(x_pool, y_pool))     # no metadata=
  ```
  Repro (`<audit-scratch>/r_meta2.py`): dataset with metadata column `quality`, pipeline `[{"exclude": MetadataFilter(column="quality", values_to_exclude=["bad"])}, KFold(3), {"model": PLSRegression(3)}]`, `nirs4all.run(pipe, ds)` (default engine) ->
  `ValueError: MetadataFilter could not be applied: MetadataFilter requires metadata containing column 'quality'. Pass metadata to get_mask().`
  Same pipeline with `engine="legacy"` runs. `_resolve_tags([{"tag": MetadataFilter(...)}], ...)` (`<audit-scratch>/r_tag.py`) fails identically.
- Impact: any pipeline that excludes/tags samples through metadata (quality flags, sample type, acquisition condition) cannot run on the default engine; it is a hard error, not a fallback.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): in `_filter_data_for_pool` also fetch `spectro.metadata({"sample": base_ints}, include_augmented=False)` re-keyed with the same `order`, and pass `metadata=` to `get_mask` when `isinstance(filter_obj, MetadataFilter)` in both `_excluded_from_pool` and `_resolve_tags`.

##### DGC-02 — Constructor params that are dicts with non-string keys (e.g. `class_weight={0: 1.0, 1: 5.0}`) are silently stringified in the DAG DSL, then crash at fit

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh class-weighted LogisticRegression with integer labels raises classes [0,1] not in class_weight on dag-ml. Conversion to string keys traced through bridge JSON.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/operator_parameters.py:29-34` (`encode_constructor_value`); coercion happens at `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml_bridge.py:138` (`_json_safe_params`: `json.loads(json.dumps(..., default=repr))`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `encode_constructor_value` is documented as "lossless nested sklearn constructor values within the JSON DSL" and has a `{"kind": "mapping", "value": [[key, item], ...]}` escape for pair-preserving dicts, but only uses it when the user dict contains the marker key. A plain dict with int keys is passed through unchanged, and `json.dumps` in the bridge then turns keys `0, 1` into `"0", "1"`. The model is rebuilt by `route_operator` with string keys.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  29  if isinstance(value, dict):
  30      if _COMPONENT in value: ... return {_COMPONENT: {"kind": "mapping", "value": [[key, ...]]}}
  34      return {key: encode_constructor_value(item) for key, item in value.items()}   # int keys kept, stringified later by json.dumps
  ```
  Repro `<audit-scratch>/q6.py`: `pipeline_to_dsl([{"model": LogisticRegression(class_weight={0:1.0,1:5.0})}], "t")` gives `"class_weight": {"0": 1.0, "1": 5.0}`; `route_operator(...)` then `.fit(X, y_int)` -> `ValueError: The classes, [0, 1], are not in class_weight`.
  End to end (`<audit-scratch>/r_cw.py`): `nirs4all.run([StratifiedKFold(3, shuffle=True, random_state=0), {"model": LogisticRegression(class_weight={0:1.0,1:5.0})}], (X, y))` on default engine -> `DagMlRuntimeError: runtime validation failed: python callback raised an exception: The classes, [0, 1], are not in class_weight`. (The legacy lane hits the same message in its refit pass, so the library-wide JSON round-trip of int-keyed dicts is also a problem there; the dag-ml-specific part is the encoder above.) `decode_constructor_value` already restores int keys from the `mapping` marker (verified in `<audit-scratch>/q7.py`), so only the encoder is missing.
- Impact: class-weighted classifiers (`LogisticRegression/SVC/RandomForestClassifier(class_weight={label: weight})`) or any estimator taking an int-keyed dict crash on the default engine; there is no pre-flight "lossless params" gate for model steps (only X transforms get `_check_x_operator`).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): in `encode_constructor_value`, use the `mapping` marker whenever any key is not a `str` (and decode already works); optionally make the bridge reject params that do not survive `json.loads(json.dumps(x)) == x`.

##### DGC-03 — `{"X", "y", "metadata"}` dataset dict: metadata columns are coerced to strings whenever one column is non-numeric

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh public dataset materializer converts temperature Float64 and integer metadata to String when combined with batch text.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/dataset.py:59-64` (`_materialize_dataset`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Per-column metadata arrays are merged with `np.column_stack(values)` and added with `add_metadata(2D array, headers)`. Stacking a string column with numeric columns promotes the whole matrix to `<U..`, so numeric columns lose their dtype (and all-numeric int+float columns are upcast to float). The dataset exposes a `add_metadata_column(column, values)` API that keeps per-column types.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  61  values = [np.asarray(value).reshape(-1) for value in supplied_metadata.values()]
  64  wrapped.add_metadata(np.column_stack(values), headers=columns)
  ```
  Repro `<audit-scratch>/r_md.py`: metadata `{"temp": [20.5, 21.0, ...], "batch": ["a","a","b",...], "n": [1,2,...]}` -> `ds.metadata({}).schema == Schema([('temp', String), ('batch', String), ('n', String)])`, `ds.metadata_column("temp") == ['20.5' '21.0' ...]`. Without the string column the schema is `Float64` for both numeric columns.
- Impact: numeric metadata (temperature, date index, numeric group ids) supplied in the public array-mapping dataset form becomes text on the dag-ml engine: numeric conditions/sorting (`MetadataFilter(condition=...)`, `metadata_numeric` label-encoding of "10.0" < "9.0") and missing-value handling (`NaN` -> "nan") silently change.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): add each column with `wrapped.add_metadata_column(name, values)` (or build a DataFrame) instead of column-stacking into one homogeneous array.

##### DGC-04 — `attach_conformal_result_to_bundle` leaves an empty/invalid ZIP at the target when it rejects an existing sidecar

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh rejected sidecar attachment creates a22-byte empty ZIP. ZIP is syntactically valid but is not a usable model archive; overwrite can destroy a prior destination.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/conformal_store.py:201-204` (`attach_conformal_result_to_bundle`); same non-atomic pattern at `:164` (`export_conformal_result_bundle`, `overwrite=True`)
- Category: persistence
- Description: The target ZIP is opened for writing (truncating/creating it) before the "bundle already contains a conformal sidecar" check, which then raises inside the `with`. The partial file stays on disk, and the retry fails with `FileExistsError`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  201  with zipfile.ZipFile(source, "r") as src, zipfile.ZipFile(target, "w", ...) as dst:
  202      existing_conformal = [...]
  203      if existing_conformal and not overwrite:
  204          raise ValueError("model bundle already contains a conformal sidecar; ...")
  ```
  Repro `<audit-scratch>/r_attach.py`: second attach on a bundle that already has a sidecar -> `ValueError`, then `out.n4a` exists with 22 bytes (empty ZIP), and a retry raises `FileExistsError: calibrated bundle target already exists`. With `overwrite=True` an existing valid target is truncated before any failure can be detected.
- Impact: a rejected attach (or a crash mid-write) leaves a corrupt `.n4a` at the output path (the default `<stem>.calibrated.n4a` included); blocks retry without manual deletion; may destroy a previous good output when `overwrite=True`.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): do the sidecar check before opening `target`, write to a temp file in the same directory and `os.replace` it on success (same for `export_conformal_result_bundle`).

##### DGC-05 — Split-conformal rank `ceil((n+1)*coverage)` suffers float rounding for coverages such as 0.68, 0.55, 0.56, 0.81

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh conformal quantile n74/.68 returns51.0 instead of decimal-arithmetic50.0. Conservative only, not undercoverage; keep low severity.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: dag-ml or the specifically named native/replay helper; default auto-routing is not universally dag-ml.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/dagml/conformal_contracts.py:1561` (`conformal_finite_sample_quantile`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: `k = math.ceil((n + 1) * cov)` is evaluated in binary floating point; when `(n+1)*coverage` is mathematically an exact integer it can come out as `k + 1e-14` and `ceil` returns `k + 1`. The artifact validator recomputes the same expression, so the error is self-consistent and not detected.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `<audit-scratch>/q3.py`: `conformal_finite_sample_quantile(np.arange(74.0), 0.68)` -> `51.0` (index 51); exact rank is 51 -> value `50.0`. `(74+1)*0.68 == 51.00000000000001`. Scan `<audit-scratch>/q2.py` over coverages 0.01..0.99 and n < 1500 finds affected coverages 0.07, 0.14, 0.17, 0.27, 0.28, 0.34, 0.54, 0.55, 0.56, 0.67, 0.68, 0.81 (0.8/0.9/0.95/0.99 unaffected).
- Impact: the interval is one order statistic wider than the stated finite-sample guarantee needs (conservative, coverage guarantee still valid, intervals slightly wider) for those (n, coverage) pairs; for `k == n+1` boundaries it can also turn a finite qhat into `inf`.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): compute the rank with exact/rounded arithmetic, e.g. `k = math.ceil(round((n + 1) * cov, 12))` or `Fraction(str(cov))`-based integer ceil.

### 5.2 Legacy engine — `nirs4all/controllers/`

#### Data controllers: branch / merge / exclude / tag / augmentation (CTD)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 18 CTD claims were reviewed with controller callers. Fresh legacy pipelines cover stacking, held-out test loss, source duplication, exclusion state, include_original, tag fitting and dict merge. CTD-02 has both alignment and held-out sample-count witnesses.

##### CTD-01 — Stacking merge pools predictions of same-named models across branches (duplicate averaged meta-feature columns)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Two PLSRegression(3)/(1) branches followed by prediction merge and Ridge produced three fits of shape(32,2) with identical columns. branch_id is not passed to the reconstructor. Confirmed in legacy with same model names across branches.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: critical
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/data/merge.py:4078` (`MergeController._collect_branch_predictions_oof`; `branch_id` param at :4037 is never used); also `merge.py:4198-4207` (`_collect_branch_predictions_unsafe`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `{"branch": [...models...]}` + `{"merge": "predictions"}` builds one meta-feature column per (branch, model name). OOF reconstruction is delegated to `TrainingSetReconstructor(source_model_names=[model_name])` called with the merge-time context, whose `selector.branch_id` is None, so the reconstructor does not filter by branch (`stacking/reconstructor.py:611-613`). Default model names are the class name, so two branches that each contain e.g. a `PLSRegression` (different preprocessing / n_components) both resolve to the same pooled prediction rows, which are averaged across branches and written into every such column.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r5.py: branches [SNV,PLS(3)] / [MSC,PLS(1)] -> merge predictions -> Ridge ; meta-features captured at Ridge fit
captured (80, 2)   corr(col0,col1)=1.0   identical? True
sample 0: branch0 OOF=1.898, branch1 OOF=1.194, merged col0 = col1 = 1.546  (= their mean)
# same pipeline with model names "pls_a"/"pls_b": col0 == branch0 OOF (max abs diff 1e-7), corr(col0,col1)=0.74
```

- Impact: any stacking pipeline whose branches contain the same model class (the normal "same PLS on different preprocessings" setup) feeds the meta-model N identical columns equal to the mean of the branches' OOF predictions; the stacked model's scores are silently wrong. Test-partition columns are affected the same way.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): scope the reconstruction to the branch (pass `branch_id` / a branch-scoped context or `source_model_branch_map` to the reconstructor, and filter by `branch_id` in the unsafe path).

##### CTD-02 — Separation prediction merge misaligns sample IDs and can also fail with a held-out test partition

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Confirmed index-alignment defect, sharing root cause CTM-02: _get_branch_oof_predictions matches branch-global IDs against stored local positions, then tolerates up to 50% missing values. Claims that all other branches are entirely NaN, or3+ branches always fail, require contiguous groups/suitable sizes; interleaved groups can yield partially misaligned values. With 48 training and 12 held-out test rows, fresh reproduction logs 50% NaN then additionally fails sample count 48 versus 60 because prediction merge uses train-only disjoint_analysis.total_samples (merge.py:2375) whereas add_merged_features requires all 60 rows (1838). Original train-only repro avoided this extra failure.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/data/merge.py:2884-2889` (`_get_branch_oof_predictions`), `merge.py:2923-2943` (`_validate_merged_trainability`, 50% threshold); root cause of the positional ids at `nirs4all/controllers/models/base_model.py:2556-2557`
- Category: correctness
- Description: Confirmed index-alignment defect, sharing root cause CTM-02: _get_branch_oof_predictions matches branch-global IDs against stored local positions, then tolerates up to 50% missing values. Claims that all other branches are entirely NaN, or3+ branches always fail, require contiguous groups/suitable sizes; interleaved groups can yield partially misaligned values. With 48 training and 12 held-out test rows, fresh reproduction logs 50% NaN then additionally fails sample count 48 versus 60 because prediction merge uses train-only disjoint_analysis.total_samples (merge.py:2375) whereas add_merged_features requires all 60 rows (1838). Original train-only repro avoided this extra failure.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r18b.py: 3 sites x 32 train rows, KFold(3), per-branch Ridge, then merge predictions
branch 0 ... first ids [0,1,2,3]    val sample_indices [2,3,6]    -> non-nan 32
branch 1 ... first ids [32,33,34,35] val sample_indices [1,11,13]  -> non-nan 0   (local positions)
branch 2 ... first ids [64,...]      val sample_indices [2,4,7]    -> non-nan 0
-> MERGE-E045 (66.7% NaN).   # r18c.py with 2 sites: no error; meta-model fit sees 21 of 42 rows with the same constant (imputed mean)
```

- Impact: Separation stacking can use misaligned/imputed OOF features; a held-out partition can also trigger an all-row versus train-only sample-count failure.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): store/consume global sample ids in branch predictions (map `val_indices` through the branch sample-id list), and make the NaN guard fail (or warn loudly) for any non-trivial fraction instead of imputing.

##### CTD-03 — Per-branch models in separation branches never produce a test-partition prediction

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh reproduction with shuffled KFold3, two groups of24train/6test rows: six branch Ridge fits and zero test prediction rows. sample_partition uses training IDs, then BaseModelController.get_xy applies that same set to test despite separate all_sample_indices metadata. An initial unshuffled KFold attempt failed earlier on an empty local fold and was not counted as proof of this issue.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/data/branch.py:477-483`, `:577-583`, `:693-699` (train-only `sample_indices`), stored as `sample_partition["sample_indices"]` at `branch.py:1275-1281`; consumed at `nirs4all/controllers/models/base_model.py:429-433` (`test_mask`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: in train mode the three separation handlers compute `sample_indices` from the *train* partition only and publish them as the branch's `sample_partition`. `BaseModelController.get_xy` masks both the train and the test arrays with that set, so every test row (never in the set) is dropped: X_test becomes empty. The new `all_sample_indices` universe is only used by the concat merge.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r2.py: ShuffleSplit -> {"branch": {"by_metadata":"site","steps":[StandardScaler(),{"model":Ridge()}]}}
A 0 train 22 / A 0 val 10 / A avg train 32 ... C final train 32      (no 'test' rows at all)
log: "No test set available for refit evaluation. final_score is set to None."
# same data, no branch (r3.py): 0 train 72 / 0 val 24 / 0 test 24 / ... final test 24
```

- Impact: the documented per-group model usage (`D06_separation_branches.py`: "Each farm trains its own PLS model") gets no test predictions/scores and no refit evaluation; `best_score` etc. are based on validation only.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): have the model controller mask the test partition with the branch's `all_sample_indices` (universe) rather than the train-only list.

##### CTD-04 — `merge_sources` / `{"merge": {"sources": ...}}` (concat, stack) leave the original sources in the dataset, so merged features are duplicated

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Multi-source SpectroDataset with8+4features followed by merge_sources concat feeds16columns to Ridge, expected12. The unmerged second source remains alongside the concatenated source0; both source-merge paths lack keep_sources.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/data/merge.py:4837-4842` (`_store_source_merge_result`) and `merge.py:5717-5722` (`_execute_source_merge_from_config`); contrast `merge.py:1446-1448` (`keep_sources(0)` in the branch merge)
- Category: correctness
- Description: after concatenating the sources, the result is written into source 0 with `add_merged_features(source=0)` but the other sources are never removed. Downstream controllers read all sources (`concat_source=True`), so the model sees the merged block plus the untouched remaining sources.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r7.py: 2 sources (20 + 10 features) -> {"merge_sources":"concat"} -> model (fit shapes captured)
{'merge_sources': 'concat'}       model fit shapes [(53, 40), (53, 40)]  n_sources 2   (expected (53, 30), 1 source)
{'merge': {'sources': 'concat'}}  model fit shapes [(53, 40), (53, 40)]
```

- Impact: every pipeline using source merging trains on duplicated columns (source 1 twice); results differ from the intended "concatenate sources" model and from the dag-ml lane.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): call `dataset.keep_sources(0)` (as the branch merge does) after the concat/stack write.

##### CTD-05 — Duplication branches share indexer state: `exclude`/`sample_augmentation` inside one branch leak into (or break) the following branches

# r14.py: KFold -> branch {with_excl:[exclude YOutlierFilter, SNV, PLS], no_excl:[SNV, PLS]}
model fit n_train rows: [52,52,52, 52,52,52, 78]   # no_excl branch should see 53/54 rows per fold (80 train rows); the 2 excluded rows stay excluded; final refit 78 not 80
# r15.py: branch {aug:[sample_augmentation(noise), SNV, PLS], plain:[SNV, PLS]}
log: "Branch 1 (plain) failed: Step execution failed: index 100 is out of bounds for axis 0 with size 100"  -> run completes with only branch 0 results (see CTD-12)
```

- Impact: comparing "with outlier exclusion" vs "without" (or "with augmentation" vs "without") in one branch step silently applies the first branch's exclusion to the others, or crashes the later branches; later non-branch steps (and the refit) also inherit the leaked state.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): snapshot/restore the indexer (excluded flags, augmented rows, tags) together with the features, or run branches on isolated dataset copies.

##### CTD-06 — Steps persisted as `(obj, name, fmt)` tuples (concat_transform, auto_transfer_preproc, resampler, feature_selection) cannot be replayed: predict/export silently skips them or crashes

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Executor saves tuple artifacts as step_artifact, constructs local metadata and does not register an operator chain or record_step_artifact. Four producer controllers and provider-based consumers checked. Legacy scope; current source confirmed, initial replay reproductions not rerun in this pass.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/execution/executor.py:1251-1267` (tuple artifacts saved as unlinked `step_artifact` rows); producers: `controllers/data/concat_transform.py:473-474,537-539`, `controllers/data/auto_transfer_preproc.py:304-305,612-615,707-710`, `controllers/data/resampler.py:270-272`, `controllers/data/feature_selection.py:228-230`; consumers that look them up through `artifact_provider`: `concat_transform.py:450-464`, `auto_transfer_preproc.py:349-364`, `resampler.py:232-248`, `feature_selection.py:183-199`
- Category: persistence
- Description: `TransformerMixinController` registers fitted transformers through the V3 artifact registry (`_persist_transformer`), which links them to the step/chain. The four controllers above instead return plain tuples; the executor saves them via `store.save_artifact(artifact_type="step_artifact")` with a random id and no chain/trace link, so `artifact_provider.get_artifacts_for_step(step)` returns `[]` at predict time.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r9.py concat_transform [PCA(5), SNV] -> PLS(3):  chain_id replay: "X has 40 features, but PLSRegression is expecting 45"
#                                                  model=best replay: "Transformer 'raw_PCA_0' not found at step 1"
# r8c.py auto_transfer_preproc(fast, picks gaussian2) -> PLS: .n4a bundle / chain_id replay -> y_pred [-59.0, -46.5, -140.0, ...] (direct = [0.03, 0.49, 0.64, ...]);
#        model=best replay: "transfer_preprocessing_recommendation not found" (get_artifacts_for_step(1) == [])
# r10.py Resampler: chain replay "X has 40 features, but PLSRegression is expecting 20"
```

- Impact: models trained with any of these steps (legacy lane) cannot be used for `predict()`/bundle export; for auto_transfer_preproc the bundle replay silently returns garbage predictions.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): register these artifacts through `artifact_registry.register(...)` / `record_step_artifact` like `TransformerMixinController`, or make the executor link tuple artifacts to the step chain.

##### CTD-07 — `exclude` followed by any source merge crashes with "Sample count mismatch"

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Outlier exclusion followed by two-source concat fails got58 samples expected60. Collection excludes flagged rows whereas replacement requires all stored rows.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/data/merge.py:4946-4952` (`_collect_source_features`) and `:1591-1597` (`_execute_source_branch_merge`) vs `nirs4all/data/dataset.py:307-312` (`add_merged_features`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: the source features are fetched with `include_excluded=False` (98 rows) but `add_merged_features` requires `features.shape[0] == dataset.num_samples` (100, excluded rows included), so the write raises.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r6.py: [{"exclude": YOutlierFilter()}, KFold, {"merge_sources":"concat"}, PLS] on a 2-source dataset
ERR Pipeline step 3 failed: ... Sample count mismatch: got 98 samples, expected 100      (without the exclude step: OK)
```

- Impact: multi-source pipelines cannot combine outlier exclusion with `merge_sources`, `{"merge": {"sources": ...}}` or a source-branch merge.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): fetch with `include_excluded=True` (as concat_transform/resampler do for the "all" array) before writing back.

##### CTD-08 — Stateful transformers inside separation branches are fit on all groups' train rows, not on the branch's samples

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Branch sample_partition stays in context.custom, but ordinary transformer training reads the entire train partition without applying that mask. Group-specific preprocessing becomes global. Distinct from EXE-01 fold-level leakage: this ignores the intended separation group.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/data/branch.py:1275-1281,1324-1331` (branch sub-steps run on the full dataset; the partition is only recorded in `context.custom["sample_partition"]`, which only the model controller and merge read)
- Category: correctness
- Description: the docs/module docstring say each separation group "is processed by its own sub-pipeline" on "a disjoint subset", but transformer controllers ignore `sample_partition`, fit on every train row, and transform every row; the merge later just selects each row from its owning branch.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r20.py: by_metadata "site" (3 sites), steps [StandardScaler()], merge concat; merged features vs references
max abs diff vs per-site scaler (fit on that site's train rows): 0.85 (train) / 0.99 (test)
max abs diff vs ONE global scaler fit on all train rows:         2.8e-07
```

- Impact: per-group preprocessing statistics (scalers, MSC reference, EMSC, PCA/centering ...) are silently global; per-group preprocessing is only effective for stateless row-wise operators.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): restrict the fit population (and ideally the transformed rows) of branch sub-steps to the branch's `sample_partition` indices.

##### CTD-09 — `merge` with `include_original: True` returns the last branch's features, not the pre-branch ones

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: StandardScaler/MinMaxScaler branches with include_original produce36columns and firstblock==lastblock. There is no writer of pre_branch_features_snapshot in the package, so fallback reads the last branch's features.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit

- Category: correctness
- Description: `_get_original_features` reads `context.custom["pre_branch_features_snapshot"]`, but nothing in the package ever sets that key (only this read exists). It falls back to the current dataset features, which after the branch loop are those of the last executed branch.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r13.py: branch [[SNV],[MSC]] -> merge {"features":"all","include_original":True}
merged shape (53, 45);  block0 (the "original") == block2 (MSC branch): True;  block0 std 1.053 vs raw std 0.985
```

- Impact: the "original" block silently duplicates the last branch instead of adding the raw spectra.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): have `BranchController` store the pre-branch feature snapshot in the context (or restore it before merging).

Resampler after: src0 feats [20, 10] | headers len src0/src1 20 20 | processing ctx [['raw'], []]

before feats [40, 10] ctx processing [['raw'], []]  ->  after feats [43, 13]      (source_1 should stay 10)
```

##### CTD-12 — A failing duplication branch is swallowed in training mode; the run succeeds with fewer branches

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Sequential branch execution catches exceptions, warns and continues during training; prediction/explain reraises. Real robustness/reporting issue, but the failure is not completely silent because a warning exists.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.

- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/data/branch.py:2551-2557` (`_execute_branches_sequential`)
- Category: error-handling
- Description: any exception in a branch's steps (train mode) is logged as a warning and the branch is dropped; `branch_contexts` simply lacks it. A later merge/ranking then works on a subset without raising.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
except Exception as e:
    if mode in ("predict", "explain"):
        raise
    logger.warning(f"  Branch {branch_id} ({branch_name}) failed: {e}")
    ...
    continue
# r15.py: run completes normally; only warning "Branch 1 (plain) failed: ... index 100 is out of bounds" and predictions only for branch 0
```

- Impact: real bugs (e.g. CTD-05) or bad hyper-parameters in one branch disappear from the results unnoticed; `best` can be picked among the surviving branches only.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): re-raise (or add an explicit `on_error: "skip"` option, with an error recorded in the result).

##### CTD-13 — `tag` fits its filters on all partitions (including the test set), contrary to its docstring

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Captured YOutlierFilter.fit inside tag receives60rows for48train+12test. It uses the all-partition selector, unlike exclude which explicitly fits on train.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.

- Category: data-leakage
- Description: the sample set is `context.selector`, whose default partition is "all", so `filter_obj.fit(X, y)` receives train+test X and y. Y/X outlier thresholds (and anything derived, e.g. `by_tag` branching on that tag) are computed with test targets.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r19.py: 80 train / 20 test rows, {"tag": YOutlierFilter(method="iqr")}
YOutlierFilter.fit called with n= 100 (train=80, test=20)
```

- Impact: tag values (and branches/analyses based on them) depend on test data; contrast `exclude`, which explicitly fits on the train partition.

##### CTD-14 — Persisted filters are never reloaded in predict mode (`by_filter`, `tag`): refit on the prediction batch / crash for y-based filters

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: by_filter refits unconditionally in every mode and emits tuple artifact only in training; tag reloads only through loaded_binaries, otherwise fits prediction X/y. Sources and provider contract checked; initial tag replay repro not rerun.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium

- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/data/branch.py:697-711,739-743` (`_execute_by_filter`: `fit` runs in every mode, artifact saved only in train and never loaded); `nirs4all/controllers/data/tag.py:160-168,195-199`
- Category: correctness
- Description: `by_filter` re-fits the filter on the incoming data in predict mode (the `branch_filter_*` artifact is only written, with the same unlinked-tuple problem as CTD-06). `tag` tries `loaded_binaries`, which is empty in the V3 flow, so it also refits on the prediction samples, where `y` is absent.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# r17.py: [{"tag": YOutlierFilter(iqr)}, KFold, PLS(2)], predict(model=best, data=X_only)
YOutlierFilter dict-replay ERR: "YOutlierFilter could not be applied: Values length (0) must match indices length (5)"
```

- Impact: pipelines with y-based `tag`/`by_filter` cannot predict through the executor replay; X-based filters are re-fit on a (possibly tiny) prediction batch, so the tag/branch assignment of new samples differs from training; `by_filter` additionally routes the whole universe with `y_all` (true targets) at `branch.py:731-733`.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): persist the fitted filter through the artifact registry and use the loaded instance (no `fit`) in predict mode.

##### CTD-17 — `feature_augmentation` default `action` is "add" although the docs and class docstring say "extend"

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**

- Category: api-contract
- Description: `action = step_info.original_step.get("action", "add")` while the documentation (and the class docstring "extend (default)") describes extend (linear growth, each operator on the base view). Results differ as soon as more than one view exists (e.g. two consecutive `feature_augmentation` steps: n*m views instead of n+m).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `action = step_info.original_step.get("action", "add")` with the comment "default: 'add' for backward compatibility".
- Impact: pipelines relying on the documented default get multiplicative view growth.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): align code and docs (change the default or the documentation).
