#### Model, splitter, transform controllers (CTM)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 16 original CTM claims were reviewed. Stacking branch_validator, multilevel, crossbranch, classification, serialization and config received additional targeted review. Transformer cache, excluded-sample OOF, MetaModel refit, splitter prediction and parallel serialization have fresh witnesses. CTM-17 is new.

##### CTM-01 — Transformer check-before-fit cache reuses a transformer fitted with different hyper-parameters

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: PCA(2)/PCA(5) sweep: second cache lookup returns PCA(2), and every PCA(5)-labelled prediction has n_features2. Stateful cache uses only chain+input-data hash. Fresh end-to-end run with current runtime overlay.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: critical
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/transforms/transformer.py:1270-1274` (`TransformerMixinController._try_cache_lookup`), `:592-606` and `:1378-1382` (persist side, `input_data_hash = dataset.content_hash()` + `registry._by_chain_and_data[...]`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: For stateful (non `_stateless`) transformers the cache key is `(chain_path_hash, dataset.content_hash())`. `chain_path` is built only from `step_index`, class name, branch/source/substep (`OperatorNode.to_key`), never from the operator parameters, and the dataset hash is computed on the data *before* the step. Two pipeline variants that differ only in the parameters of the operator at the same step (`_or_`, `_range_`, `_grid_` sweeps) therefore share a key; the second variant silently gets the first variant's fitted transformer. The existing tests only assert "different params must not be reused" for `_stateless` operators.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r5.py : pipe = [{"_or_": [PCA(n_components=2), PCA(n_components=5)]}, KFold(3), {"model": Ridge()}]
  CACHE LOOKUP -> None op= PCA(n_components=2)
  CACHE LOOKUP -> PCA(n_components=2) op= PCA(n_components=5)     # <- hit, wrong object
  ('..', 'PCA(n_components=2)', 2, 'avg')   # (pipeline, preprocessings label, n_features, fold)
  ('..', 'PCA(n_components=5)', 2, 'avg')   # label says 5 components, model saw 2 features
  ```

- Impact: any generator sweep over a preprocessing hyper-parameter whose operator is stateful (PCA n_components, StandardScaler(with_std=...), MinMaxScaler(feature_range=...), supervised transformers such as OSC/EPO, ...) evaluates all variants after the first with the first variant's fitted transformer while labelling them with their own parameters. Scores/rankings of the sweep are wrong and the stored `preprocessings` string is misleading.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): include `_compute_operator_params_hash(operator)` (and `fit_on_all`) in the lookup/persist key for stateful operators as well (e.g. `f"{params_hash}:{data_hash}"`).

##### CTM-02 — Val/train prediction `sample_indices` are positional indices, test ones are sample IDs; OOF reconstruction (stacking) and ResidualModel treat them all as IDs

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: After excluding sample0, stored validation indices0.. match y[indices+1], not y[indices]; test IDs are correct. train_sample_ids is forwarded through execute->train, then lost at train->_train_with_folds/_train_single_model (1070-1080), before launch_training. Original wording that it is never forwarded anywhere was too broad.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/models/base_model.py:1855-1856` (`_assemble_prediction_result`: `indices['train']/['val']` = fold positions into `X_train`), `:1852` (`test` = real sample IDs), `:2119` + `:2556` (`_create_fold_averages`/`_assemble_avg_prediction`: `all_val_indices` positional); consumers `nirs4all/controllers/models/stacking/reconstructor.py:1319` (`_collect_oof_predictions_with_proba`), `:938`, `nirs4all/controllers/models/meta_model.py:954-958`, `nirs4all/controllers/models/residual_model.py:167` (`residual_full[v_idx] = r_oof`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `launch_training` only receives `test_sample_ids`; `train_sample_ids` is computed in `execute` (`_get_partition_sample_indices`, docstring: "we need the actual sample IDs (not positional indices)") but never forwarded. Fold `val_indices` are positions in the filtered train matrix (`_remap_folds_to_positions`), and are stored unchanged as `sample_indices` of the `val`/`train` partitions. `TrainingSetReconstructor` maps them through `id_to_pos` built from `x_indices(...)` sample IDs. The two spaces coincide only when train IDs are exactly `0..n_train-1` (no excluded samples, train rows first, no sample-partition branch).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r1.py: [{"exclude": YOutlierFilter(iqr)}, KFold(3), PLSRegression] on (X,y,{"train":48}); y[0]=500 is excluded
  val fold0 sample_indices[:8] = [0 1 2 3 4 5 6 7]   np.allclose(y[idx], y_true) -> False
  np.allclose(y[idx+1], y_true) -> True                # real IDs are 1..47
  test fold0 sample_indices[:5] = [48 49 50 51 52]      np.allclose(y[idx], y_true) -> True
  # r2.py: + MetaModel(Ridge) (stacking)
  STRICT (default): RuntimeError: Incomplete OOF coverage: 1/63 samples missing predictions
  IMPUTE_MEAN: corr(OOF meta-feature, y): control (no exclusion) 0.927 ; with 1 excluded sample -0.050
  ```

- Impact: a pipeline with any excluded sample (`exclude`/YOutlierFilter, NaN filtering) followed by a `MetaModel` either crashes (default `CoverageStrategy.STRICT`) or, with DROP_INCOMPLETE/IMPUTE_*, trains the meta-learner on OOF features shifted onto the wrong samples (correlation with y collapses to ~0). Same misalignment for `ResidualModel` (`residual_full` is indexed by sample ID but filled with positional `v_idx`) and for any dataset whose train rows do not carry IDs `0..n-1`.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): forward `train_sample_ids` through `train -> _train_with_folds -> launch_training` and store `train_sample_ids[val_indices]` (and `train_sample_ids` positions for the `train` partition / `all_val_indices`) as `sample_indices`.

##### CTM-03 — MetaModel step cannot be refit under the default `refit=True` (STRICT: refit pass aborts; IMPUTE_*: meta-learner refit on all-zero features)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: KFold3->PLS->MetaModel with default refit logs incomplete OOF coverage48/48 during refit. Run returns30CV rows and zero final MetaModel rows. Imputation variants not rerun, but refit having no validation rows confirms the underlying path.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/models/meta_model.py:344-374` (`MetaModelController.get_xy` re-runs OOF reconstruction in the REFIT phase), `nirs4all/controllers/models/stacking/reconstructor.py:964-972` (STRICT raise) and `:1009`, `:1043` (zero imputation)
- Category: correctness
- Description: During REFIT the base models are refit on a single `[(all_train, [])]` fold (`_FullTrainFoldSplitter`), so they store no `val` partition; the MetaModel step then calls `reconstruct()`, finds no OOF predictions (coverage 0 %) and either raises or fills NaN with 0.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r12.py: [KFold(4), PLSRegression(3), MetaModel(Ridge, StackingConfig(coverage_strategy=S))] , default refit
  S=STRICT : "[X] Refit execution failed: ... Incomplete OOF coverage: 64/64 samples missing predictions"
             "[!] Refit pass did not complete successfully"   -> final rows: 0
  S=IMPUTE_ZERO: reconstruct in refit: nan 0, zeros 64 (X_train_meta is all zeros)
             FINAL MetaModel_Ridge test y_pred std=0 (y_true std=1.31)   # CV avg of same model: std 1.174
  ```
  Existing legacy-lane tests (`tests/integration/parity/test_dagml_sequential_metamodel.py:39`) pass `refit=False`, which hides this.
- Impact: every legacy-lane `MetaModel` pipeline run with the default `refit=True` loses its final model (logged error, run continues with CV-only results); with `IMPUTE_ZERO/MEAN/FOLD_MEAN` or `DROP_INCOMPLETE` the "final" meta-model is trained on constant features and predicts a constant, and it is what `RunResult` reports as the refit model.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): in REFIT build the meta-features from the stored fold/`final` base predictions (or refuse the refit explicitly) instead of re-running OOF reconstruction against a store that has no `val` rows.

##### CTM-04 — `TestAggregation.BEST_FOLD` / `WEIGHTED_MEAN` rank folds in the wrong direction for regression (val_score is RMSE)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Isolated _aggregate_test_fold_features with RMSE[1,9] and predictions[zeros,ones] returns ones for BEST_FOLD, choosing the worst fold. Weighted path clips error values as direct weights. Also wrong for entirely negative higher-is-better scores: firstfold fallback replaces argmax.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/models/stacking/reconstructor.py:1544-1550` (live path `_aggregate_test_fold_features`) and `:894-900` (older `_collect_test_predictions`); `nirs4all/controllers/models/meta_model.py:1126-1133` (`_aggregate_test_predictions_for_model`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Fold scores are `pred["val_score"]`, which for regression is RMSE (lower is better, `ModelControllerUtils.get_best_score_metric`). The code uses `np.argmax(scores)` for BEST_FOLD and `np.clip(scores, 0, None)` as weights for WEIGHTED_MEAN, i.e. it selects the worst fold and weights folds proportionally to their error. Only classification (balanced_accuracy, higher is better) is correct.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r7.py : KFold(4) -> PLS(3) -> MetaModel(Ridge, StackingConfig(test_aggregation=...))
  fold val RMSEs: [0.494 0.681 0.715 0.593]
  BEST_FOLD     : X_test_meta equals fold 2's test prediction (diff 0.0)  # fold 2 has the WORST rmse 0.715
  WEIGHTED_MEAN : max|X_test_meta - rmse-weighted mean| = 4.4e-16        # weights ∝ rmse, not 1/rmse
  ```

- Impact: with `StackingConfig(test_aggregation=BEST_FOLD|WEIGHTED_MEAN)` on a regression stack, the meta-learner's test (and predict-time) inputs come from the worst fold / over-weight the worst folds, while its training OOF features are unaffected: silent train/test feature mismatch and degraded test scores.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): derive direction from the stored `metric` (`infer_ascending`) as `ModelSelector` does (argmin / inverse weights for lower-is-better).

##### CTM-05 — MetaModel with `finetune_space`: all CV folds train the same estimator object (fold ensemble degenerates, RMSECV leaks)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: _get_model_instance(force_params) returns the same user Ridge twice, mutating alpha2then3. best_params path skips model cloning. Fresh mechanical aliasing repro; initial full Optuna finetuning repro not rerun.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/models/meta_model.py:287-296` (`_get_model_instance` calls `set_params` on `meta_operator.model` and returns it, no copy); consumed at `nirs4all/controllers/models/base_model.py:1531-1534` (best_params path skips `_clone_model`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: With `best_params` (finetune) `_build_and_train_model` uses `_get_model_instance(...)` directly. For sklearn/other controllers the factory rebuilds a new object, but `MetaModelController` returns the wrapped user estimator itself, so every fold fits and returns the same object; `_persist_model` and `_create_fold_averages` run after all folds are trained, so every "fold model" is the last fit.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r8.py: [KFold(4), PLS(3), RF(10), MetaModel(Ridge, finetune_space={"model__alpha": ("float_log",1e-3,10), "n_trials":3})]
  no-finetune: max|avg_test - mean(fold test preds)| = 0.0     max|avg_test - last fold pred| = 0.094
  finetune   : max|avg_test - mean(fold test preds)| = 0.0946  max|avg_test - last fold pred| = 0.0
  ```

- Impact: `avg`/`w_avg` predictions equal the last fold model, all persisted fold artifacts are identical, and the "OOF" val predictions of `avg` are produced by a model that was fit on most of those samples (optimistic RMSECV).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): clone before `set_params` (`sklearn.clone(model).set_params(...)`) in `MetaModelController._get_model_instance`.

##### CTM-06 — `{"split": "<fold file>"}` makes every `predict()`/`explain()` of the trained pipeline fail

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: FoldFileLoader.execute lacks a prediction/explain short-circuit; it validates training IDs from the old fold file against the new dataset's empty train partition. Exact mode/ID-validation path checked in current source.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/splitters/fold_file_loader.py:429-450` (`FoldFileLoaderController.execute`, no predict/explain short-circuit although `supports_prediction_mode` is True)
- Category: correctness
- Description: In predict/explain mode the controller re-parses the fold file and validates its sample IDs against the *new* dataset's train partition (empty), so >10 % missing IDs raises. `CrossValidatorController` handles predict mode explicitly (`split.py:584-602`).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r6.py: [{"split": "folds_manual.json"}, PLSRegression(2)] ; run OK ; nirs4all.predict(model=res.best, data=X[48:55], engine="legacy")
  RuntimeError: Pipeline step 1 failed: ... Fold file contains 48 sample IDs not in dataset. Sample IDs in dataset: 0.
  ```

- Impact: a model trained with pre-computed folds can never be used for prediction/explain in the legacy lane.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): in `mode in ("predict","explain")` skip parsing/validation and set placeholder folds of the right count (as `CrossValidatorController` does).

##### CTM-07 — Predicting with a pipeline that uses LeaveOneOut (or any splitter whose `get_n_splits` needs X/groups) crashes

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: LeaveOneOut training on12samples succeeds; predicting3samples with explicit workspace_path fails LeaveOneOut.get_n_splits missing X. An earlier attempt without workspace_path failed manifest resolution and was not treated as evidence for this bug.

- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit

- Category: correctness
- Description: In predict mode the controller calls `op.get_n_splits(y=...)`. `LeaveOneOut.get_n_splits(X, ...)` has a required `X`; `LeaveOneGroupOut`/`LeavePGroupsOut` require `groups`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r3.py: [LeaveOneOut(), PLSRegression(2)] on 18 train rows : run OK ; predict ->
  RuntimeError: Pipeline step 1 failed: ... LeaveOneOut.get_n_splits() missing 1 required positional argument: 'X'
  (KFold(3) predict OK)
  ```

- Impact: train works, predict/explain of a LOO/LOGO-validated model fails in the legacy lane.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): pass `X=X` (and `groups` when available) to `get_n_splits`, or derive `n_folds` from the stored artifacts.

##### CTM-08 — `train_params["n_jobs"] > 1` (or -1) crashes every fold-trained model step ("Could not pickle the task")

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: KFold3 with Ridge train_params.n_jobs=2 fails Could not pickle the task. Full RuntimeContext/dataset/prediction store are passed to loky. n_jobs=0 is a separate unsupported input case outside this finding.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/models/base_model.py:1048-1052` and `:1204-1207` (`train` / `_train_with_folds`)
- Category: concurrency
- Description: `train_params["n_jobs"]` is interpreted as fold-level parallelism and the whole `self.launch_training(*args)` (with `runtime_context`, `prediction_store`, `dataset`, which hold `threading.RLock`s) is shipped to loky workers: `TypeError: cannot pickle '_thread.RLock' object`. The same key is also the natural way to pass `n_jobs` to estimators (`_apply_train_params` forwards it), so `RandomForestRegressor` with `train_params={"n_jobs": -1}` hits it.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r11.py: [KFold(3), {"model": RandomForestRegressor(10), "train_params": {"n_jobs": -1}}]
  FAILED: RuntimeError: Pipeline step 2 failed: Step execution failed: Could not pickle the task to send it to the workers.
  ```
  (Even if pickling worked, `next_op()` would be incremented in the worker copy only, duplicating `op_counter` across folds.)
- Impact: crash for any multi-fold model step with `n_jobs != 1` in `train_params`.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): drop the fold-parallel branch (or use threads / a picklable per-fold payload and allocate op counters in the parent).

##### CTM-09 — AOM-PLS with "auto" pipeline folds crashes when the pipeline splitter does not partition the training rows (ShuffleSplit, RepeatedKFold, ...)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: make_pipeline_fold_splitter checks only candidate existence and n_splits>=2; it forwards external_folds to AOM which requires an exact partition. Auto fallback defect for overlapping/incomplete splits confirmed by source; AOM runtime not rerun here.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/models/pipeline_cv.py:112-138` (`make_pipeline_fold_splitter` accepts any restricted fold set), `:191` (`updates["external_folds"] = splitter.validation_folds`), `:56-70` (`fold_ids`), consumed by `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/aom_pls_aomlib.py:108-114`
- Category: error-handling
- Description: The default policy `use_pipeline_folds_for_aom="auto"` must fall back to the estimator's own CV when pipeline folds are unusable, but the restricted splitter is not checked for being an exact partition. `AOMPLSAomlibRegressor` then raises `external_folds must partition training samples exactly once` (also `PrecomputedFoldSplitter.fold_ids` raises for the same condition).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r19.py: [splitter, {"model": AOMPLSAomlibRegressor(n_components=5)}] on (X,y,{"train":64})
  KFold(4)        : OK
  ShuffleSplit(3) : FAILED: RuntimeError: Pipeline step 2 failed: ... external_folds must partition training samples exactly once.
  ```

- Impact: AOM-PLS cannot be used with Monte-Carlo / repeated CV splitters under default settings.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): in `for_training_subset`/`make_pipeline_fold_splitter` return `None` when validation folds overlap or leave rows unassigned (so `auto` degrades; `required` still errors).

##### CTM-10 — `CoverageStrategy.DROP_INCOMPLETE` never drops anything: `valid_train_mask` is ignored, zero-filled rows are trained on

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: DROP_INCOMPLETE returns nan_to_num features plus complete_mask, but MetaModel.get_xy returns unmasked X/y. No controller reference applies valid_train_mask. Independent of why OOF rows are missing.

- Category: correctness
- Description: `_apply_coverage_strategy` replaces NaN by 0 and returns a mask "(samples will be masked)", and warns "Dropping n/N samples...", but nothing in the controller applies `valid_train_mask` (no reference in `meta_model.py`).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):

  rows 63  mask False count 1  zero-feature rows 1      # the incomplete row is kept with features 0

- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): apply `valid_train_mask` to X/y (and fold indices) in `get_xy`.

##### CTM-12 — TensorFlow/JAX `_predict_model` turns any multi-column output into argmax class indices (multi-target regression crashes)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: TensorFlow multi-column outputs undergo unconditional argmax; JAX wrapper does likewise without a task guard. Regression outputs collapse to(n,1). Current code checked; optional DL backends not executed in this pass.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/models/tensorflow_model.py:412-415`, `nirs4all/controllers/models/jax_model.py:357-358` (the Torch controller guards with the stored `task_type`, `torch_model.py:390-392`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Output conversion is unconditional on task type, so a regressor with k>1 outputs is collapsed to one column of argmax indices.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r18.py: 2-target regression (Y shape (60,2)), @framework("tensorflow") model with Dense(2), KFold(2)
  ValueError: Target shape mismatch: y_true(24, 2) vs y_pred(24, 1)
  ```

- Impact: multi-target regression is unusable with TF/JAX models (crash; with 1-D metrics it would be silent garbage).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): only argmax when `task_type.is_classification` (store it on the model as Torch does).

##### CTM-14 — `PredictionAggregator._aggregate_weighted_mean` divides by zero when all weights are 0

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Weighted aggregation with R2model_scores[-.2,-.5] raises ZeroDivisionError. Higher-is-better scores all<=0 yield zero total weight.

- Category: numerical
- Description: For higher-is-better metrics `weight = max(score, 0.0)`; if every model has score <= 0 (e.g. R2 of poor models) `total == 0` and `w / total` raises (the sibling `aggregate_folds` guards this case).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```

  ```

- Impact: merge with `weighted_mean` + `metric="r2"` crashes when all selected models have non-positive R2.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): fall back to the uniform mean when `total <= 0`.

##### CTM-15 — Fold files written by `CrossValidatorController` lose the validation sets of non-partitioning / single-fold splits when reloaded

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: CSV loader defines its sample universe as union of training sets, then computes validation complements inside that union. It cannot recover single-fold ShuffleSplit or samples absent from all training sets. Do not generalize to JSON with explicit validation IDs, or every repeated split whose training union can be complete.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/splitters/fold_file_loader.py:175-185` (`_parse_csv_nirs4all_format`) vs `nirs4all/controllers/splitters/split.py:752-764` (writer stores train IDs only)
- Category: persistence
- Description: Validation is reconstructed as `union(train of all folds) - train_k`. For a single train/val split (`ShuffleSplit(n_splits=1)` with a test partition present) the union equals the train set, so the reloaded validation set is empty; for ShuffleSplit/Monte-Carlo splits samples that are in no training set vanish from every validation set.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # r17.py: ShuffleSplit(1, test_size=0.25) on 40 ids -> original val size: 10 | reloaded val size: 0
  ```

- Impact: re-using a saved fold CSV does not reproduce the original split (model trained with empty/partial validation).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): persist val IDs as well (or use the JSON/assignment formats), and have the writer emit them.

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: best chooses the first fold, confirmed. fold1 is documented for dagml rather than promised legacy syntax; legacy accepts fold_1. Unknown selectors silently fall back to best. Keep the real best-validation mismatch while distinguishing engine syntax contracts.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/models/base_model.py:1337-1350` (`_resolve_warm_start_fold`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: best chooses the first fold, confirmed. fold1 is documented for dagml rather than promised legacy syntax; legacy accepts fold_1. Unknown selectors silently fall back to best. Keep the real best-validation mismatch while distinguishing engine syntax contracts.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `# Default: "best" -- return fold 0 as a heuristic ... return fold_ids[0] if fold_ids else None`
- Impact: Warm-start best can use a suboptimal first fold; unknown selectors silently fall back. fold_1 is the supported legacy spelling.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): resolve "best" from stored fold val scores and raise on unrecognised selectors.

##### CTM-17 — MetaModel controller ignores max_level validation failures

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: The validator emits "Detected level 2 exceeds maximum 1", but the controller searches for "exceeded" and returns without raising. A level-2 meta-model therefore trains with max_level=1. Other validation messages also lack a fallback raise.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/models/meta_model.py:523-537; nirs4all/controllers/models/stacking/multilevel.py:186-190`
- Category: correctness
- Description: The validator emits "Detected level 2 exceeds maximum 1", but the controller searches for "exceeded" and returns without raising. A level-2 meta-model therefore trains with max_level=1. Other validation messages also lack a fallback raise.
- Evidence: Fresh legacy KFold(3) → Base → MetaModel_A(source Base) → MetaModel_B(source MetaModel_A, max_level=1) succeeds and stores validation rows for folds 0, 1, 2, avg and w_avg.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Use structured validation error kind or handle all nonvalid results; match exceeds and raise fallback.

### 5.3 Public API — `nirs4all/api/`

#### run / predict / session / generate / results (API)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 11 API claims were reviewed with engine routing and session ownership. API-01 through API-08 are legacy-path claims. API-09 and API-11 are freshly reproduced engine-independent helpers; API-10 is grouped under SYN-19. Previously listed native witness/training/lineage and tuning validators received targeted contract/caller review; no claim of exhaustive native-protocol verification is made.

##### API-01 — `RunResult.export()` / `final` / `final_score` / `models` use the FIRST refit row, not the best one (wrong model exported)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh multi-model legacy result ranks PLSRegression best (RMSE.0399315), exposes DummyRegressor final (2.7365359) and exports constant predictions. models pairs Dummy final with PLS CV.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/api/result.py:2126` (`RunResult.final`), `:2154` (`final_score`), `:2797` (`RunResult.export`: `source = self.final or self.best`), `:2205-2257` (`models`, uses `self.final` + global `self.cv_best`); same `self.final` is reused by `Session.save` (`session.py:872`).
- Category: correctness
- Description: with several refit rows (legacy lane refits one model per distinct model class, or `refit={"top_k": n}`), `final` returns the first `fold_id="final"` row found in per-dataset order, while `best`/`best_score`/`best_final` correctly rank them. `export()` (documented "uses best prediction", `run()` docstring "export the best model") nevertheless feeds `self.final` to the exporter, so the exported `.n4a` can hold a worse model than `result.best`. `final_score` and `models` are equally inconsistent (`models` pairs the first model's final score with the CV entry of a different model).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
2138  for ds_info in self.per_dataset.values(): ...
2142      entries = ds_preds.filter_predictions(fold_id="final")
2144          if str(entry.get("fold_id")) == "final":
2145              return dict(entry)                                      # first, not best_final
2797  source = self.final or self.best
```
  Repro (`t23.py`): pipeline `[StandardScaler(), KFold(3), {"model": {"_or_": [DummyRegressor(), PLSRegression(8)]}}]`, `engine="legacy"`, sample_data/regression_2:
  `best model: PLSRegression | final model: DummyRegressor | final_score 2.2988 | best_score 0.3795`
  `r.export("exp.n4a")` then `nirs4all.predict(model="exp.n4a", data=X6, engine="legacy")` -> `[12.544 12.544 12.544 12.544 12.544 12.544]` (constant = DummyRegressor); `models` -> `{'DummyRegressor': final_score 2.2988, cv_score 0.3855, cv_entry model 'PLSRegression'}`. With the `_or_` order reversed (PLS first) the result is correct, i.e. the bug depends on generator order. dag-ml engine is unaffected (single final row).
- Impact: any multi-model comparison run on the legacy lane followed by `result.export(...)`, `Session.save(...)`, `result.final_score` or `result.models` silently ships/reports the wrong model.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): make `final` (and `export`'s default source) use `self.best_final` (falling back to the first row only when `best_final` is empty); build `models` from all final rows with their own CV entries.

##### API-02 — Multi-config refit (`refit={"top_k": k>1}`) on a multi-model-class pipeline refits each model's best variant k times and ignores the selected configs (out-of-area: pipeline/execution)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh top_k3 across Dummy and two PLS variants produces six final train rows: each class best repeated3 times, no runner-up PLS variant. Scope multi-class legacy refit.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/execution/orchestrator.py:1379` (loop over `refit_configs` calling `_execute_single_refit`), `:1896` (`return self._execute_per_model_refit(...)` -> `:2011`/`:2041`, which re-extracts per-model best configs and never looks at the passed `refit_config`).
- Category: correctness
- Description: for a pipeline with >1 model class (e.g. `_or_` over model classes) the multi-criteria refit pass selects k distinct configs, but `_execute_single_refit` falls to `_execute_per_model_refit`, which refits "each unique model independently on its best variant" regardless of `refit_config`. Each of the k iterations therefore produces the same per-model refits: k duplicated `final` rows per model, and the k-1 runner-up variants are never refit. `run(refit={"top_k": 3,...})` is documented as "refit top 3".
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t15.py`, 3 variants LR / KNN(1) / KNN(15), legacy): `refit=True` -> 2 final test rows (one per model class, by design); `refit={"top_k":3,"ranking":"rmsecv"}` -> 6 final test rows: `LogisticRegression config_177c1ff8_refit` x3 (different prediction ids, identical scores) and `KNeighborsClassifier config_6d9ed356_refit` x3 (KNN(15) never refit, KNN(1) refit three times). With a single model class (`t2.py`, 3 PLS variants) top_k=3 correctly yields 3 distinct rows.
- Impact: wasted compute, duplicated rows in `result.predictions`/store, and the requested top-k variants are not what gets refit; amplifies API-01.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): in the multi-config branch skip per-model dispatch (use the supplied `refit_config`), or dedupe by `(model_name, config_name)`.

##### API-03 — `Session.run()` (legacy lane) silently returns an empty result for `(X, y)` / array datasets and crashes on `{"X","y"}` dicts

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh legacy Session tuple returns0 predictions and no datasets; X/y dictionary fails No features available. File/config inputs are unaffected.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/api/session.py:553-558` (`Session.run`: `dataset_config = DatasetConfigs(dataset)` for anything that is not a path/DatasetConfigs)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: `Session.run` documents `Numpy arrays: (X, y)` and `Dict: {"X": X, "y": y}`. In the legacy branch these are wrapped with `DatasetConfigs(...)`, whose normaliser returns `None` for tuples/arrays ("Skipping invalid dataset config") and treats a dict as a file config. (The orchestrator's own `_normalize_dataset` handles tuples/arrays correctly, but `Session.run` pre-wraps them so it is never reached.) For a tuple the run executes on 0 datasets, `status` becomes `"trained"` and an empty `RunResult` is returned; for a dict it crashes with `No features available`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t18.py`, `Session(pipeline=pipe, workspace_path=ws).run(data, engine="legacy")`):
  `tuple num_predictions: 0 best_score nan per_dataset keys []` (log: `Skipping invalid dataset config: (array(...`, `0 dataset(s)`), then dict -> `RuntimeError: Pipeline step 1 failed: Step execution failed: No features available`.
- Impact: documented stateful-session usage with in-memory arrays on the legacy lane yields an empty/"trained" session (later `predict` has no model) or a misleading crash.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): pass non-path inputs straight to `runner.run(dataset=...)` (as `run()` does) instead of wrapping them in `DatasetConfigs`.

##### API-04 — `run(engine="legacy")` rejects the documented dict dataset `{"X": X, "y": y, "metadata": ...}`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh public legacy run with X/y dictionary fails No features available. This shares dataset-normalization work with API-03, but direct run and Session preprocessing differ.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/api/run.py:1309-1317` (dataset forwarded unchanged to `runner.run`), `nirs4all/pipeline/execution/orchestrator.py:1135-1151` (`_normalize_dataset` only wraps SpectroDataset/ndarray/tuple; a dict goes to `DatasetConfigs`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: the `run()` docstring (run.py:792-793) and `_is_single_dataset` (run.py:677-681, "dict with 'X' ... is a data dict") accept `{"X": X, "y": y, "metadata": meta}`. The dag-ml lane translates it (`pipeline/dagml/dataset.py:54-75`), the legacy lane does not: the keys are unknown to the file-config loader, so an empty dataset is built.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t20.py`): `nirs4all.run(pipe, {"X": X, "y": y}, engine="legacy")` -> `Dataset 'array_dataset' ... Features (samples=0, sources=0) ... RuntimeError: Pipeline step 1 failed: Step execution failed: No features available`; same call with the default engine succeeds.
- Impact: the rollback lane cannot take the dict input form the public docs promise; the error message points at the pipeline, not the dataset form.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): reuse `_materialize_dataset`-style X/y dict handling in `_normalize_dataset` (or in `run()`'s legacy closure) before building `DatasetConfigs`.

##### API-05 — `Session.run()` (legacy lane) drops `refit=`/other run kwargs and resets the configured `plots_visible`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh Session file-style in-memory config with refit=False still has final rows; Session configured plots_visible=True becomesFalse. Using a raw SpectroDataset in the repro would instead trigger API-03, so the corrected repro uses train_x/train_y.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/api/session.py:567-568` (`valid_run_params = {'pipeline_name','dataset_name','max_generation_count'}` filter), `:561-564` (`self.runner.plots_visible = plots_visible` with default `False`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: docstring says `**kwargs: Additional arguments passed to runner.run()`, but every kwarg except three names is silently discarded on the legacy path (`refit`, `store_run_id`, ...), while the non-legacy branch of the same method forwards `**kwargs` to `run()`. Separately, the `plots_visible: bool = False` default is always written to the shared runner, overwriting a `Session(plots_visible=True)` configuration.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t19.py` `s.run(path, engine="legacy", refit=False)` -> folds `['0','1','2','avg','final','w_avg']` (refit still done) whereas `nirs4all.run(pipe, X, session=s, engine="legacy", refit=False)` -> no `final`. `t16.py`: `Session(..., plots_visible=True)`; after `s.run(...)`: `runner.plots_visible = False`.
- Impact: user-requested `refit=False` is ignored (extra full refit, `final` rows present); configured plotting silently turned off.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): forward `refit` (and reject unknown kwargs instead of dropping them); only touch `plots_visible` when the caller passes it explicitly (use `None` default).

##### API-06 — `run(session=s, engine="legacy")` ignores explicit overrides (contrary to its docstring) and leaks `verbose` into the shared runner

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh public session-owned legacy run with verbose1/save_artifactsFalse/random_stateNone retains runner verbose2/save_artifactsTrue/random_state7. Overrides cannot be inferred from constructor defaults.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/api/run.py:1252-1257` (`runner = session.runner`; only `verbose` is applied and only `if verbose != 1`)
- Category: api-contract / state-leak
- Description: the docstring (run.py:810-813) promises "explicit values (including False or None) override" the Session configuration. On the legacy path the session's already-built runner is used as is; `save_artifacts`, `save_charts`, `plots_visible`, `report_naming`, `random_state` passed to `run()` are ignored (only `refit`/`cache` are applied). `verbose` is special-cased with `!= 1`, so an explicit `verbose=1` cannot override a session verbosity, and any other value is written permanently into the shared runner (later calls with the default `verbose` do not restore it).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t9.py` Session(verbose=2, save_artifacts=True, random_state=7); `run(..., session=s, verbose=1, save_artifacts=False, random_state=None)` -> `runner.verbose 2`, `runner.save_artifacts True`, `runner.random_state 7`. `t10.py` default session: `run(verbose=0)` then `run()` (default) then `run(verbose=1)` -> `runner.verbose` stays `0` after all three.
- Impact: explicit `save_artifacts=False` still persists artifacts; a single quiet run silences every later run of the session; seeds cannot be overridden per run.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): apply every resolved option to the runner per call (and restore after the call) or build a per-call runner view; drop the `!= 1` sentinel.

##### API-07 — Results of a session-owned legacy run cannot be exported after the session is closed

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh result exported after session context closes raises WorkspaceStore is closed. Dag-ml detached archive lifecycle is a different path.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/api/result.py:2629-2630` (`_open_store_for_export`, attached branch) and `:2802-2803` (`export` -> `self._runner.export`); `nirs4all/api/session.py:887-889` (`Session.close` closes the runner but does not detach the results it produced)
- Category: resource-leak / api-contract
- Description: non-session results are `detach()`ed so export re-opens the store on demand; session results keep `_owns_runner=False` and a reference to the session's runner. After `with nirs4all.session(...)` exits, the runner/store are closed and `result.export()` raises.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t24.py`): inside the block `r.export(...)` works; after the block `RuntimeError: WorkspaceStore is closed` (`best_score`/`top()` still readable). dag-ml results from the same pattern export fine (`t25.py`).
- Impact: the natural pattern `with session() as s: r = run(..., session=s)` followed by `r.export(...)` outside the block fails (legacy lane).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): on `Session.close()` detach every result it handed out (or have `_open_store_for_export` fall back to a fresh `WorkspaceStore(self._workspace_path)` when the runner's store is closed).

##### API-09 — `generate.from_template(<path>)` discards the template's wavelength axis (and any user-supplied `wavelengths`)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh path-template CSV with 30 wavelengths spanning 1100–2400 nm generates headers 0..29 instead. The code reads nonexistent SpectroDataset.wavelengths and overwrites the caller axis. SYN-02 is the same defect; API-09 is the canonical high-severity item.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/api/generate.py:909-911` (`from_template`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: for a string template the code does `wavelengths = getattr(template_ds, 'wavelengths', None)`; `SpectroDataset` has no `wavelengths` attribute (only `wavelengths_nm()`/`wavelengths_cm1()`/`headers()`), and `getattr(..., None)` never raises, so the `suppress(AttributeError, TypeError)` is moot and `wavelengths` is always overwritten with `None` (also clobbering the caller's argument). `fit_to(template_array, wavelengths=None)` then generates on an index axis.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t8.py`, sample_data/regression_2, axis 5966-11012 cm-1 / 908-1676 nm): `SpectroDataset has .wavelengths: False`; array template with `wavelengths=nm` -> headers `['908','914',...,'1670','1676']`; path template with the same `wavelengths=nm` -> headers `['0','1',...,'123','124']`.
- Impact: synthetic datasets mimicking a real dataset by path carry no spectral axis (wrong units for any wavelength-aware preprocessing/plot).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use `template_ds.wavelengths_nm(0)` (or `float_headers(0)` with unit handling) when `wavelengths is None`; never overwrite a caller-supplied value.

##### API-10 — `nirs4all.generate(...)` silently ignores unknown keyword arguments

- Re-audit verdict: **DUPLICATE — canonical item below**

- Fix disposition: Track and fix only under SYN-19; retain this location/reference.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: underlying behavior reviewed; not an independent finding
- Location: `nirs4all/api/generate.py:188-194` (`generate`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: `**kwargs` is documented as "passed to SyntheticDatasetBuilder", but only `distribution` and `batch_effects` are consumed; anything else (e.g. `n_groups=5`, a typo) is dropped without error. (`generate.regression/classification` have strict signatures, only the namespace call is affected.)
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t4.py`): `generate(n_samples=30, random_state=1, ..., totally_bogus_option=123, n_groups=5)` returns arrays identical to the call without them (`True`).
- Impact: mistyped/unsupported options produce a dataset that silently differs from what was requested.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): raise `TypeError` for leftover kwargs after the two handled keys.

##### API-11 — `normalize_dataset_document` rejects valid tab-indented JSON config files

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh normalize_dataset_document on valid JSON with tab indentation raises YAML ScannerError because YAML preflight runs before JSON parser.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/api/dataset_documents.py:153` (`_BoundedNormalizer._load_config_file` calls `_preflight_yaml` for every file, including `.json`), `:78-111`
- Category: error-handling
- Description: the YAML alias-bomb preflight uses `yaml.parse(..., SafeLoader)` on JSON files; PyYAML's scanner refuses TAB characters used for indentation, so a perfectly valid JSON config raises `yaml.scanner.ScannerError` instead of being parsed by `_parse_json`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t11.py`): `normalize_dataset_document("tab.json")` (`json.dumps(cfg, indent="\t")`) -> `ScannerError: found character '\t' that cannot start any token`; the same document with 2-space indentation works and `json.load` parses the tab file.
- Impact: Studio/host dataset previews fail for JSON configs written with tab indentation.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): only run `_preflight_yaml` for `.yaml/.yml` files.

**Auditor's out-of-scope observations**

> Observations outside the scope (not verified as bugs, for the lead): (1) the packaged `examples/sample_data/regression` CSVs have no header row, but the default `has_header=True` (`nirs4all/data/loaders/csv_loader_new.py:192`, auto-detection bypassed) consumes row 0 of each file as the header (131->130 train, 60->59 test samples; headers become spectrum values), so docs/examples built on that sample silently lose one sample per file. (2) A few early repros (retrain/predict, before I moved the cwd) wrote logs/arrays into the git-ignored `/home/delete/nirs4all/nirs4all/workspace/`.

### 5.4 Pipeline infrastructure

#### Config, generators, core, utils, sklearn wrapper, Optuna (CFG)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 25 CFG claims were checked against generator, optimizer, metric and sklearn contracts. Explicit dag-ml inference wrappers were reproduced. List categoricals versus tuple ranges and intentionally transductive behavior are distinguished from defects.

##### CFG-01 — Finetune of a classification model without `metric` maximises a minimise-oriented score (picks the WORST hyper-parameters)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: The classification resolver defaults to maximize while the sklearn controller emits negative balanced accuracy. Confirmed inconsistent ranking in the legacy lane; DAG host tuning supplies its own metric.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/optimization/optuna.py:606-610` (`OptunaManager._resolve_metric_direction`); same logic at `nirs4all/optimization/n4m_engine.py:219-221`; score producers `nirs4all/controllers/models/sklearn_model.py:643,645`, `nirs4all/controllers/models/tensorflow_model.py:511`, `torch_model.py` (`MSELoss`), `jax_model.py` (MSE)
- Category: correctness
- Description: When `finetune_params` has no `metric`, a classification task gets `direction="maximize"`, but every controller's legacy `_evaluate_model(metric=None)` returns a lower-is-better value (`-balanced_accuracy`, MSE, Keras loss). The study therefore maximises `-balanced_accuracy` and returns the hyper-parameters with the lowest balanced accuracy. The unit test `test_no_metric_classification_defaults_maximize` only checks the resolver, so it encodes the inconsistent default.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # optuna.py:606-610
  task_type = getattr(dataset, "task_type", "regression")
  if "classification" in task_type:
      params.setdefault("direction", "maximize")      # <- but score is -balanced_accuracy
  # sklearn_model.py:643
  return float(-balanced_accuracy_score(y_val_1d, y_pred))
  ```
  Repro (`t5.py`, DecisionTreeClassifier, `max_depth` grid, real `SklearnModelController._evaluate_model`, no metric): `direction: maximize best_params: {'max_depth': 1} best_value: -0.733` while depth 2-4 give `-0.821` (better). The chosen value is the worst trial.
- Impact: any legacy-lane (`run(engine="legacy")` / `PipelineRunner`) classification `finetune_params` without an explicit `metric` (sklearn, TF, torch, JAX controllers) returns the worst candidate, which is then used for the refit. (The dag-ml host path sets its own metric default, so it is not affected.)
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): with no metric, use `direction="minimize"` for every task (the legacy scores are loss-oriented), or default `metric="balanced_accuracy"` for classification so the raw metric and `maximize` agree.

##### CFG-02 — A single nested list is flattened when a pipeline is expanded (chain `[[A, B]]` becomes parallel `[A, B]`); expansion is also triggered by a plain `count` key

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Literal branch [[1,2]] is flattened to [1,2]. PipelineConfigs detects plain sample_augmentation.count as a generator modifier, activating the flattening even without a generator.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/config/_generator/core.py:271-276` (`_expand_list`), `core.py:503-507` (`_expand_list_with_choices`), `nirs4all/pipeline/config/_generator/iterator.py:160-166`; trigger `nirs4all/pipeline/config/pipeline_config.py:147` (`_has_gen_keys` tests `ALL_KEYWORDS`, which contains the modifier `count`, see `_generator/keywords.py:108-110`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `_expand_list` special-cases a one-element list whose element expands to "lists" and returns the element's variants directly. Because expanding any list returns list-variants, this also fires for a literal nested list (`[[X, Y]]`), dropping one nesting level (`[X, Y]`). This was meant for `[{"_or_":..., "pick": 2}]`. It changes pipeline semantics for e.g. `concat_transform: [[StandardScaler(), PCA()]]` (one chain) which becomes two parallel transforms. Worse, `_has_gen_keys` treats the ordinary `count` option of `sample_augmentation` as a generator keyword, so the expansion (and the flattening) runs on pipelines that contain no generator at all.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # core.py:271-275
  if len(node) == 1:
      element_result = _expand_internal(node[0], seed)
      if element_result and isinstance(element_result[0], list):
          return element_result            # [[A,B]] -> [[A,B]] variants == [A,B], nesting lost
  ```
  Repro (`t17.py`, no generator anywhere, only `sample_augmentation={"transformers":[...], "count": 2}`):
  `original : [["...StandardScaler", {"class": "...PCA", ...}]]`
  `expanded : ["...StandardScaler", {"class": "...PCA", ...}]`
  (`t14.py`: same flattening when the PCA `n_components` uses `_range_`). `expand_spec({"branch": [[1, 2]]})` -> `[{'branch': [1, 2]}]`.
- Impact: `ConcatAugmentationController` runs `SS` and `PCA` in parallel (features = all SS columns + PCA columns) instead of PCA(SS(X)); the branch definition list of a single-branch `branch: [[...]]` is flattened too. Unit test `test_chain_executes_sequentially` shows chain = 10 features, so results silently differ from the user's pipeline. Applies to `PipelineConfigs` and every direct `expand_spec` caller.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): do not use the "starts with a list" heuristic; mark generator-produced sub-pipelines explicitly (or only splice when the single element is itself a generator node). Make `_has_gen_keys` use `GENERATION_KEYWORDS`, not `ALL_KEYWORDS`.

- Category: correctness

  # pipeline.py:449-450

- Impact: DAG bundle transform/probability access can omit learned preprocessing and disagree with predict.

##### CFG-05 — Failed Optuna trials/folds are scored `+inf`, which is the BEST value when the direction is "maximize"

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: NaN validation features yield +inf despite direction=maximize. Objective exception fallbacks also append/return +inf, which Optuna accepts as a complete best value.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/optimization/optuna.py:720`, `:838`, `:864`, `:986` (objective fallbacks); producers `nirs4all/controllers/models/sklearn_model.py:622,626,649` (`_evaluate_model` returns `float('inf')` on NaN/exception) and the same pattern in torch/TF/JAX/AutoGluon `_evaluate_model`
- Category: correctness
- Description: With `metric="r2"`/`"accuracy"`/... the direction is `maximize` (via `is_higher_better`), but every error path returns `+inf` (and a failed fold appends `inf`). Optuna accepts `inf` as a COMPLETE trial value, so the broken configuration wins. `N4MFinetuneManager` handles this correctly (`n4m_engine.py:543` uses `-inf` for maximize); `OptunaManager` does not.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # optuna.py:984-986
  except Exception as e:
      ...
      return float("inf")
  ```
  Repro (`t5.py` test B: `metric="accuracy"`, configs with `max_depth>=8` raise in `_train_model`): `B direction: maximize best_params: {'max_depth': 8} best_value: inf`.
- Impact: any exception/NaN in a candidate (e.g. `n_components` > n_features, divergent NN) makes that candidate the "best" hyper-parameter set for every maximize-direction study (explicit higher-is-better `metric`, or classification default), and it is then used for the refit.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use a direction-aware failure value (`-inf` for maximize, or raise `optuna.TrialPruned`/mark the trial FAIL) in all four objective paths and in the controllers' `_evaluate_model` fallbacks.

##### CFG-06 — `OptunaManager._aggregate_scores` ignores the optimisation direction

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Both best and robust_best aggregate [.9,.5] to .5. They have no direction argument; mean returns .7 normally, so mean is not intrinsically direction-wrong (its inf problem belongs to CFG-05).
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/optimization/optuna.py:1087-1107` (`_aggregate_scores`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `eval_mode="best"` returns `min(scores)` and `"robust_best"` returns `min(valid_scores)` regardless of direction, so for higher-is-better metrics the "best fold" aggregate is the WORST fold. `"mean"` propagates a `+inf` fold failure (see CFG-05). `N4MFinetuneManager._aggregate` takes `direction` and is covered by tests; the Optuna twin was not updated.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # optuna.py:1098-1105
  if eval_mode == "best":    return min(scores)
  elif eval_mode == "mean":  return float(np.mean(scores))
  elif eval_mode == "robust_best":
      valid_scores = [s for s in scores if s != float("inf")]
      return min(valid_scores) if valid_scores else float("inf")
  ```
  Repro: `_aggregate_scores([0.9, 0.5], "best") -> 0.5`; `([0.9, inf], "mean") -> inf`; `([0.9, 0.5, inf], "robust_best") -> 0.5`.
- Impact: with `metric="r2"` (or accuracy) and grouped folds, the optimised quantity is the worst fold, not the best/mean; reported `best_value` is misleading and trial ranking changes.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): pass `direction` and use `max`/`-inf` for maximize (same as `N4MFinetuneManager._aggregate`).

##### CFG-07 — `RotatingRunFileHandler` deadlocks on its first size-based rotation (`max_log_bytes`)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Daemon emit thread remains blocked after max_bytes threshold because emit and _rotate_current_file acquire the same nonreentrant Lock. Test unregisters handler before process shutdown to avoid hanging audit.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/core/logging/handlers.py:276-280` (`emit`) and `:230-235` (`_rotate_current_file`); lock created at `:156`
- Category: concurrency
- Description: `emit` holds `self._lock` (a non-reentrant `threading.Lock`) while calling `_rotate_current_file`, which does `with self._lock:` again. The first time the size limit is exceeded the thread blocks forever (the surrounding `try/except Exception` cannot catch a deadlock).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # handlers.py:276-280
  with self._lock:
      ...
      if self.max_bytes and self._current_size + msg_bytes > self.max_bytes:
          self._rotate_current_file()          # -> with self._lock:  (line 235)
  ```
  Repro (`t11.py`): `RotatingRunFileHandler(log_dir, "r1", max_bytes=50)` + 5 `emit()` calls in a thread -> `emit finished within 5s: False`.
- Impact: `configure_logging(log_file=True, log_dir=..., max_log_bytes=N)` (documented parameter) hangs the program at the first record that crosses the limit.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): make `_lock` an `RLock`, or split `_rotate_current_file` into a lock-free inner method called from `emit`.

  ```
  # component_serialization.py:209-219
  if "." in obj and not obj.endswith(('.pkl', '.h5', '.keras', '.joblib', '.pt', '.pth')):
      try:

  ```
  Repro (`t3.py`/`t4.py`): `serialize_component("./x.csv")` -> `TypeError ... relative import for './x'`; `serialize_component("./ckpt")` -> `ValueError: Empty module name`; `deserialize_component("../a/b.txt")` -> `TypeError`; `PipelineConfigs([Op(ref_path="./ref.csv")])` -> `TypeError`.
- Impact: Some legitimate relative file paths cannot be serialized or decoded; the failing input sets differ between the two functions.

##### CFG-09 — Second-order selection leaves nested generators unexpanded; documented pick syntax is ambiguous

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: then_pick leaves nested _range_ raw; documented pick=[2,(1,2)] raises TypeError. Numeric two-list ranges are deliberately supported, so the latter is a documentation/grammar ambiguity; preserve their legitimate range meaning.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/config/_generator/strategies/or_strategy.py:459-645` (`_handle_pick_then_pick`, `_handle_pick_then_arrange`, `_handle_arrange_then_pick`, `_handle_arrange_then_arrange`; none receives `expand_nested`), `or_strategy.py:673-690` (`_normalize_spec`); docs `docs/source/reference/pipeline_syntax.md:825-835`
- Category: correctness
- Description: then_pick leaves nested _range_ raw; documented pick=[2,(1,2)] raises TypeError. Numeric two-list ranges are deliberately supported, so the latter is a documentation/grammar ambiguity; preserve their legitimate range meaning.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  base = [{"name": "sg", "w": {"_range_": [5, 7, 2]}}, "snv", "msc"]
  expand_spec({"_or_": base, "pick": 1})                      # [[{'name':'sg','w':5}], [{'name':'sg','w':7}], ...]  (4 variants)
  expand_spec({"_or_": base, "pick": 1, "then_pick": 2})      # [[{'name':'sg','w':{'_range_': [5, 7, 2]}}, 'snv'], ...]  (_range_ left raw)
  expand_spec({"_or_": list("ABCD"), "pick": [2, (1, 2)]})    # TypeError: can only concatenate tuple (not "int") to tuple
  ```

- Impact: Nested alternatives remain unresolved; the additional documented pick failure involves a grammar ambiguity, not proof all numeric-list ranges are invalid.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Expand nested generators, then reconcile documented second-order selection syntax with the legitimate numeric-list range grammar.

##### CFG-10 — Tuple hyperparameter ranges lose their range semantics during PipelineConfigs serialization

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: PipelineConfigs turns tuple (1,30) into [1,30]; sampling changes from suggest_int to categorical endpoints. Explicit numeric lists are correctly categorical per API; interpreting all two-lists as ranges would break that contract. Preserve tuple intent or require typed range objects.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/optimization/optuna.py:1293-1306` (list handling) vs `:1313-1322` (tuple `(min, max)`); `nirs4all/optimization/n4m_engine.py:292-298`; conversion at `nirs4all/pipeline/config/component_serialization.py:233-236`
- Category: persistence
- Description: PipelineConfigs turns tuple (1,30) into [1,30]; sampling changes from suggest_int to categorical endpoints. Explicit numeric lists are correctly categorical per API; interpreting all two-lists as ranges would break that contract. Preserve tuple intent or require typed range objects.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  steps = [{"model": PLSRegression(), "finetune_params": {"n_trials": 5, "model_params": {"n_components": (1, 30)}}}]
  fp = PipelineConfigs(steps).steps[0][0]["finetune_params"]   # n_components -> [1, 30]
  # sampling 40 trials from fp: sampled n_components values: [1, 30]
  # un-serialized tuple (1, 30): 21 distinct values
  ```

- Impact: A range supplied as a tuple may explore only its endpoints after serialization. Explicit list categoricals remain legitimate.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Preserve tuple range intent or use an explicit typed range representation. Do not reinterpret numeric two-element lists: those are documented categoricals.

##### CFG-11 — A generator that expands to zero variants is not rejected; legacy lane then runs the raw generator dict as a no-op step

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Invalid pick3 from two options remains an unexpanded raw dict in one PipelineConfigs variant. Empty expansion is not validated at configuration boundary. Executing the claimed legacy no-op was not independently repeated.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/config/pipeline_config.py:75-94` (`PipelineConfigs.__init__`)
- Category: error-handling
- Description: If `count_combinations` returns 0 (e.g. `pick` larger than the number of choices, empty/inverted `_range_`), `if count >= 1:` is skipped, `was_expanded` stays False and the UNEXPANDED template (with literal `_or_`/`_range_` dicts) is wrapped as the single pipeline. If constraints (`_mutex_`...) prune everything while the (constraint-blind) count is >= 1, `PipelineConfigs.steps == []`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  # pipeline_config.py:81, 92-93
  if count >= 1: ... was_expanded = True
  if not was_expanded:
      self.steps = [self.steps]         # raw {'_or_': [...], 'pick': 3} kept as a "step"
  ```
  Repro (`t15.py`/`t16.py`/`t19.py`/`t20.py`): `PipelineConfigs([{"_or_":[S,M],"pick":3}, model])` -> 1 variant whose first step is `{'_or_': [...], 'pick': 3}`; `nirs4all.run(..., engine="legacy")` completes ("DUMMY CONTROLLER ... UNHANDLED OPERATOR", `best_rmse: nan`); default engine -> bare `IndexError`. All-pruned mutex: `PipelineConfigs.steps == []`, legacy -> bare `AssertionError`.
- Impact: a mis-specified sweep yields either a silent run without the sweep step (NaN scores) or an opaque IndexError/AssertionError instead of a clear "generator produced no pipeline" error.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): raise a `ValueError` when expansion yields no variant (count == 0 or empty expansion result).

##### CFG-12 — `PipelineConfigs("<yaml string>")` returns garbage for dict/scalar YAML strings

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Inline YAML root dict loads as ['pipeline']; scalar path without suffix loads as characters. _load_str_steps applies list(yaml.safe_load(...)) rather than the dict/list loader.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/config/pipeline_config.py:336-347` (`_load_str_steps`, line 340)
- Category: correctness
- Description: For a string that is not valid JSON and has no .json/.yaml/.yml suffix, the code does `list(yaml.safe_load(definition))` and skips the `_load_steps` dict handling used for JSON. A YAML dict becomes the list of its keys; a plain scalar string (e.g. a path with an unexpected extension case or no extension) becomes a list of characters.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  PipelineConfigs("pipeline:\n  - class: sklearn.preprocessing.StandardScaler\n")  -> steps ['pipeline']
  PipelineConfigs("configs/my_pipeline")                                           -> steps ['c','o','n','f','i','g', ...] (19 "steps")
  PipelineConfigs("configs/my_pipeline.YAML")                                      -> ValueError: Empty module name
  ```

- Impact: a mistyped/upper-case path or a YAML-string pipeline is silently turned into nonsense steps and fails later with unrelated errors.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): pass the parsed YAML through `_load_steps` (and reject non-list/dict results); match suffixes case-insensitively.

- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/config/_generator/strategies/range_strategy.py:260-266` vs `:204-229`; `grid_strategy.py:153-157`; `or_strategy.py:327-364,417-454` (nested counts unused); `zip_strategy.py:105-106,150`
- Category: correctness
- Description: counts are computed analytically and drift from the real expansion: float ranges use `int((end-start)/step)` (floating error) while expansion uses an epsilon loop; `_grid_` counts `len(list)` although lists containing generator dicts are expanded; `pick`/`arrange` ignore nested generator counts; `_zip_` with an empty list counts 0 but expands to `[{}]`; constraints are ignored (overestimate, can make `count>=1` while expansion is empty).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```
  {"_range_": [0.1, 0.7, 0.1]}                      expand 7, count 6
  {"_range_": [0.0, 0.3, 0.1]}                      expand 4, count 3
  {"_grid_": {"x": [{"_range_":[1,3]}, 10]}}        expand 4, count 2
  {"_or_": [{"_range_":[1,3]}, "B"], "pick": 2}     expand 3, count 1
  ```

- Impact: `PipelineConfigs` can accept configurations bigger than `max_generation_count`, and `has_configurations` can be wrong.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): count with the same primitives as expansion (use `len(expand)` for float ranges; pass `count_nested` through pick/arrange/grid).

##### CFG-16 — Mixed `_or_` dicts leak `_seed_`/`_weights_`/constraint/metadata keys into the output and ignore them for sampling

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Mixed OR expansion emits _seed_ in generated dicts instead of moving it to the sampling node. Source modifier-key allowlist omits seed, weights, constraints and tags.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/config/_generator/core.py:303-305`, `:538-540`, `:696-698`; `nirs4all/pipeline/config/_generator/iterator.py:191-193`
- Category: correctness
- Description: the mixed-node splitter only moves `{_or_, count, pick, arrange, then_pick, then_arrange}` to the OR part. `_seed_`, `_weights_`, `_mutex_`, `_requires_`, `_exclude_`, `_tags_` stay in the base dict, so the seed is not used for the `count` sampling and the key appears in every produced dict.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `expand_spec({"_or_": [{"a":1},{"a":2},{"a":3}], "count": 2, "_seed_": 1, "b": 5})` -> `[{'_seed_': 1, 'b': 5, 'a': 2}, {'_seed_': 1, 'b': 5, 'a': 3}]`.
- Impact: non-reproducible sampling and an unexpected `_seed_` parameter handed to the operator constructor.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): derive the OR-part key set from `PURE_OR_KEYS - {...}` (include `SEED`, `WEIGHTS`, constraints).

##### CFG-17 — `expand_spec_iter` does not normalise the sibling-form `param` sweep / `_grid_`/`_zip_` operator maps

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Sibling param range produces 3 normalized models eagerly but one raw generator dict lazily. Lazy iterator lacks eager normalization.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/config/_generator/iterator.py:100-140` (`_expand_iter_internal`; compare `core.py:228-229`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: the eager path calls `_normalize_param_sweep`/`_normalize_param_grid` before strategy dispatch; the lazy iterator does not.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `{"model": {"class": P}, "_range_": [2, 6, 2], "param": "n_components"}` -> `expand_spec` gives 3 models (n_components 2/4/6); `list(expand_spec_iter(...))` gives one unexpanded dict with `_range_` and `param` keys.
- Impact: the public lazy iterator and `batch_iter` disagree with `expand_spec` for the documented sibling syntax.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): apply the same two normalisations at the top of `_expand_iter_internal`.

##### CFG-18 — `_log_range_` / `_sample_` round values to 10 decimals, turning small values into 0.0

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Log sweep [1e-12,1e-6,4] returns [0,1e-10,1e-8,1e-6]. Absolute rounding to 10 decimals loses positive tiny values; same rounding source exists in sample strategy.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/config/_generator/strategies/log_range_strategy.py:241`; `sample_strategy.py:145` (and `:127`,`:155`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: `round(val, 10)` is absolute, not relative, so log-spaced values below 5e-11 collapse to 0.0 and sub-1e-9 values lose significant digits.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `expand_spec({"_log_range_": [1e-12, 1e-6, 4]})` -> `[0.0, 1e-10, 1e-08, 1e-06]`; `[3e-11, 3e-8, 4]` -> `[0.0, 3e-10, 3e-09, 3e-08]`.
- Impact: a log sweep of tiny regularisation/tolerance values yields `alpha=0.0` (singular fit) instead of the requested start value.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): round with significant digits (e.g. `float(f"{val:.12g}")`).

##### CFG-19 — `is_higher_better("jaccard_score")` is False although `jaccard_score` is a supported higher-is-better metric
