##### CFG-21 — Importing core.metrics installs a broad process-wide Warning ignore filter

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Import installs a process-wide generic Warning ignore filter. Later/specific filters can override it (scipy SpecialFunctionWarning is already always in this environment), so 'ALL warnings disabled' is false literally; broad unrelated-warning suppression is confirmed.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/core/metrics.py:22` (`warnings.filterwarnings('ignore')`)
- Category: other
- Description: Import installs a process-wide generic Warning ignore filter. Later/specific filters can override it (scipy SpecialFunctionWarning is already always in this environment), so 'ALL warnings disabled' is false literally; broad unrelated-warning suppression is confirmed.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): after `import nirs4all.data`, `warnings.warn("user warning")` prints nothing (before the import it is displayed).
- Impact: Unrelated generic warnings may be suppressed; later or more specific warning filters can still override this behavior.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): scope the suppression (`warnings.catch_warnings()` around the sklearn/scipy calls) instead of mutating global filters.

##### CFG-22 — NIRSPipeline inference adapter fails sklearn clone and lacks temporary export cleanup

Temp export directory lacks cleanup in source; no repeated leak measurement made.

- Category: api-contract
- Description: clone(NIRSPipeline()) raises unexpected fold constructor argument; bogus set_params is silently accepted. Wrapper is primarily a fitted inference adapter whose fit is compatibility-only, so advertising trainable GridSearchCV use also requires correcting docs. Temp export directory lacks cleanup in source; no repeated leak measurement made.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `clone(p)` -> `TypeError: NIRSPipeline.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'fold'`; `p.set_params(bogus=1)` returns silently.

##### CFG-23 — Log-file rotation is off by one and can delete the active log (`max_log_runs=1`)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: max_runs=1 deletes active cur.log and old.log, leaving no log files. Current file was created before retention and +1 removes one too many.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/core/logging/handlers.py:210-214` (`_rotate_logs`; called at `:177` after the current file was already created at `:164`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: the current run's file is already in `log_files` when the count rule runs; `excess = len(current_files) - max_runs + 1` then removes one file too many (keeps `max_runs - 1`), and with `max_runs=1` removes every file, including the one being written.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t11.py`): `max_runs=1` with one old log -> files left `[]` (expected `['cur.log']`); `max_runs=3` with three old logs -> `['cur.log', 'old2.log']` (expected three).
- Impact: log retention below the configured count; with `max_log_runs=1` the active log is unlinked (lost on POSIX; `OSError` swallowed on Windows).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): exclude `self._log_file` from `current_files` and use `excess = len(current_files) - self.max_runs + 1`.

##### CFG-24 — `FileFormatter.format` mutates the shared `LogRecord`, corrupting later handlers/JSON output

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Repeated formatting with branch_name and extra_fields doubles appended context; JSON sees mutated text. LogRecord.msg is modified in place at line434.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/core/logging/formatters.py:434` (`record.msg = f"{record.msg} [...]"`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: the extras are appended to `record.msg` in place, so any later formatter of the same record (the JSON log written by `RotatingRunFileHandler.emit`, a second file handler, a repeated format) sees the appended text again.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): formatting one record twice gives `"Model saved [branch=snv, scope=cv, rmse=0.1235] [branch=snv, scope=cv, rmse=0.1235]"`, and `JsonFormatter` afterwards emits `"message": "Model saved [branch=snv, ...] [branch=snv, ...]"`.
- Impact: duplicated/garbled messages in `.jsonl` logs (extras are also present as structured fields).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): format from a copy (`copy.copy(record)`) or build the string without assigning to `record.msg`.

##### CFG-25 — `inspect_workspace_format` builds an unquoted `file:` URI and misreports legacy workspaces under paths containing `#`, `%` or `?`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Legacy prediction_arrays detected under plain path, but paths with #, percent or question mark misclassified v2; unescaped SQLite file URI truncates/decodes the path.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/workspace/compat.py:63-71` (`_sqlite_has_prediction_arrays`)
- Category: persistence
- Description: `f"file:{path.as_posix()}?mode=ro"` is not percent-encoded; sqlite fails to open such paths, the `sqlite3.DatabaseError` is swallowed and `False` ("no legacy table") is returned. `upgrade.py` uses `database.as_uri()` correctly.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`store.sqlite` with a `prediction_arrays` table): `ws_plain -> sqlite-workspace-legacy-arrays`; `ws#1`, `ws%41`, `ws?x -> sqlite-workspace-v2` (conversion not required).
- Impact: a legacy (prediction_arrays) workspace in such a directory is reported as current V1/V2, so no conversion warning is shown.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use `path.resolve().as_uri() + "?mode=ro"` (or `urllib.parse.quote`).

#### Storage, bundles (.n4a), trace (STO)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 16 STO claims were checked against persistence callers, SQLite state and archive reload behavior. Fresh probes cover refcounts, failed deletes, overwritten bundles, interrupted transactions, traversal and supported JAX serialization failures. 651 existing storage/bundle/trace tests passed.

##### STO-01 — Deleting one chain's predictions deletes artifacts still used by sibling chains (ref_count undercounts shared artifacts)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Two surviving chains reference the same artifact row with ref_count=1; deleting the first prediction prunes its chain and deletes the artifact row while the sibling chain remains.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/workspace_store.py:3998` (`WorkspaceStore._prune_empty_chains`), `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/workspace_store.py:1876` (`register_existing_artifact`), `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/store_queries.py:241` (`INSERT_ARTIFACT`), `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/workspace_store.py:3414` + `:3741` (`gc_artifacts`)
- Category: persistence
- Description: `register_existing_artifact` inserts one `artifacts` row per artifact id with `ref_count=1` and is idempotent for repeated registration (no increment). `ChainBuilder` gives every model chain of a pipeline the same shared preprocessing artifact ids (`shared_artifacts`), so N chains reference one row with `ref_count=1`. When the last prediction of ONE chain is deleted, `_prune_empty_chains` decrements every artifact id of that chain, the count reaches 0, and `gc_artifacts` deletes the row (unconditionally, `DELETE_GC_ARTIFACTS`) and the file if no other live row shares its path. The sibling chain still points to the now-unknown artifact.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# nirs4all/pipeline/storage/workspace_store.py
3998            for aid in self._collect_artifact_ids_from_chain_row(row_dict):
3999                conn.execute(DECREMENT_ARTIFACT_REF, [aid])
4001            conn.execute(DELETE_CHAIN, [chain_id])
# store_queries.py:241  INSERT INTO artifacts (...) VALUES (..., 1)   -- ref_count fixed to 1 per artifact id
```
Repro (`<audit-scratch>/sto/repro_real.py`, real run, `engine="legacy"`, pipeline `[MinMaxScaler(), ShuffleSplit(2), {"model": PLS(2)}, {"model": PLS(3)}]`): both chains list the same `shared_artifacts {"1": ["<id>$7c1fa10edc2c:all"]}`, scaler row `ref_count=1`. After `store.delete_prediction(...)` for every prediction of chain 1 the scaler row is gone (5 artifact rows -> 2) and `store.export_chain(<chain 2>, ...)` raises `KeyError: 'Unknown artifact: ...$7c1fa10edc2c:all'` (same for `load_artifact`/`replay_chain`).
- Impact: any multi-model pipeline (several `{"model": ...}` steps or branches sharing a preprocessing prefix): deleting a model's predictions (Studio "delete prediction", `delete_chain_predictions`, `delete_dataset_predictions` of a subset) permanently destroys the preprocessing artifact (row and file) needed to replay/export every other model of the pipeline.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): count references per chain (increment on `save_chain`, or compute liveness by scanning `chains.fold_artifacts/shared_artifacts` of surviving chains in `gc_artifacts`) instead of a fixed `ref_count=1`.

##### STO-02 — `delete_run` is not atomic and aborts with `IntegrityError` when tuning/conformal/robustness results reference the run; the aborted delete leaves surviving chains whose artifacts get garbage-collected later

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Tuning-result fixture: delete_run raises IntegrityError, leaves run/pipeline/chain but deletes all predictions and changes artifact ref_count to0. Auxiliary-result FKs are not deleted and no transaction encloses the cascade.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/workspace_store.py:3241-3255` (`WorkspaceStore.delete_run`); same pattern in `:3403` / `:4001` (`_delete_predictions_matching`, `_prune_empty_chains`); schema FKs at `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/store_schema.py:190-193,207-209,226-230`
- Category: persistence
- Description: `conformal_results`, `tuning_results` and `robustness_results` carry `REFERENCES runs/pipelines/chains/predictions` and `create_schema` turns `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`. `save_tuning_result(run_id=..., pipeline_id=..., chain_id=...)` (and the conformal/robustness equivalents, exposed by `api/tuning.py`, `api/calibrate.py`, `api/robustness.py`) store those ids. `delete_run` never deletes those dependent rows and runs ~6 autocommit statements without a transaction: `_decrement_artifact_refs_for_run` (3241), then logs/predictions deleted, then the `chains` delete raises `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
3241            self._decrement_artifact_refs_for_run(run_id)
3247            conn.execute(CASCADE_DELETE_RUN_LOGS, [run_id])
3248            conn.execute(CASCADE_DELETE_RUN_PREDICTIONS, [run_id])
3249            conn.execute(CASCADE_DELETE_RUN_CHAINS, [run_id])      # <- IntegrityError here
```
Repro (`<audit-scratch>/sto/repro_fk.py`, using the repo's own test fixtures `_create_full_run` + `_make_tuning_result`): `before {'runs':1,'pipelines':1,'chains':1,'predictions':1,'artifacts':1,'tuning_results':1}`, `delete_run raised: IntegrityError FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`, `after {'runs':1,'pipelines':1,'chains':1,'predictions':0,...}`, artifact `ref_count` is now 0 although the chain survives, and after any later `gc_artifacts()` (every delete triggers it) `load_artifact(...)` of the surviving chain raises `KeyError: Unknown artifact`.
- Impact: a run that has a persisted tuning/conformal/robustness result linked to it can never be deleted; the failed attempt silently destroys its predictions and (at the next GC) the artifacts of chains that remain in the workspace; Parquet arrays are orphaned (tombstone at 3255 never reached).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): wrap the delete in `self.transaction()`, delete (or null) dependent `*_results` rows first, and decrement artifact refs only after the row deletes succeeded.

##### STO-03 — `.n4a.py` export through the resolver path embeds no artifacts for store-resolved predictions (generated script cannot predict)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh explicit legacy run and store-resolved BundleGenerator export embeds ARTIFACTS={} and generated predict raises No model step found. Scope is resolver-based legacy export, not native export.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/bundle/generator.py:856` (`BundleGenerator._generate_portable_script`); the missing fallback exists in the sibling `_write_artifacts_to_zip` at `:730`
- Category: correctness
- Description: `_generate_portable_script` collects artifacts only `if resolved.trace and resolved.artifact_provider`. A prediction resolved from the workspace store (the normal case for a run in a workspace) has `trace=None` and a `MapArtifactProvider`; `_write_artifacts_to_zip` knows this ("Store-resolved predictions have no trace; get steps from artifact_map", line 730) and falls back to `artifact_map`, but the portable-script generator has no such fallback, so `ARTIFACTS = {}` and no warning is emitted.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
856        if resolved.trace and resolved.artifact_provider:
857            for step in resolved.trace.steps:
...                                  # no else / artifact_map fallback (compare :730-732)
```
Repro (`<audit-scratch>/sto/repro_py2.py`): legacy-engine run (`KFold(3)`, PLS), `BundleGenerator(ws, store=store).export(res.best, "r.n4a.py", format="n4a.py")` -> `source_type: prediction trace: False provider: MapArtifactProvider`, embedded artifact keys `[]` (also for `refit=True`). Running the script: `predict()` raises `RuntimeError: No model step found in pipeline`.
- Impact: `RunResult.export(path, format="n4a.py")` (legacy engine) succeeds and returns a file that is a valid-looking but empty "portable predictor".
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): mirror the `artifact_map` fallback of `_write_artifacts_to_zip`, and raise if no artifact was collected. (Secondary, same function, by reading: for V3 ids `artifact_id.split(":")` has 2 parts, so `fold_part` at `:867` is always `"all"` and per-fold models would be emitted as `step_N`/`step_N_subK` instead of `step_N_foldK`.)

##### STO-04 — `export_from_chain(..., "n4a.py")` emits keys the generated script cannot parse (`step_3_foldfold_0`) and crashes on multi-source chains

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: A fitted LinearRegression chain with canonical fold_0 exports step_1_foldfold_0; generated predictor raises ValueError parsing fold_0. The _source_map failure is confirmed by reading, not fresh multisource execution.
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path; this claim does not establish an equivalent native/dag-ml defect.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/bundle/generator.py:348` and `:328` (`BundleGenerator._export_chain_as_py`); parser in the embedded template at `:1060`
- Category: correctness
- Description: chain `fold_artifacts` keys are canonical (`fold_0`, `fold_final`, via `normalize_fold_key`), but the key is built as `f"step_{model_step_idx}_fold{fold_id}"`, giving `step_3_foldfold_0`. The script's `get_fold_artifacts` does `int(key.split("_fold")[1])` -> `int("fold_0")`. `WorkspaceStore.export_chain` strips the prefix (`removeprefix("fold_")`, `workspace_store.py:3015`) and skips `_`-prefixed metadata keys (`:3007`); `_export_chain_as_py` does neither: `int(str_idx)` at `:328` fails on the `_source_map` key written by `ChainBuilder` for multi-source steps.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
326            if not artifact_ids_val: continue
328            idx = int(str_idx)                     # '_source_map' -> ValueError
...
348            artifacts_data[f"step_{model_step_idx}_fold{fold_id}"] = encoded   # fold_id == "fold_0"
# template (:1060): fold_id = int(key.split("_fold")[1])
```
Repro (`<audit-scratch>/sto/repro_py.py`): 3-fold chain: generated keys `['"step_1"', '"step_3_foldfold_0"', '"step_3_foldfold_1"', '"step_3_foldfold_2"']`; `runpy` + `predict(X[:3])` -> `ValueError: invalid literal for int() with base 10: 'fold_0'`. Multi-source run (`repro_srcmap.py`): `export_from_chain(cid, p, "n4a.py")` -> `ValueError: invalid literal for int() with base 10: '_source_map'` (while `export_chain(..., "n4a")` on the same chain works).
- Impact: the documented "preferred" store-based `.n4a.py` export never yields a working script for any CV/refit chain.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): reuse the naming of `WorkspaceStore.export_chain` (strip `fold_`, skip `_*` keys).

##### STO-05 — Resolver export creates duplicate ZIP members; reload selects the last member

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Filename contains step/fold/class but no source/substep/artifact identity; two same-class artifacts in one step produce identical ZIP paths. ZIP retains both entries; name-based reload/index keeps the last. Do not claim ZIP physically overwrites the preceding member.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.

##### STO-07 — ArtifactRegistry never repairs a truncated artifact file (existence is treated as integrity; writes are non-atomic)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**

A file truncated by a crash/ENOSPC (the workspace disk did fill up on 2026-09-26) keeps the final name. On the next run with identical fitted content `_find_existing_by_hash` correctly rejects the file (hash mismatch, lines 1365-1372) but the code then computes the same path, sees `exists()` and skips the write; the record points to the corrupt file.

445                if not artifact_path.exists():
446                    artifact_path.write_bytes(content)
```

- Impact: after one interrupted run, every later run/export that reuses identical fitted content registers (and later fails to load) a corrupt artifact; no automatic recovery.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): write to a temp name and `os.replace`; or overwrite when the existing file's hash differs from `content_hash`.

##### STO-08 — `WorkspaceStore.transaction()` only rolls back on `Exception`: a `KeyboardInterrupt` leaves the connection inside an open write transaction

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: KeyboardInterrupt inside store.transaction leaves connection.in_transaction=True; except Exception cannot handle BaseException. Store.close releases the lock; no permanent on-disk lock claim.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/workspace_store.py:625` (`WorkspaceStore.transaction`)
- Category: concurrency
- Description: the docstring says "Rolls back on any exception", but `except Exception:` does not catch `KeyboardInterrupt`/`SystemExit`. The transaction stays open, `conn.in_transaction` is True, so every later `transaction()` on the store takes the "join the enclosing transaction" branch (never commits) and other connections cannot write.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
621            conn.execute("BEGIN TRANSACTION")
622            try:
623                yield
624                conn.execute("COMMIT")
625            except Exception:
626                conn.execute("ROLLBACK")
```
Repro (`<audit-scratch>/sto/repro_misc.py`): `with st.transaction(): st.begin_run(...); raise KeyboardInterrupt` -> `conn.in_transaction after KeyboardInterrupt: True`; a second `WorkspaceStore` on the same workspace then fails `database is locked`.
- Impact: Ctrl-C / notebook kernel interrupt during `run()` (orchestrator and `Predictions.flush` run inside `transaction()`) leaves the workspace write-locked until the old store object is closed/collected; further writes through that store are silently never committed.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): `except BaseException:` (rollback, re-raise).

- Category: security

75        category_path = self.library_path / category
231        shutil.rmtree(template_path)
396        invalid_chars = '<>:"/\\|?*'     # '.' not handled
```

With templates `general/keep` and `other/keep2`, `delete_template("..", category="general")` raises `FileNotFoundError` after deleting everything: `library dir GONE | keep2 exists: False`.
- Impact: if template name/category come from a UI/REST request (Studio library panel), a crafted value reads/writes arbitrary directories or deletes all saved templates.

##### STO-11 — SQLite retry wrappers retry every `OperationalError` (~30 s stall) instead of only lock conflicts

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Both retries catch every sqlite3.OperationalError; source has no locked/busy discrimination. Delay is exponential+jitter and exact~30seconds is environment dependent; no new wall-clock delay measurement.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/workspace_store.py:202-214` (`_retry_on_lock`), `:544-555` (`_execute_with_retry`)
- Category: error-handling
- Description: both wrappers catch `sqlite3.OperationalError` without inspecting the message. "database or disk is full", "attempt to write a readonly database", "no such table/column" are permanent but are retried 8 times with exponential backoff (0.15 s * 2^k, total ~30 s) before being raised; `log_step`/`fail_run` use `_safe_execute`, so each call stalls ~30 s and then silently drops the log.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `_execute_with_retry("UPDATE no_such_table SET x = 1")` (`<audit-scratch>/sto/repro_retry.py`) -> `raised: no such table: no_such_table`, `retries: 8 total requested sleep: 30.2s`.
- Impact: on a full disk / read-only workspace the pipeline appears to hang (30 s per logged event) before failing.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): retry only when `"locked" in str(e) or "busy" in str(e)`.

##### STO-12 — `list_runs(dataset=...)` uses an unescaped substring `LIKE`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Dataset filter is LIKE '%name%' on JSON text; substring and SQL wildcard characters match unrelated dataset names. Bound SQL parameter prevents SQL injection; bug is filter semantics.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/workspace_store.py:2362-2365` (`WorkspaceStore.list_runs`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: the docstring says "runs that reference this dataset name in their datasets list", but the filter is `CAST(datasets AS TEXT) LIKE '%<name>%'` over the raw JSON, so it matches substrings of other dataset names, of any JSON key/value, and `_`/`%` in the name act as wildcards.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): runs with datasets `corn_2024` and `wheat`: `list_runs(dataset="corn")` -> `['a']` (the `corn_2024` run), `list_runs(dataset="co_n_2024")` -> `['a']` (`<audit-scratch>/sto/repro_misc.py`).
- Impact: dataset filters in run listings return runs of other datasets whose name contains the query (and underscores in dataset names are wildcards).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): filter on parsed JSON (`json_each(datasets)` with `json_extract(value,'$.name') = ?`) or escape `%`/`_` and compare quoted names.

##### STO-16 — `_write_artifacts_to_zip` swallows per-artifact serialization failures and still writes the bundle

Reload/predict then raises No model step found in bundle. This validates a supported-model trigger, beyond an artificial unpicklable object. DAG FrameworkEstimator uses a different cloudpickle path.

- Category: error-handling

A bundle missing a fitted preprocessing artifact is still a valid ZIP; `BundleLoader` only iterates steps that have artifacts (`_predict_from_index`, `loader.py:1173-1181`), so the step is silently skipped at predict time. (`BundleLoader` itself treats an unreadable indexed artifact as fatal, `BundleArtifactLoadError`, so the exporter is inconsistent with that stance.) Trigger needs an object registered with cloudpickle but not plain-joblib-picklable.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
760                except Exception as e:
761                    logger.warning(f"Failed to serialize artifact {artifact_id}: {e}")
```

- Impact: export reports success and later predictions skip a preprocessing step (wrong values without error).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): raise (or collect failures and raise after the loop).

### 5.5 Data layer — `nirs4all/data/`

#### Dataset, Predictions, indexer, targets (DT1)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 24 original DT1 claims were reviewed against selectors, store-backed maintenance and target contracts. Fresh store probes distinguish deletion, merging and metric ranking defects. DT1-25 is a new zero-fraction deletion bug; DT1-23 is grouped under CFG-19.

##### DT1-01 — Test partition with fewer features is silently zero-padded; headers are overwritten with the shorter test headers

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Dataset train4cols/test3cols becomes4cols with a zero last column; header list is replaced with3headers. Padding implementation is intentional generally but incompatible train/test schemas are not validated.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/_features/array_storage.py:386` (`ArrayStorage._prepare_data_for_storage`), `nirs4all/data/_features/feature_source.py:171` (`FeatureSource.add_samples`), `nirs4all/data/_features/feature_source.py:39` (`padding=True` default), caller `nirs4all/data/config.py:709` (`DatasetConfigs._load_dataset`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `Features.add_samples` builds `FeatureSource()` with `padding=True`; when a second `add_samples` call (the test set) has fewer columns than the first (train), the new rows are padded with `0.0` instead of raising. Then the headers (and unit) passed with the test set replace the train headers, so `len(headers) != num_features`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
386  if self.padding and data.shape[1] < self.num_features:
387      padded_data = np.full((data.shape[0], self.num_features), self.pad_value, dtype=self.dtype)
392      padded_data[:, :data.shape[1]] = data
171  if headers is not None:
173      self._header_mgr.set_headers(headers, unit=current_unit)   # overwrites train headers
```
  Repro (`dt1/r12.py`, real CSV loading through `DatasetConfigs`): train CSV with 12 wavelengths, test CSV with 10 -> `train (10,12)`, `test (5,12)`, test row tail `[1.369 0. 0.]`, `len(ds.headers(0)) == 10` vs 12 features. (`dt1/r11.py` shows the same via `add_samples`). Expected: `ValueError` on train/test feature-count mismatch.
- Impact: a test set exported with a different spectral range/number of variables loads without any error; the model is evaluated on spectra whose last columns are zeros and whose wavelength headers no longer match the data. Silent wrong test scores. (More features than train raises a confusing `np.concatenate` ValueError, and leaves the indexer out of sync, see DT1-19.)
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): in `Features.add_samples` / `FeatureSource.add_samples` raise on `data.shape[1] != num_features` (padding is only meaningful for the 3D batch/processing paths), and do not overwrite existing headers with ones of a different length/content.

##### DT1-02 — `Predictions.remove_bottom` deletes the BEST predictions for higher-is-better metrics

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Five accuracy rows .5,.6,.7,.8,.95; remove_bottom(.4) leaves .5,.6,.7. Direction inferred from val instead of stored metric deletes the two best rows.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/predictions.py:3108` (`Predictions.remove_bottom`)
- Category: correctness (data loss)
- Description: the sort direction is inferred from the column name stripped of `_score` (`"val_score"` -> `"val"`), which is never a known metric, so `is_higher_better("val")` is `False` and "lower is better" is always assumed. The `*_score` columns hold the score of the row's own `metric`; for accuracy/balanced_accuracy/r2/F1 rows the "bottom" (tail) is therefore the highest score.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
3108  ascending = _infer_ascending(metric.replace("_score", ""))      # "val" -> unknown -> lower-is-better
3109  sorted_df = df.sort(metric, descending=not ascending)
3112  to_remove = sorted_df.tail(n_remove)                              # = highest scores
```
  Repro (`dt1/r5.py`): 5 binary-classification predictions with `val_score` (accuracy) = .50,.60,.70,.80,.95; `remove_bottom(0.4)` -> remaining val_scores `[0.5, 0.6, 0.7]` (the two best, 0.80 and 0.95, were deleted from SQLite and the arrays tombstoned). Existing unit test only checks the removed count.
- Impact: the maintenance helper destroys the best models of any classification (or R2-ranked) workspace and keeps the worst; irreversible. It also mixes metrics across datasets (df is not grouped by `metric`).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): derive direction per row from the `metric` column (`is_higher_better(row.metric)`), e.g. rank by direction-normalised score within each metric group.

##### DT1-03 — `merge_stores` treats rows already merged from the same source as conflicts (natural key too coarse): data loss, or FK crash with the default `keep_best`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh source store with three distinct pipelines and identical model/fold/partition: keep_best raises IntegrityError and leaves the target with zero predictions; keep_existing keeps one of the three variants (two conflicts). The cached merge target chain is pruned during conflict replacement.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/predictions.py:2824-2848` (`_merge_resolve_conflict`), cached merge chain at `predictions.py:2780-2806` (`_merge_ensure_target_context`)
- Category: correctness / persistence
- Description: the conflict key is `(model_name, fold_id, partition)` within a dataset, compared against the whole target store, which already contains rows merged earlier from the same source. Any source workspace holding two predictions with the same key (a preprocessing/hyper-parameter sweep with the same model, or the same model run twice) is therefore self-conflicting. With `keep_existing` all but the first are silently dropped; with `keep_best`/`overwrite` the "replaced" row is the only prediction of the cached merge chain, `delete_prediction` prunes the now-empty chain/pipeline, and the following `save_prediction` raises `sqlite3.IntegrityError: FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` (partial merge left behind).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
2828  if ex_row.get("model_name") == row.get("model_name") and ex_row.get("fold_id") == row.get("fold_id") and ex_row.get("partition") == row.get("partition"):
2837      target_store.delete_prediction(ex_row["prediction_id"])      # may prune the cached merge chain/pipeline
```
  Repro (`dt1/r25.py`): source store with 3 pipelines (preprocessings SNV/MSC/SG, same model_name "PLSRegression", fold 0, partition val). `keep_existing` -> `merged=1 conflicts=2`, target has only `['SNV']`; `keep_best` and `overwrite` -> `IntegrityError: FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`.
- Impact: merging any realistic workspace (sweeps, repeated runs) with the default `on_conflict="keep_best"` crashes midway, or silently collapses distinct pipelines into one row per (model, fold, partition).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): only test conflicts against rows that existed in the target before the merge started (snapshot ids), and include pipeline/chain identity (`config_name`/`preprocessings`/`model_class`/`branch`) in the natural key.

##### DT1-04 — `Predictions.top()` drops the refit (`final`) entries of all but one dataset (dedupe key lacks `dataset_name`)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Two in-memory final rows for datasetsA/B, same model/preprocessing/no pipeline_id: top(scope=refit,n=10) returns onlyA. Persisted rows with distinct pipeline IDs may be unaffected; scope must be stated.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/predictions.py:1446` (`Predictions._dedupe_final_candidates`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: finals are deduplicated by `(model_name, pipeline_id, preprocessings)`. In-memory run predictions have no `pipeline_id` (only set when reloaded from a store), so the key degenerates to `(model_name, None, preprocessings)` and the finals of different datasets collide; only the first survives.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `identity = (c.get("model_name"), c.get("pipeline_id"), c.get("preprocessings"))`. Repro (`dt1/r27.py dag-ml` and `legacy`, two named datasets A and B, PLS + KFold + refit): buffer contains finals for A and B, but `preds.top(n=10, score_scope="refit")` -> `[('A','PLSRegression')]`; `top(n=5, group_by="dataset_name")` (default scope) lists A's `final` but no B `final`.
- Impact: in multi-dataset runs `result.top(..., score_scope="refit")`, per-dataset best/refit selection and any report built on it silently omit the refit model of every dataset but the first.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): include `dataset_name` (and `chain_id`/`pipeline_uid`/`branch_id`) in the identity tuple.

##### DT1-05 — Display metrics for a partition that has no rows are computed from the entry's own partition and reported as that partition

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Validation-only prediction with RMSE.2: top(display_partition=test,display_metrics=rmse/median_ae) returns.2 for both despite test_score=None. Missing-display arrays retain original partition arrays.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/predictions.py:2642-2648` and `:2684` (`Predictions._enrich_display_metrics`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `display_y_true/display_y_pred` start as the entry's own (e.g. `val`) arrays; they are only replaced when `get_entry_partitions(row)[display_partition]` exists. When it is `None` (no test set) the code keeps the val arrays and the fallback branch evaluates every metric not present in `scores[display_partition]` on them. `top()` defaults to `display_partition="test"`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
2643  display_y_true = y_true            # entry partition arrays (val)
2646  if entry_partition != display_partition:
2648      if display_part is not None: ...   # no else: val arrays stay in place
2684  elif display_y_true is not None and ... .size > 0: enriched[m] = evaluator.eval(display_y_true, display_y_pred, m)
```
  Repro (`dt1/r15.py`, KFold CV without test set): `top(n=2, rank_partition="val", display_partition="test", display_metrics=["rmse","median_ae"])` shows `rmse=0.19247` which equals `val_score` (`test_score=None`, `scores['test']={}`).
- Impact: `result.top(n, display_metrics=[...])` on any dataset without a test partition reports validation (OOF) metrics labelled as test metrics instead of `None`.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): when `display_part is None` (or has no arrays) set `display_y_true = display_y_pred = None` so the metric resolves to `None`.

##### DT1-06 — `test_x` without `test_y` (documented optional) crashes dataset loading; unlabeled partitions also break `y()`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Public DatasetConfigs with trainX/trainY/testX/no testY raises Target data has0targets expected1. The secondary unlabeled-dataset y access remains confirmed by row-indexing source.

- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/loaders/loader.py:285`, `nirs4all/data/config.py:722-723` (`DatasetConfigs._load_dataset`), `nirs4all/data/targets.py:385-386` (`Targets.add_targets`); secondary `nirs4all/data/targets.py:491-493`
- Category: correctness / api-contract
- Description: when no `*_y` file is configured the loader returns an empty `(n, 0)` array instead of `None`; `_load_dataset` only guards `is not None`, so `add_targets(y_test)` is called with 0 target columns after train targets exist and raises `Target data has 0 targets, expected 1`. `docs/source/user_guide/data/loading_data.md:34` lists `test_y.csv` as optional.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
loader.py:285      y = y_df.values if not y_df.empty else np.empty((x_df.shape[0], 0))
config.py:722-723  if y_test is not None: dataset.add_targets(y_test)
```
  Repro (`dt1/r20.py legacy dag-ml`): train_x/train_y/test_x CSVs -> both engines raise `ValueError: Target data has 0 targets, expected 1`. If the targets are skipped, `ds.y({"partition":"test"})` and `ds.y({})` raise `IndexError: index 6 is out of bounds` (`dt1/r19.py`) because target rows are addressed by sample id and only exist for labelled base samples.
- Impact: predicting/evaluating an unlabeled external test set through `DatasetConfigs` is impossible although documented as supported.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): treat a zero-column `y` as "no targets" in `_load_dataset` (and make `Targets.y` return an empty array for ids beyond the stored rows).

##### DT1-07 — `merge_stores(on_conflict="keep_best")` never skips a worse source row and hard-codes lower-is-better

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Two distinct source workspaces with RMSE.1 then.5 and keep_best merge both rows [.1,.5]. Worse row is not skipped. Higher-is-better comparison also hard-coded wrongly by reading.

- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/predictions.py:2832-2837` and `:2843-2848` (`_merge_resolve_conflict`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: for `keep_best`, when the existing row is better (`src_score >= ex_score`) the code `break`s without deleting, but the function then returns `False`, so the worse source row is still saved (duplicate natural key). The comparison also assumes lower-is-better for every metric, so for accuracy/r2 the worse source (lower score) replaces the better existing row.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `if src_score is not None and ex_score is not None and src_score >= ex_score: break` followed by `return False` (line 2848).
  Repro (`dt1/r6.py`): rmse existing 0.1 vs source 0.9 -> target keeps both `[0.1, 0.9]`; accuracy existing 0.9 vs source 0.1 -> target ends with only `[0.1]` (best prediction deleted).
- Impact: merged workspaces contain duplicates or lose the best prediction; `conflicts_resolved` is misleading.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): return `True` (skip) when the existing score is at least as good, and compare with `is_higher_better(row["metric"])`.

##### DT1-08 — Empty selections return ALL targets / metadata rows instead of none

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**

- Category: correctness

- Impact: any caller asking for a partition that does not exist (test/val on CV-only data) silently receives the training targets/metadata.

##### DT1-09 — `BinningCalculator.bin_continuous_targets` returns `bins+1` classes: the minimum value gets its own bin

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: np.linspace0..1,100samples,bins5 yields labels0..5. Digitization includes both outer edges and right=True isolates the minimum.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/binning.py:58` (`np.digitize(y, bin_edges, right=True)`)
- Category: correctness (off-by-one)
- Description: `digitize` with all `bins+1` edges and `right=True` maps `y == edges[0]` to 0 and `(e0,e1]` to 1 ... `(e_{b-1}, e_b]` to `b`, i.e. indices `0..bins`. The first interval is never closed on the left, so the minimum value is isolated in a singleton class. Docstring: "0-based bin index", `bins` bins.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`dt1/r4.py`, 100 uniform values, `bins=5`): `equal_width` counts `[1, 18, 15, 17, 22, 27]`, `quantile` `[1, 19, 20, 20, 20, 20]` (6 classes). Impact demonstrated in `dt1/r14.py` with `BalancingCalculator.calculate_balanced_counts(..., ref_percentage=1.0)` on 200 samples, `bins=10`: 11 labels; the single minimum-y sample is assigned 23 augmentations (cloned 24x) to bring its singleton class up to the majority size.
- Impact: regression `sample_augmentation` with `balance="y"` (default `bins=10`) over-samples the extreme-low sample; `analysis/playground_partition.py:101` stratifies on the same labels (singleton class -> StratifiedKFold warnings).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): digitize against the interior edges only (`np.digitize(y, edges[1:-1], right=True)`), so labels are `0..bins-1`.

##### DT1-10 — `top(score_scope="refit")` ranks on the stored `val_score` but applies the direction of the requested `rank_metric`

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Two final rows with stored RMSE 0.02/0.5 rank good/bad for rmse but bad/good for r2. The selection score is deliberately pre-test evidence: fix should validate/reconcile requested metric or obtain CV evidence, not recompute ranks from held-out test arrays as initial suggestion implies.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/data/predictions.py:1527-1532` (`Predictions._rank_candidates`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Two final rows with stored RMSE 0.02/0.5 rank good/bad for rmse but bad/good for r2. The selection score is deliberately pre-test evidence: fix should validate/reconcile requested metric or obtain CV evidence, not recompute ranks from held-out test arrays as initial suggestion implies.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`dt1/r24.py`, two finals with stored rmse 0.019 ("good") and 0.385 ("bad")): `scope=refit rank_metric=rmse -> good, bad`; `scope=refit rank_metric=r2 -> bad, good`; `scope=all rank_metric=r2` correctly recomputes -> `good (1.0), bad (0.926)`.
- Impact: A refit ranking requested in an incompatible metric may invert the intended order. Repair must continue using pre-test CV selection evidence.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Reconcile the requested metric with CV selection evidence, or reject an incompatible request. Do not select models by recomputing ranks on held-out test targets.

##### DT1-11 — `get_entry_partitions` matches entries without `branch_id`/`pipeline_uid`/`preprocessings`, attaching another branch's train/val/test rows

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: get_entry_partitions scans buffer on dataset/config/model/fold/step only; branch_id,chain_id,pipeline_uid/preprocessings are absent. Branches sharing these fields can attach the first branch's partition.

- Location: `nirs4all/data/predictions.py:2109-2120` (`Predictions.get_entry_partitions`), used by `:2647` (`_enrich_display_metrics`) and `:2745` (`_enrich_result`, `aggregate_partitions=True`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: the entry identity is `(dataset_name, config_name, model_name, fold_id, step_idx)`. Duplication branches share all of these (same config, same model, same `step_idx`), so the first branch's rows win for every branch.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `[SNV]` / `[MSC]` branches + PLS + KFold (legacy engine, `dt1/r7.py`): all buffer rows are `PLSRegression, fold, step_idx=3, config_5ac42a46` with `branch_id` 0/1. For the MSC entry, `top(..., aggregate_partitions=True, branch_id=1)` returns `partitions -> {'train': (0,'SNV'), 'val': (0,'SNV')}`, and a display metric not pre-computed (`median_ae`, `display_partition="train"`) is `0.0801` (SNV branch) instead of the MSC value `0.3271`.
- Impact: `aggregate_partitions=True` payloads and fallback display metrics of every branch but the first describe the wrong branch.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): add `branch_id`, `branch_path`, `pipeline_uid`/`chain_id` and `preprocessings` to the match.

##### DT1-12 — Metadata has no rows for augmented samples: `metadata()`/`metadata_column()` are shorter than `x()`/`y()`, `metadata_numeric()` raises

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Metadata stores only base row_ids, metadata accessor passes augmented row IDs directly; get/get_column omit unmatched rows; to_numeric searches row_id and indexes empty np.where. Targets remap origins, metadata does not.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/data/_dataset/metadata_accessor.py:74,96,124`, `nirs4all/data/metadata.py:101-112,134-137,167-172,213-216`
- Category: correctness (index alignment)
- Description: `add_samples_batch` registers augmented samples in the indexer/features but not in `Metadata` (row ids = base samples only). With `include_augmented=True` (the documented default) `x_indices` returns the augmented ids too; `Metadata.get/get_column` filter by `row_id.is_in(indices)` and silently drop them (and return rows in table order, not index order); `to_numeric` does `np.where(all_row_ids == idx)[0][0]` -> `IndexError`. `y()` maps augmented ids to their origin instead.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`dt1/r28.py`, 6 base + 2 augmented): `X rows: 8 | y rows: 8 | metadata_column rows: 6 | metadata rows: 6`; `metadata_numeric -> IndexError: index 0 is out of bounds for axis 0 with size 0`.
- Impact: any consumer pairing X/y with a metadata column on an augmented partition (chart colouring, prediction metadata for the train partition, aggregation keys) gets misaligned/shorter arrays (the assembler paths drop the metadata when lengths differ) or an `IndexError`.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): resolve augmented ids to their origin (as `TargetAccessor.y` does) before indexing metadata, preserving the request order.

##### DT1-13 — Multi-source signal-type detection ignores `src` and runs on the concatenation of all sources

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Sources absorbance.3..1.5 and reflectance20..80 yield identical UNKNOWN detection with range.3..80 for both src0/src1. Detection reads concatenated source features.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/dataset.py:584-586` (`SpectroDataset._detect_signal_type`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `self.x({"partition": "train"}, layout="2d")` uses the default `concat_source=True`, so it never returns a list; the `isinstance(spectra, list)` branch is dead and every source is analysed on the same concatenated matrix (also flattening all processings), while wavelengths are taken from `src` (length mismatch disables the band heuristic).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`dt1/r2.py`, source 0 absorbance-like 0.3-1.5, source 1 percent reflectance 20-80): `detect_signal_type(0)` and `(1)` both return `UNKNOWN, 0.625, 'Range: [0.300, 79.970] ...'`; per source alone: `ABSORBANCE (1.0)` and `REFLECTANCE_PERCENT (0.71)`.
- Impact: `signal_type(src)`/`signal_types` are wrong for multi-source datasets (any auto-conversion depending on them).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): call `self.x(..., concat_source=False)` and take `spectra[src]` (keep the 3D->2D flattening per source).

##### DT1-14 — `rep_to_sources` / `rep_to_pp` rebuild discards wavelength headers/unit and the original class labels

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Rebuilt feature sources are fresh without headers/unit. Targets rebuilt from numeric encoded view replace raw labels; original encoder chain is not preserved.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.

- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/data/dataset.py:1393-1397` (`_build_rebuilt_features`), `nirs4all/data/dataset.py:1514-1522` (`_rebuild_targets_from_indices`)
- Category: correctness (state loss)
- Description: the rebuilt `Features` use fresh `FeatureSource()` objects (no headers, default unit `cm-1`); the rebuilt `Targets` are created from the already-encoded `numeric` view, so `raw` becomes the encoded floats and the label encoder/class names are lost.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `dt1/r16.py`: before `headers ['1000','1010','1020'] nm`; after `reshape_reps_to_preprocessings`: `headers None, unit cm-1`, `wavelengths_nm() -> ValueError: No headers available`. `dt1/r17.py`: string labels `['wheat','corn',...]`; after the reshape `raw = [3. 0. 2. 1.]` and `transform_predictions([0,1,2], 'numeric','raw')` returns `[0. 1. 2.]` instead of class names.
- Impact: after a repetition reshape, wavelength-aware operators/plots lose their axis (and the unit silently flips to cm-1); classification predictions can no longer be mapped back to the original labels.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): carry headers/unit (per source, replicated across new sources) into the rebuilt `FeatureSource`s and rebuild targets from `raw` + the existing numeric transformer (copy the processing chain).

##### DT1-15 — `DataSelector.tag_filters` is silently ignored by `dataset.x/y/metadata*` (only honoured by `Indexer.x_indices` called directly)

No production caller of `with_tag_filter` exists today, and the test (`tests/integration/pipeline/test_tag_workflow.py:112-119`) only checks the attribute.
- Impact: tag-filtered selectors work on the indexer but are a silent no-op on the public dataset API (X/y not filtered).

##### DT1-18 — Replacing only part of the processings with a different feature count silently truncates/zero-fills the others and drops headers

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: UpdateStrategy only checks widths among replacements, not coverage of all existing processings; FeatureSource resizes all blocks and clears headers. Existing production controllers normally replace all blocks; public partial replacement is the relevant trigger.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/data/_features/update_strategy.py:106-109` (`should_resize_features`), `nirs4all/data/_features/array_storage.py:470-478` (`resize_features`), `nirs4all/data/_features/feature_source.py:243-245`
- Category: correctness
- Description: the docstring says "True if all processings are being replaced with same new dimension" but the condition only checks that all *replacements* share one new width; unreplaced processings are resized too (truncated to the first `new_num_features` columns, or zero-extended) and headers are cleared.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`dt1/r18.py`): processings `raw`,`scaled` (10 features each); `replace_features(["raw"], [X[:, :6]], ["raw_crop"])` -> 3D shape `(6, 2, 6)`; the untouched `scaled` block is now the first 6 columns of the old one.
- Impact: partial replacement with a different width (crop/resample/feature-selection on a subset of processings, e.g. when `context.selector.processing` is a subset) corrupts the other processings without error. Current controllers replace all processings of the source, so reachability is narrow.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): only resize when the replacements cover every processing, otherwise raise.

##### DT1-19 — `FeatureAccessor.add_samples` is not atomic: a failed feature append leaves phantom indexer rows

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Failed2-row append with5features to6x4dataset leaves8indexer rows and6feature rows; later X access raises. Indexer mutation occurs before feature validation/append.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/_dataset/feature_accessor.py:188-190`
- Category: error-handling
- Description: the indexer is extended (`add_samples_dict`) before `self._block.add_samples(...)` runs; any exception from the features block (feature-count mismatch, source-count mismatch, adding after preprocessing) leaves the indexer with ids that have no feature rows.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`dt1/r10.py`): after a rejected `add_samples(rand(3,12))` onto an 8-feature dataset: `feature rows: 5 | indexer rows: 8`, and `ds.x({"partition":"train"})` -> `IndexError: index 5 is out of bounds for axis 0 with size 5`.
- Impact: a caught load error leaves the dataset unusable; mainly relevant to retry/diagnostic code paths.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): add to the features block first (or roll the indexer back on exception).

##### DT1-20 — `Features.update_features` / `add_features` crash on a bare ndarray despite documenting it

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**

##### DT1-21 — `add_samples_batch` with mixed partitions crashes and `augment_rows` assigns the first sample's partition to all augmented rows

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Mixed-partition batch passes a list into categorical partition Series construction; augment_rows assigns partitions[0] to all origins. Production augmentation uses train-only; stored label versus origin-based filtering is an important distinction.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/data/_dataset/feature_accessor.py:248-259` (`FeatureAccessor.add_samples_batch`), `nirs4all/data/indexer.py:962-969` (`Indexer.augment_rows`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: when the index dicts contain several partitions the accessor passes a list as `partition` ("Will be handled by indexer"), but `Indexer._append` builds `pl.Series([partition] * count, dtype=Categorical)` and fails. `augment_rows` documents that augmented rows inherit the partition of their origin but uses `partitions[0]` for the whole batch.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `dt1/r3.py`: `add_samples_batch(..., [train, test, train, train])` -> `TypeError: unexpected value while building Series of type String; found value of type List(String)`. `dt1/r9.py`: `augment_rows([0..4],...)` over 3 train + 2 test samples labels the augmented copies of the two test samples (origins 3, 4) as `partition='train'`; `dataset.augment_samples(..., selector=None)` shows the same. Two-phase `x_indices` masks it for selections (origin decides), so the effect is on the stored label.
- Impact: latent; production augmentation controllers only insert `partition="train"`. Public APIs mislabel/crash for mixed input.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): pass per-row partitions through `_append` (list support) and use each origin's partition in `augment_rows`.

##### DT1-22 — `DataCache(max_entries=0)` hangs forever; `clear()` bypasses `on_evict`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: DataCache(max_entries=0).set() was killed by timeout after 3 seconds (exit 124). The entry-count loop does not terminate when the cache is empty. clear() bypassing on_evict remains confirmed by source inspection. Zero is not documented as a disable switch, so the valid claim is missing boundary validation and a hang.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/performance/cache.py:290-291` (`DataCache._evict_if_needed`), `:253-257` (`DataCache.clear`)
- Category: other (hang)
- Description: `while len(self._cache) >= self.max_entries: self._evict_one()` never terminates for `max_entries <= 0` because `_evict_one` returns immediately on an empty cache. Separately `clear()` drops entries without calling `_on_evict`, so the SharedBlocks CoW references that `StepCache` releases on eviction are never released.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `DataCache(max_entries=0).set("a", np.zeros(3))` -> killed by `timeout 20` (exit 124). Reachable via `CacheConfig(step_cache_enabled=True, step_cache_max_entries=0)` (`pipeline/execution/orchestrator.py:260-263`).
- Impact: setting the entry limit to 0 (a natural "disable") freezes the run; `clear()` leaks CoW references (extra copies later).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): guard `while self._cache and len(self._cache) >= self.max_entries` (and skip caching when `max_entries <= 0`); iterate `_remove` in `clear()`.
