#### Loaders, parsers, schema, config, signal type (DT2)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 21 original DT2 claims were reviewed across normalization/loading routes. Existing relation/repetition_spec joins are implemented and excluded from the overbroad DT2-14 claim. DT2-22 is a new categorical train/test encoding defect. Unlabeled test targets are grouped under DT1-06.

##### DT2-01 — Any load failure is swallowed when the file path contains `train_x` / `test_x`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Missing file named train_x_broken.csv is swallowed; DatasetConfigs returns0train/2testrows. Catch matches text containing train_x/test_x rather than only absence of a configured partition.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/config.py:671` and `:680` (`DatasetConfigs._load_dataset`)
- Category: error-handling
- Description: `_load_dataset` tries to detect "this partition is simply not configured" by substring-matching the exception text (`"train_x" in str(e)` / `"test_x" in str(e)`). The messages raised by `load_XY` embed the failing file path (`"Error loading X data from <path>: ..."`), so any real loading error (NaN under the default abort policy, parse error, row-count mismatch, bad params) for a file whose path contains `train_x` / `test_x` (e.g. the very natural `data/train_x.csv`) is silently converted into "no data for this partition".
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```python
  670  except (ValueError, FileNotFoundError) as e:
  671      if "x_path is None" in str(e) or "train_x" in str(e):
  672          x_train, ... = None, None, None, None, None, None, None
  ```
  Repro (`r1.py`, `r34.py`): `train_x.csv` with one NaN cell, default `na_policy` -> `DatasetConfigs(cfg).get_dataset_at(0)` returns a dataset with **0 train samples** and 10 test samples; the control `Xcal.csv` with the same NaN correctly raises `ValueError: Error loading X data ...`. With the NaN in `test_x.csv` instead, `nirs4all.run(...)` completes ("RUN OK") with `train 30 / test 0` and only a `No test set available for refit evaluation` notice; with the NaN in `train_x.csv`, `run` dies much later with the unrelated `Found input variables with inconsistent numbers of samples: [0, 10]`.
- Impact: a corrupted/NaN-containing/misparsed train or test file is silently dropped; the user gets either a run without test scoring (silent) or a cryptic downstream error that hides the real cause.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): raise a dedicated "partition not configured" signal (e.g. check `config.get(f"{t_set}_x") is None` before calling `handle_data`) instead of matching message text; never swallow errors that carry a path.

##### DT2-02 — `na_policy="remove_sample"` drops rows from X and Y independently: X/Y silently mis-paired (or hard error)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: X drops its row1andYits row2; equal3-row lengths pass but Xrow[5,6] getsY20ratherthan30. Source masks/indexes never reconcile frames.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/loaders/loader.py:211,224-226` (`load_XY`), `nirs4all/data/loaders/base.py:88-92` (`apply_na_policy`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: X, Y and metadata are loaded by independent loader calls, each applying `remove_sample` to its own file. `load_XY` never uses the returned `na_mask` / removed indices to align the frames; it only compares row counts and then pairs `x_df.values` with `y_df.values` positionally. If X and Y each lose the same number of rows (different rows), they are silently shifted against each other; if the counts differ, the load fails with `Row count mismatch`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```python
  211  y_df, y_report, y_na_mask, _, _ = _load_file_with_registry(y_path, **y_params_copy)
  ...
  225  if not y_df.empty and x_df.shape[0] != y_df.shape[0]:   # only a count check
  ```
  Repro (`r2.py`): X rows i=0..9 (first feature 3*i), y = 100*i; NaN in X row 2 and in y row 6, `global_params={"na_policy": "remove_sample"}` -> 9 samples, and X sample 3 is paired with y=200 (expected 300), X sample 4 with 300, ... X sample 6 with 500 (expected 600). `r32.py`: NaN only in X + a Y file -> `ValueError: Row count mismatch: X(9) Y(10)` (so `remove_sample` can never be used together with a Y/metadata file unless NaNs sit in identical rows).
- Impact: with `remove_sample` and NaNs in different rows of the spectra and target files, models are trained on wrongly paired spectrum/target rows with no warning (silent wrong results).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): align on the original row index: after loading, intersect `x_df.index`, `y_df.index`, `m_df.index` (all kept as the original RangeIndex) and subset all frames to the common index; or apply the NA policy once on the joined X/Y.

##### DT2-03 — Some dataset config syntaxes discard repetition, aggregation and other top-level semantics

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Files parser fresh normalisation preserves name but drops repetition/aggregate/method/signal_type/folds. Name/task_type preservation varies by syntax; do not state every listed key is dropped by every parser. Source/variation legacy conversion preserves newer relation fields separately.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/data/parsers/normalizer.py:296-301`; `nirs4all/data/parsers/folder_parser.py:180-203`; `nirs4all/data/parsers/files_parser.py:160-192` (FilesParser), `:475-494` (SourcesParser), `:783-810` (VariationsParser)
- Category: correctness (data-leakage)
- Description: Files parser fresh normalisation preserves name but drops repetition/aggregate/method/signal_type/folds. Name/task_type preservation varies by syntax; do not state every listed key is dropped by every parser. Source/variation legacy conversion preserves newer relation fields separately.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r26.py`, same extra keys `repetition="sid", aggregate_method="median", aggregate_exclude_outliers=True, task_type="regression"` in every form):
  ```
  legacy train_x  rep=['sid'] agg_method=['median'] excl=[True]  task=['regression']
  folder          rep=[None]  agg_method=[None]     excl=[False] task=['auto']
  files           rep=[None]  agg_method=[None]     excl=[False] task=[regression]
  sources         rep=[None]  agg_method=[None]     excl=[False] task=['regression']
  variations      rep=[None]  agg_method=[None]     excl=[False] task=['regression']
  ```

- Impact: Dropped repetition/aggregation/fold semantics can change experimental-unit grouping; exact lost keys depend on the chosen config syntax.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): pass through every non-parsed top-level key (`{**input_data minus consumed keys}`) into the produced config, or at least copy `repetition`, `aggregate*`, `signal_type`, `folds`; warn on dropped keys.

##### DT2-04 — `global_params` / `train_params` containing `signal_type` crash whenever a Y or metadata CSV is loaded

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: global_params signal_type causes CSV Y loading to forward unknown keyword to pandas.read_csv. Train_params follows same merge path by reading; X removes it separately.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/loaders/csv_loader_new.py:372-375` (`CSVLoader.load`), `nirs4all/data/loaders/loader.py:169,205-211` (`load_XY`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: `load_XY` pops `signal_type` from the X params only. The Y and metadata calls receive the merged `global_params` / `{set}_params` unchanged; `CSVLoader.load` forwards every unknown param to `pd.read_csv`, which raises `unexpected keyword argument 'signal_type'`. (`ParquetLoader` explicitly drops it at `parquet_loader.py:172`, `ExcelLoader` pops it, `CSVLoader` does not.)
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r15.py`, `r28.py`): with the repo's own sample data `examples/sample_data/regression/{Xcal,Ycal,Xval,Yval}.csv.gz`:
  `DatasetConfigs({"train_x":..., "train_y":..., "test_x":..., "test_y":..., "global_params": {"header_unit":"cm-1","signal_type":"absorbance","delimiter":";"}})` ->
  `ValueError: ... Could not parse CSV. Python engine error: read_csv() got an unexpected keyword argument 'signal_type' | C engine error: ...`; removing only `signal_type` loads 189 samples. `train_params: {signal_type: ...}` fails the same way. The shipped `examples/configs/basic_dataset.yaml` uses exactly this shape (`global_params: {header_unit, signal_type}` + `train_y`/`train_group`), and the `DatasetConfigs` docstring documents `signal_type` under `global_params`.
- Impact: the documented way to set a signal type for all files of a dataset makes every dataset that has a target/metadata CSV unloadable.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): strip loader-framework keys (`signal_type`, `header_unit` where not applicable) for Y/metadata in `load_XY`, or drop `signal_type` in `CSVLoader.load` like the Parquet/Excel loaders do.

##### DT2-05 — A missing class label in a categorical Y CSV bypasses the NA policy and becomes an extra class (-1)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Pandas3.0.5 categorical Y ['wheat','barley',NA,'wheat'] becomes[0,1,-1,0] under na_policy=abort with na_detectedFalse. Pandas2 may encode stringnan as positive category; extra-class result is the stable claim.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/loaders/csv_loader_new.py:425-427` (`CSVLoader.load`, `data_type="y"`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: categorical Y columns are factorised *before* `apply_na_policy` runs. `pd.factorize` turns a missing entry (`NA`/empty) into code `-1` (pandas 3; in pandas 2 `astype(str)` first turns it into the literal category `"nan"`). Afterwards the column holds integers, `data.isna()` is False everywhere, and neither the default `abort` nor `remove_sample` ever sees the missing label.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r13.py`, `r14.py`):
  ```python
  425  codes, categories = pd.factorize(original_col_series.astype(str))
  426  data[col] = codes
  ```
  Y file `label\nwheat\nbarley\nwheat\nbarley\nNA\nwheat` -> loader returns `[0 1 0 1 -1 0]`, `na_handling.na_detected=False`, no error; `DatasetConfigs(...)` then reports `task: multiclass_classification` (3 classes: the real two plus the bogus one), whereas the same file with a numeric target and one `NA` correctly aborts.
- Impact: unlabeled samples are silently trained on as a third class, and a binary problem becomes multiclass (wrong task type, metrics and models).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): compute the NA mask on the raw column before factorising (or run `apply_na_policy` first / `pd.factorize(..., use_na_sentinel=False)` + restore NaN).

##### DT2-06 — Per-source loading params are shifted onto the wrong sources when only some sources declare `params`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Sources legacy conversion appends params only for sources with params and no placeholders; path/parameter positions shift. Parser applies global defaults to every source when present, so trigger requires partial per-source params and no global params.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/data/schema/config.py:1804-1808` and `:1821-1824` (`DatasetConfigSchema.to_legacy_format`); same pattern in `variations_to_legacy_format` `:1678-1701`
- Category: correctness
- Description: `train_x_params` is built by appending one entry per train path *only for sources that have `params`*. The loader (`handle_data`, `loader.py:518-520`) indexes this list by source position, so the list is misaligned as soon as one source has no `params` (with exactly one entry it also collapses to a dict applied to every source).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r27.py`): sources A (no params), B (`cm-1`/absorbance), C (`nm`/reflectance) -> `train_x_params` has 2 entries; resulting dataset: A unit `cm-1`, signal `absorbance`; B unit `nm`, signal `reflectance` (B holds 4000..4020 cm-1 headers); C unit `cm-1`, signal `unknown`.
- Impact: wrong header units / signal types silently attached to sources -> wrong wavelength conversion, wrong signal-type-dependent behaviour.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): append `source.params.model_dump(...) if source.params else {}` for every path so the list stays index-aligned.

##### DT2-07 — Source/variation partition inference substring-matches the whole path (`'cal'` in `local`, `'val'`, ...), putting test files into train

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Source/variation path inference tests full lower_path for train/cal first; directory local is enough to misclassify. Explicit partition fields prevent this path.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/data/schema/config.py:719,728-729,748,757-758` (`SourceConfig.get_train_paths/get_test_paths`), `:1026,1035-1036,1055,1064-1065` (`VariationConfig`)
- Category: data-leakage
- Description: for `files` given as plain strings (or dicts without `partition`) the partition is inferred with `any(p in lower_path for p in ('train', 'cal'))` / `('test', 'val')` on the *entire path string*, directory names included. `'cal'` matches `local`, `physical`, `chemical`, `scaled`, `calibrations`...; `'val'` matches `eval`, `interval`, `values`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r3.py`, `r3b.py`):
  ```
  SourceConfig(files=["/home/jdoe/local_data/NIR_train.csv", "/home/jdoe/local_data/NIR_test.csv"])
  get_train_paths() -> ['.../NIR_train.csv', '.../NIR_test.csv']   # test file also in train
  get_test_paths()  -> ['.../NIR_test.csv']
  ```
  Full `DatasetConfigs` on such a config fails with `Multi-source feature blocks have different row counts [8, 3]` (the test file became a second train source); with equal row counts it would silently load the test spectra as an additional training source.
- Impact: crash with a misleading error, or test data used as training features.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): infer from the file *stem* with token/word-boundary matching (as `FolderParser._pattern_matches` does), and refuse ambiguous results instead of listing a file in both partitions.

##### DT2-08 — `FilesParser._resolve_partition_from_path` uses substring matching; train wins on any `cal`/`train` substring

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: FilesParser uses Path.stem, not full path, but substring cal/train still matches names like local_test. Train patterns win over test patterns; explicit partition avoids it.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/data/parsers/files_parser.py:318-333` (`FilesParser._resolve_partition_from_path`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: the stem is tested with `pattern in path_lower` (train patterns first, then test, then predict). Any stem containing `cal` (`chemical`, `physical`, `optical`, `vertical`, `local`, `scaled`, `typical`) is classified train even if it also says `test`/`val`; `values`/`eval` -> test; `renewal`/`newton` -> predict.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r3.py`): `data/chemical_test.csv -> train`, `spectra_physical_test.csv -> train`, `local_val.csv -> train`, `test_vertical.csv -> train`, `scaled_val.csv -> train`, `X_values.csv -> test`, `renewal.csv -> predict`.
- Impact: hold-out files of a `files:` config are assigned to the training partition (leakage into training / no test set).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): tokenise the stem (`re.split(r"[^a-z0-9]+")`) and match whole tokens; do not let `train` silently win over an explicit `test` token.

##### DT2-09 — `na_fill_config` given as a dict (the only form JSON/YAML can express) crashes the loader

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: apply_na_policy(replace) dereferences na_fill_config.method directly without converting dict. Direct CSV loader kwargs can supply dict; JSON/YAML-schema paths may normalise specific typed fields, so scope should name loader entry.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/data/loaders/base.py:112-119` (`apply_na_policy`); entry via `nirs4all/data/loaders/loader.py:42-45`
- Category: api-contract
- Description: `apply_na_policy` expects a `NAFillConfig` object (`na_fill_config.method`), but dataset configs carry plain dicts, and even the pydantic parsers return plain dicts (`model_dump`). Nothing converts the dict to `NAFillConfig`, so `na_policy: replace` can only be used with the default fill (0.0).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r7.py`): `global_params={"na_policy":"replace"}` works (NaN -> 0.0); `{"na_policy":"replace","na_fill_config":{"method":"mean"}}` -> `ValueError: ... Unexpected error in CSVLoader: 'dict' object has no attribute 'method'`.
- Impact: YAML/JSON dataset configs cannot select mean/median/ffill imputation; the failure message points at the loader internals.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): in `apply_na_policy` (or `handle_data`) coerce `dict` -> `NAFillConfig(**dict)`.

##### DT2-10 — Default pandas NA conventions can erase literal metadata categories

- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/data/loaders/csv_loader_new.py:365-366` (`CSVLoader.load`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: keep_default_na=True converts metadata literalsNone/NA/null/etc to missing. Some are legitimate NA conventions, so describe configurable category-loss/round-trip problem rather than declare every default NA token interpretation intrinsically wrong.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r21.py`): metadata file `treatment;country / None;NA / Low;DE / ...` -> `treatment = [None, 'Low', None, 'High', 'Low', 'High']`, `country = [None, 'DE', None, 'DE', None, 'DE']` (all `None`/`NA` entries lost; metadata uses `na_policy="ignore"` so no error either).
- Impact: Literal metadata categories that coincide with pandas NA tokens may lose their category identity under the default policy.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Provide explicit metadata-category/NA-token controls and document how literal strings round-trip.

##### DT2-15 — Decimal-comma headers are not normalised: wavelengths unusable with `decimal_separator=","`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**

- Category: correctness
- Description: `decimal_separator` is applied to data cells only; header cells such as `4000,5` stay literal strings, and the numeric-header regex accepts only `.` decimals.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r8.py`): ';'-delimited file with header `4000,5;4008,5;...` and `global_params={"decimal_separator": ",", "header_unit": "cm-1"}` -> data parsed correctly (`X[0]=[0, 0.1, 0.2]`), `headers == ['4000,5', '4008,5', '4016,5']`, and `ds.wavelengths_cm1(0)` raises `ValueError: Headers are not numeric ... cannot convert to wavelengths`.
- Impact: any wavelength-aware operator/chart fails for European-locale exports even though the user declared the decimal separator.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): when `header_unit` is spectral, normalise header text with the configured decimal separator (`h.replace(decimal_sep, ".")`) before storing.

##### DT2-16 — `DatasetConfigs.get_dataset(config, name)` resolves per-dataset settings by *name*: datasets sharing a name get the first one's settings

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: get_dataset(config,name) chooses first matching name, not config identity. get_dataset_at/iter_datasets avoid this; orchestrator still calls name-based method.

- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed

- Category: correctness
- Description: the index used to fetch `task_type`, `signal_type`, `aggregate*`, `repetition` is found with `cfg_name == name`. Names are derived from the folder leaf (`_extract_name_from_path`), so `a/data` and `b/data` both become `data`; the second config is then loaded with the first config's overrides (the orchestrator calls `get_dataset(config, name)` for refit and for parallel runs).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`r19.py`): `DatasetConfigs([".../a/data", ".../b/data"], task_type=["regression","binary_classification"])`; for the 2nd config `get_dataset(cfg_b, 'data').task_type == 'regression'` while `get_dataset_at(1).task_type == 'binary_classification'`.
- Impact: refit/parallel passes of the second same-named dataset use the wrong forced task type / aggregation / repetition (wrong metrics or wrong grouping).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): look up by config identity (`cfg is config`) or have callers pass the index (`get_dataset_at`); also deduplicate names.

A bare data-file path (documented in `docs/source/user_guide/data/loading_data.md`: `DatasetConfigs("spectra.csv", signal_type=...)`) is also "invalid" because only folders/JSON/YAML are accepted.

Available datasets: 0 to -1.`; `DatasetConfigs("spectra.csv")` -> `len(configs) == 0`.

##### DT2-21 — `ToAbsorbance(clip_negative=...)` has no effect

- Re-audit verdict: **DUPLICATE — canonical item below**
- Re-audit result: Canonical: `OPO-16`. np.clip(X,epsilon,None) and np.maximum(X,epsilon) are equivalent for finite X; clip_negative switch changes no result.
- Fix disposition: Track and fix only under OPO-16; retain this location/reference.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: underlying behavior reviewed; not an independent finding
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/transforms/signal_conversion.py:129` (`ToAbsorbance.transform`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: both branches compute the same thing: `np.clip(X, eps, None)` is identical to `np.maximum(X, eps)`, so the documented `clip_negative` switch never changes the output (non-positive reflectances are always replaced by `epsilon`, giving A = 10).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```python
  129  X = np.clip(X, self.epsilon, None) if self.clip_negative else np.maximum(X, self.epsilon)
  ```

- Impact: callers cannot get NaN/raising behaviour for non-physical (<=0) reflectance; values silently become A=10.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): drop the parameter or implement the `False` branch (e.g. leave non-positive values to produce NaN / raise).

##### DT2-22 — Categorical target CSVs are encoded independently per train/test file, silently swapping classes

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: Train labels [wheat, barley, wheat] become [0, 1, 0]; test labels [barley, wheat] become [0, 1], whereas consistent class IDs should be [1, 0]. load_XY takes only y_df.values and drops each file categorical mapping. This alters classification test scores and loses original class names.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/data/loaders/csv_loader_new.py:425`
- Category: data-integrity
- Description: Train labels [wheat, barley, wheat] become [0, 1, 0]; test labels [barley, wheat] become [0, 1], whereas consistent class IDs should be [1, 0]. load_XY takes only y_df.values and drops each file categorical mapping. This alters classification test scores and loses original class names.
- Evidence: Trigger: Train labels wheat/barley/wheat; test labels barley/wheat; DatasetConfigs(train_x/train_y/test_x/test_y) default categorical_mode=auto

```python
from pathlib import Path
from tempfile import TemporaryDirectory
from nirs4all.data import DatasetConfigs

with TemporaryDirectory() as folder:
    p = Path(folder)
    contents = {
        "X.csv": "1000;1100\n1;2\n3;4\n5;6\n",
        "XT.csv": "1000;1100\n7;8\n9;10\n",
        "Y.csv": "label\nwheat\nbarley\nwheat\n",
        "YT.csv": "label\nbarley\nwheat\n",
    }
    for name, text in contents.items():
        (p / name).write_text(text)
    ds = DatasetConfigs({
        "name": "classes", "train_x": str(p/"X.csv"),
        "train_y": str(p/"Y.csv"), "test_x": str(p/"XT.csv"),
        "test_y": str(p/"YT.csv"),
        "global_params": {"delimiter": ";", "has_header": True},
    }).get_dataset_at(0)
    print(ds.y({"partition": "train"}, "raw").ravel())  # [0 1 0]
    print(ds.y({"partition": "test"}, "raw").ravel())   # [0 1], expected [1 0]
```

- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Keep raw class labels through file loading or fit one train encoder and apply it consistently to all other partitions.

### 5.6 Operators — `nirs4all/operators/`

#### Transforms, augmentation, filters, splitters (OPO)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 21 OPO claims were checked using focused fixtures or source review. EMSC, EPO, sample-axis differentiation, feature selection, augmentation and split behavior were probed. Statistical defaults and singleton-stratum limitations are qualified, not declared intrinsically incorrect.

##### OPO-01 — ExtendedMultiplicativeScatterCorrection (EMSC) with default `scale=True` outputs ~1e20 garbage

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Default EMSC reference max2.1e-15 and output6.8e22 on raw mean5 data; absent constant design term additionally prevents proper additive correction. Scale of blow-up is data-dependent, not universally1e20. The separate copied implementation in AOM-02 has the same defect and must be included in the same repair.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/transforms/nirs.py:418` (`ExtendedMultiplicativeScatterCorrection.partial_fit`), `nirs4all/operators/transforms/nirs.py:425`, `nirs4all/operators/transforms/nirs.py:445-455` (`transform`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: With the default `scale=True`, `partial_fit` column-centres X and then sets `reference_ = mean(centred X, axis=0)`, which is identically ~0 (1e-16). `transform` then regresses each spectrum on `[reference_, wl, wl^2]`, so `coeffs[0]` (the multiplicative term) is numerically arbitrary and the spectrum is divided by it. The same bug was already diagnosed and fixed for `MultiplicativeScatterCorrection` (see the comment at `nirs.py:289-300`) but not for EMSC. Even with `scale=False` the design matrix has no constant (additive) term, so an additive offset is not removed.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
reference_ max abs: 9.66e-16
EMSC().transform(X)[0,:3] -> [1.56e+21 1.53e+21 1.49e+21]      (default scale=True)
X = a + b*ref (mean(b)=1, mean(a)=0):  MSC rmse vs ref = 6.7e-16 ;  EMSC(scale=False) rmse vs ref = 0.035 (ref std 0.71)
```
```python
tmp_x = scaler.transform(tmp_x)               # nirs.py:422 (centred)
self.reference_ = np.mean(tmp_x, axis=0)      # nirs.py:425 -> ~0
design_matrix = np.column_stack([self.reference_, *[self.wavelengths_ ** d ...]])   # nirs.py:445
X_transformed[i] = (X_transformed[i] - polynomial_part) / coeffs[0]                 # nirs.py:455
```

- Impact: any pipeline using `EMSC()` / `ExtendedMultiplicativeScatterCorrection()` with defaults (documented in preprocessing guides and generator examples) feeds ~1e20 values (or inf/NaN on some data) to the model. No test covers EMSC.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): do not centre before computing the reference (use the raw mean spectrum), add a constant column to the design matrix, and use the real wavelengths (or a normalised axis) instead of integer indices.

##### OPO-02 — Derivate uses the documented sample axis, conflicting with spectral-derivative use

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Slope 2 across wavelengths gives zeros; one-sample transform raises. axis0 is explicitly documented for this class, so its spectral use/preset semantics are wrong, while describing all sample-axis gradients as leakage is too broad (within-batch dependence does not prove train/test mixing).
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/transforms/scalers.py:541` (`Derivate.transform`), `nirs4all/operators/transforms/scalers.py:567` (`derivate`); used by `nirs4all/operators/transforms/presets.py:26-27,36-38,47-48,87-89,121-123,145-147,171-173`
- Category: correctness
- Description: Slope 2 across wavelengths gives zeros; one-sample transform raises. axis0 is explicitly documented for this class, so its spectral use/preset semantics are wrong, while describing all sample-axis gradients as leakage is too broad (within-batch dependence does not prove train/test mixing).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
Derivate on data with slope 2 along features -> [0. 0. 0. 0.]      FirstDerivative -> [2. 2. 2. 2.]
row 4 of a 50-sample batch vs same sample in a 5-sample batch: differ by 10.59
Derivate().transform(X[:1]) -> ValueError: Shape of array too small to calculate a numerical gradient
```

- Impact: Using this sample-axis operator as a wavelength derivative produces inappropriate spectral features and fails on a single sample; the sample-axis contract is itself documented.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Clarify the sample-axis contract and remove spectral-derivative ambiguity in presets/docs; choose any axis behavior change explicitly.

##### OPO-03 — `EPO.transform` is the identity, `fit_transform` is not (EPO never corrects held-out data)

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Default scale=True transform is identity up to2e-16; fit_transform changes spectra by 1.87. scale=False transform is also identity. Learn/apply mismatch and legacy no-op confirmed. The assertion that true EPO needs d at prediction time in source is false; spectral projection should be reusable.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/transforms/orthogonalization.py:521-597` (`EPO.transform`), `nirs4all/operators/transforms/orthogonalization.py:599-640` (`EPO.fit_transform`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Default scale=True transform is identity up to2e-16; fit_transform changes spectra by 1.87. scale=False transform is also identity. Learn/apply mismatch and legacy no-op confirmed. The assertion that true EPO needs d at prediction time in source is false; spectral projection should be reusable.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
EPO().fit(X_train, temp).transform(X_test) is X_test: True
corr(temp, X_test[:,0]): -0.861 raw -> -0.861 after transform
corr(temp, X_train[:,0]): -0.822 raw -> -5e-16 after fit_transform
```

- Impact: The reusable transform omits the learned correction, including in the legacy fit-then-transform path; held-out spectra are uncorrected.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): implement the real EPO projection `P = I - V(V'V)^-1 V'` (V = dominant directions of the d-induced variation, e.g. rows of `projection_coefs_`) in `transform` and make `fit_transform` use the same operator.

LocalMixup uses the fitted full train set, so it need not be an exact copy in that scope; its problem is unmixed labels. Direct X-only transform is intentional, while sample_augmentation integration is incorrect.

##### OPO-05 — Wavelength-axis augmenters silently break (or crash) when wavelengths are descending

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Ascending versus reversed-axis result differs by.994 for wavelength shift and1 for stretch. np.interp receives descending xp. Environmental/broadening source has same ordering assumption; only shift/stretch independently replayed.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/augmentation/spectral.py:259-274` (`WavelengthShift`), `:307-322` (`WavelengthStretch`); `nirs4all/operators/augmentation/environmental.py:250,289,488` (`TemperatureAugmenter`, `MoistureAugmenter`); `nirs4all/operators/augmentation/synthesis.py:216-238` (`InstrumentalBroadeningAugmenter`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: These call `np.interp(query, wavelengths, spectrum)` which requires increasing `xp`; with a descending axis it returns nonsense without error. `_ordered_warp_inputs` (`spectral.py:34`) was added for `LocalWavelengthWarp`/`SmoothMagnitudeWarp` but not applied to the other augmenters. `InstrumentalBroadeningAugmenter` computes `wl_step = median(diff(wl))` (negative for a descending axis) so `gaussian_filter1d` gets a negative sigma and crashes. Wavelengths come straight from the dataset headers (`controllers/transforms/transformer.py:140-180`), whose order is not normalised.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
WavelengthShift(shift=1nm), gaussian peak, ascending axis : max|out-ref| = 0.0   ; descending axis: 0.999 (peak destroyed)
WavelengthStretch(1.01) descending: output max 3.7e-44 (peak 0.999)
TemperatureAugmenter(10) asc-vs-desc max diff 0.94 ; MoistureAugmenter(0.3) 0.99
InstrumentalBroadeningAugmenter(fwhm=8) on descending wavelengths: ValueError: Radius must be a nonnegative integer.
```

- Impact: augmenting a dataset whose headers are descending (e.g. ascending-cm⁻¹ files converted to nm) produces flat/garbage spectra as training samples.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): reuse `_ordered_warp_inputs` (sort, interpolate, restore order) in all `np.interp`-based augmenters; use `abs(median(diff(wl)))` for the step.

##### OPO-06 — `KMeansSplitter` always puts sample 0 in train and can drop samples from both train and test

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Seed0,n60,test_size.3 returns42train+17test and loses index59; sample0 appears in all10 train sets. Zero-filled surplus entries and truncation after complement explain the bias/drop.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/splitters/splitters.py:474-487` (`KMeansSplitter.split`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `index_train = np.zeros(n_samples, dtype=int)` is only filled for the first `n_train` entries; the remaining zeros are kept by `np.unique`, so index 0 is always added to the training set even if no centroid selects it. The set then has `n_train+1` members and is truncated with `index_train[:n_train]` (sorted, so the highest index is silently removed) after `index_test` was already computed from the untruncated train set; the removed sample is in neither set. Test is also truncated (`:487`) when train is short.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
30 random datasets (n=60, test_size=0.3): 6 had a sample in neither train nor test; in the same 6, sample 0 was forced into train although no centroid picked it.
n=100: test_size=0.3 seed 0 -> train 70, test 29 (1 sample lost); test_size=0.5 -> test 49 for all 3 seeds
```

- Impact: biased split (sample 0 always in the calibration set), reduced data, requested sizes not honoured.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): build `index_train` from only the `n_train` picks, handle duplicate picks by choosing the next-nearest unused sample, and derive `index_test` as the exact complement.

- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):

```
SPlitSplitter(test_size=0.3|0.4|0.15).split -> test fraction 0.34 | 0.50 | 0.17
two runs with random_state=42 -> identical test indices: False
```

##### OPO-08 — `CARS.fit` crashes when the target has a small scale (probabilities do not sum to 1)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: CARS succeeds at target std~1 and raises probabilities-not-summing-to1 after scaling y by1e-3 or1e-5. Epsilon in denominator prevents normalization.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/transforms/feature_selection.py:246-258` (`CARS.fit`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: Sampling weights are `coefs / (coefs.sum() + 1e-10)`. NumPy's `Generator.choice(p=...)` requires `p.sum()` to equal 1 within ~1.5e-8; when `sum|coef|` is below about 7e-3 (targets expressed as small fractions) the `1e-10` makes the sum differ by more than the tolerance and `choice` raises.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
CARS(n_components=5, n_sampling_runs=15, random_state=0).fit(X, y)  y ~ std 1.0 -> ok
                                                                    y ~ std 1e-3 -> ValueError: Probabilities do not sum to 1.
                                                                    y ~ std 1e-5 -> same
```

- Impact: CARS wavelength selection fails for targets in g/g fractions or similar small-valued units.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): normalise with `weights = coefs / coefs.sum()` (fall back to uniform if the sum is 0).

##### OPO-09 — `EdgeArtifactsAugmenter` builds its sub-augmenters in `__init__`: `set_params` is stale and per-sample seeding is ineffective

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Wavelength-aware replay: clone plus random_state0/1/2 produces identical artifacts (maxdiff0); overall_strength2 leaves child curvature_strength=0.02 and disabling curvature leaves cached child present. Constructor caches children and inherited set_params changes only outer attributes, so controller per-sample reseeding is ineffective.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/augmentation/edge_artifacts.py:819-849` (`__init__`, `_init_augmenters`); consumer `nirs4all/controllers/data/sample_augmentation.py:795-802` (`_set_operator_random_state`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: Sub-augmenters and their seeds are derived once in `__init__`. `set_params(random_state=...)`, `overall_strength`, `edge_curvature=False`, etc. only change the outer attributes. The sample-augmentation controller (default scope) clones the operator per sample and calls `set_params(random_state=base+i)`; the sub-augmenters keep the seeds derived from the clone's original `random_state`, so with an explicit seed every sample gets the same noise/curvature/truncated-peak realisation.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
clone + set_params(random_state=0|1|2) + transform -> added artifacts identical across the three seeds: True
EdgeArtifactsAugmenter(random_state=1).set_params(overall_strength=2.0)._curvature_aug.curvature_strength -> 0.02 (expected 0.04)
set_params(edge_curvature=False) leaves self._curvature_aug present
```

- Impact: with `random_state` set, all augmented copies share identical artifacts (no diversity); parameter sweeps that rely on `set_params` do nothing.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): build the sub-augmenters lazily inside `_transform_impl` from the current parameters (seeded from `self.random_state`).

##### OPO-12 — `norml()` ignores min-max normalisation for `(0, 1)` and does not subtract the minimum otherwise

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: norml(range0,1) yields .03..16 rather than0..1; range2,4 yields2.476..4.476 because min is not subtracted. Wrong conditional and formula confirmed.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/transforms/scalers.py:484-505` (`norml`); contrast with `Normalize` at `scalers.py:338`
- Category: correctness
- Description: The condition `feature_range[0] != -1 and feature_range[1] != 1` is false for `(0, 1)` (and `(-1, 2)` etc.), so the column-norm branch runs; for the remaining ranges `imin + f*d` omits `- min`, so values do not land in the requested range. `Normalize` uses `or` and subtracts the minimum.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
norml(X, feature_range=(0, 1)) -> min 0.066, max 0.374 (divided by column norm)
```

- Impact: public functional API (`nirs4all.operators.norml`) returns wrong values.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use the `or` condition and `imin + f * (d - min)` as in `Normalize`.

##### OPO-14 — `Normalize` and `SimpleScale` divide by zero for constant/zero-padded columns

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Zero column gives nonfinite values for Normalize defaults, range0,1 and SimpleScale. They divide by zero scales/norms without a guard.

- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/transforms/scalers.py:407,434` (`Normalize`), `nirs4all/operators/transforms/scalers.py:609` (`SimpleScale.transform`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: Per-column scale factors (`max-min` or L2 norm) have no zero guard (unlike `StandardNormalVariate` and sklearn's scalers). A column that is constant across the training rows (e.g. the zeros produced by `Resampler(fill_value=0.0)` outside the original range) yields NaN/inf in all rows.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `SimpleScale().fit_transform(X)` with `X[:,2]=0` -> NaN column; `Normalize()` and `Normalize((0,1))` -> NaN column.
- Impact: NaNs propagate into the model (the transformer controller then warns about introduced NaNs).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): replace zero scales by 1 as sklearn does.

##### OPO-21 — `SystematicCircularSplitter` reseeds Python's global `random` module

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Splitter changes next random.random from.05236 to.94787 despite unrelated seed123. Global rd.seed is the source; process-global RNG side effect confirmed.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/splitters/splitters.py:209-210`, `:218`
- Category: concurrency
- Description: `rd.seed(self.random_state)` mutates the process-wide RNG (and the roll uses `rd.randint`, also global) instead of a local `random.Random`/`default_rng`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): after `random.seed(123)`, running the splitter with `random_state=7` changes the next `random.random()` value (`r0 != r1`).
- Impact: other code relying on `random` gets reset/deterministic state (including in threaded or joblib-in-process runs); runs with `random_state=None` share the global stream.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use `np.random.default_rng(self.random_state).integers(0, n_samples)` locally.

#### sklearn-style PLS family and DL builders (OM1)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 26 OM1 claims were reviewed; sklearn, Torch, JAX and selected TensorFlow builders were exercised. DiPLS produced finite predictions with trendfitter. A suspected factory num_classes bug was disproved: both Torch and JAX received three classes. Real TabPFN weights/network/GPU and every historical TensorFlow architecture were not executed.

##### OM1-01 — 17 estimators are not recognised as regressor/classifier by scikit-learn >= 1.6 (mixin after `BaseEstimator`, `_estimator_type` ignored)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: All17 listed estimators report neither regressor nor classifier under sklearn1.9.1; IKPLS reports regressor. Mixin-after-BaseEstimator MRO confirms why; source meets allowed dependency range.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/simpls.py:390` (`SIMPLS(BaseEstimator, RegressorMixin)`), same pattern in `plsda.py:11`, `oplsda.py:12` (its `_estimator_type` at `oplsda.py:15-16` is even inside the class docstring), `opls.py`, `mbpls.py:418`, `dipls.py`, `sparsepls.py`, `lwpls.py`, `ipls.py:1050`, `robust_pls.py:498`, `recursive_pls.py:448`, `kopls.py:547`, `nlpls.py:632`, `oklmpls.py:670`, `fckpls.py:504`, `pcr.py:20`, `tabpfn_nirs.py:45`. Consumers hit: `operators/models/multimodal.py:599`, `pipeline/dagml/multimodal_contracts.py:98`.
- Category: api-contract
- Description: With sklearn >= 1.6 the estimator type comes from `__sklearn_tags__`, and mixins must precede `BaseEstimator` in the MRO. These classes put `BaseEstimator` first and rely on the old class attribute `_estimator_type = "regressor"/"classifier"` (commented "for StackingRegressor"), which sklearn 1.9 no longer reads. Only `IKPLS` and `AOMPLSAomlibRegressor` (mixin first) are correct. `pyproject.toml` allows `scikit-learn>=1.5`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
from sklearn.base import is_regressor, is_classifier
is_regressor(SIMPLS()) -> False   is_classifier(PLSDA()) -> False   (IKPLS -> True)
StackingRegressor([("s", SIMPLS(n_components=3)), ("r", Ridge())], final_estimator=Ridge()).fit(X, y)
  -> ValueError: The estimator SIMPLS should be a regressor.
MultimodalClassifier({"a": None, "b": None}, PLSDA(n_components=2)).fit([X1, X2], y)
  -> ValueError: MultimodalClassifier requires a sklearn-compatible classifier as model.
check_cv(5, y, classifier=is_classifier(PLSDA())) -> KFold   (not StratifiedKFold)
```

- Impact: nirs4all's own PLS-family regressors cannot be used as estimators of sklearn's `StackingRegressor` (verified) or anywhere else sklearn/nirs4all calls `is_regressor`/`is_classifier`; `PLSDA`/`OPLSDA` cannot be the model of `MultimodalClassifier` (verified); `check_cv`/`cross_val_*` with an integer `cv` picks plain KFold for PLSDA; `generated_prediction_contract` (`multimodal_contracts.py:98`) therefore returns `None` for a source-concat regressor built from these models (read, not run).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): put the mixin first (`class SIMPLS(RegressorMixin, BaseEstimator)`, `ClassifierMixin` for the DA classes) in all listed classes and drop the dead `_estimator_type` attributes.

##### OM1-02 — RobustPLS retains contaminated unweighted means after fitting robust weights

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: With 10 vertical outliers, robust fits retain plain contaminated y mean 50.98 and clean-test biases 1.07/1.30 even after outlier weights drop. Weighted recentering omission is a real limitation, but robustness is reduced rather than completely defeated: weights still improve slope/noise resistance.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/robust_pls.py:706-724` (unweighted mean/std), `:733` (`_compute_irls_weights`), `:115-190` (`_weighted_simpls_fit`, weighted covariances of data centred with unweighted means)
- Category: correctness
- Description: With 10 vertical outliers, robust fits retain plain contaminated y mean 50.98 and clean-test biases 1.07/1.30 even after outlier weights drop. Weighted recentering omission is a real limitation, but robustness is reduced rather than completely defeated: weights still improve slope/noise resistance.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (100 samples, y = sum(X[:, :5]) + noise, first 10 y shifted by +15, 500 clean test rows; model with 5 components):
```
PLS(all)            test bias +1.489  rmse 2.767
RobustPLS huber     test bias +1.468  rmse 1.845   (outlier weights ~0.14)
RobustPLS tukey     test bias +1.384  rmse 1.911   (outlier weights 0.01)
PLS(inliers only)   test bias +0.004  rmse 0.076
```

- Impact: Contaminated unweighted means leave residual target/intercept bias, reducing robustness even though slope/noise resistance still improves.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): inside the IRLS loop and in the final fit, centre (and scale) X and Y with the weighted means (`sum(w x)/sum(w)`), store those as `x_mean_`/`y_mean_`.

##### OM1-03 — Unscaled kernel PLS configurations lose the target intercept; centering contracts differ

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Both centered-kernel models with scaling disabled predict mean ~0 instead of target 49.48, RMSE~49.48. KOPLS docs promise variance scaling only; KernelPLS explicitly documents centering+scaling together, so its parameter behavior is documented but intercept interaction remains a harmful design limitation.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/kopls.py:749-757` (`scale=False` also sets `y_mean_ = 0`); `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/nlpls.py:889-897` (same for `scale_y`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Both centered-kernel models with scaling disabled predict mean ~0 instead of target 49.48, RMSE~49.48. KOPLS docs promise variance scaling only; KernelPLS explicitly documents centering+scaling together, so its parameter behavior is documented but intercept interaction remains a harmful design limitation.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (y = 50 + 2 x0 + x3 + noise, linear kernel):
```
KOPLS scale=True : mean(pred)=49.88 rmse=1.07     KOPLS scale=False : mean(pred)=0.28 rmse=49.49
KernelPLS scale_y=True : rmse=1.31                KernelPLS scale_y=False: mean(pred)=0.24 rmse=49.53
```

- Impact: An uncentered offset can be lost and cause severe prediction bias; the two estimators document their scaling/centering flags differently.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): always centre Y (`y_mean_ = y.mean(0)`), apply only the std division conditionally.

##### OM1-04 — `SparsePLS(scale=...)` is ignored (always standardises X and y)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: SparsePLS scale True/False predictions exactly equal on unequally scaled columns. Both backends standardize unconditionally and fit omits self.scale.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/sparsepls.py:63-74` (NumPy), `:200-211` (JAX), `:534-543` (fit never passes `self.scale`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: Docstring says "scale : Whether to scale X and y before fitting" but neither backend reads `self.scale`; both unconditionally centre and divide by `std(ddof=0)`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `SparsePLS(n_components=3, alpha=0.0, scale=True).fit(X, y).predict(X)` vs `scale=False` -> max abs difference `0.0` on data with columns of std 1..30.
- Impact: `scale=False` (raw-absorbance PLS-like use) silently behaves like `scale=True`; results and variable selection differ from what the user configured.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): honour `scale` (and keep centring) in both `_sparse_pls_fit_*` implementations.

##### OM1-05 — `SparsePLS.get_selected_features()` raises `AttributeError` on the default (numpy) backend

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fitted default SparsePLS.get_selected_features raises missing _model. hasattr accesses undefined self._model before checking its child attr.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/sparsepls.py:632` (`SparsePLS.get_selected_features`, defined at `:614-637`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: The numpy branch evaluates `hasattr(self._model, 'get_selected_feature_names')`, but `self._model` is never assigned anywhere in the class (leftover of a former wrapper around the `sparse-pls` package), so the attribute access itself raises before `hasattr` can answer.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
m = SparsePLS(n_components=3, alpha=5.0).fit(X, y); m.get_selected_features()
AttributeError: 'SparsePLS' object has no attribute '_model'
```

- Impact: the one public method that exposes the variable selection (the reason to use SparsePLS) crashes for every default-configured model; only `backend='jax'` works.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): drop the `_model` branch and use `np.where(np.any(self.coef_ != 0, axis=-1))[0]` for both backends.

##### OM1-06 — `MBPLS.transform` / `SparsePLS.transform` return `X @ W`, not the latent scores

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: MBPLS.transform training-score mismatch1.37; SparsePLS.transform differs from proper rotation by3.80. Both multiply nondeflated X by deflation weights rather than rotation.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/mbpls.py:284-288`, `:290-299`, `:682-718`; `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/sparsepls.py:164-168`, `:590-612`
- Category: correctness
- Description: These are NIPALS models whose weights `W` act on the *deflated* matrix; scores for new data need the rotation `R = W (P'W)^-1` (as `SIMPLS`, `RecursivePLS`, `RobustPLS` do and as sklearn's `x_rotations_`). `transform` documents "Latent variables (scores)" but multiplies by `W` only, so for >= 2 components it differs from the training scores `_T`; in the multiblock case the unscaled super-score normalisation is also lost.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
MBPLS(n_components=3).fit(X, y); np.abs(m.transform(X) - m._T).max()   -> 1.2485   (sklearn: 1.3e-15)
multiblock: np.abs(mm.transform([X1, X2]) - mm._T).max()                -> 3.897
SparsePLS(alpha=0): |abs(transform(X)) - abs(sklearn x_scores_)|.max()  -> 1.39
```

- Impact: anything downstream of `transform` (score plots, T2/Q diagnostics, stacking on latent variables, `x_scores_`-based outlier tests) gets coordinates that are inconsistent with the model.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): store `R = W @ pinv(P.T @ W)` and use `X_c @ R` in `transform` (and for the multiblock path include the block-score normalisation).

##### OM1-07 — `IntervalPLS` JAX backend scores the last (shorter) interval on the wrong columns

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Actual JAX dynamic_slice with p103,start90,maxwidth30 produces columns73..85 after13-widthmask, expected90..102. This is the exact kernel primitive invoked by unequal-interval evaluation, not an end-to-end interval fit replay.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/ipls.py:552-563` (`_eval_single_interval_static`), selected at `:903-914`
- Category: correctness
- Description: Intervals of unequal width are evaluated with `lax.dynamic_slice(X, (0, start_idx), (n, max_interval_width))` and then masked with `arange(max_w) < actual_width`. When `start_idx + max_w > n_features` (always true for the last, shorter interval) `dynamic_slice` clamps the start index, so the window is shifted left and the mask keeps the wrong columns. This path is used whenever `interval_width` is given or `n_features % n_intervals != 0`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (n=100, p=103, signal in the last 8 columns):
```
n_intervals=10 : numpy last-interval R2 0.978   jax 0.760
interval_width=30 : numpy [-0.628 -0.577 -0.271  0.978]   jax [-0.628 -0.575 -0.266 -0.187]
```

- Impact: the JAX backend can rank/select the wrong interval (final interval is scored on features belonging to the previous one), and forward/backward selection reuses the same scoring.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): build the interval with gather indices `start_idx + arange(max_w)` clipped to `n_features - 1` plus the width mask (or pad X with `max_w` zero columns before slicing).

##### OM1-08 — `FractionalConvFeaturizer` cannot be cloned, so it fails as a pipeline step

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: clone(FractionalConvFeaturizer()) raises constructor-modifies-alphas RuntimeError. Pipeline transformer controller clones operators, establishing reachability.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/fckpls.py:314-315` (`self.alphas = list(alphas)`, `self.sigmas = list(sigmas)`); clone sites `nirs4all/controllers/transforms/transformer.py:557,724,1123`
- Category: api-contract
- Description: `__init__` converts the constructor arguments to new lists, violating the sklearn rule that parameters are stored unchanged; `clone()` raises. The transformer controller clones every operator before fitting.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `clone(FractionalConvFeaturizer())` -> `RuntimeError: Cannot clone object FractionalConvFeaturizer(alphas=[0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0], sigmas=[2.0]), as the constructor either does not set or modifies parameter alphas` (`clone(FCKPLS())` is fine).
- Impact: the publicly exported featurizer (`nirs4all.operators.models.FractionalConvFeaturizer`) crashes when used as a preprocessing step (and in `GridSearchCV`/any `clone`).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): store `self.alphas = alphas`, `self.sigmas = sigmas` unchanged and convert inside `fit`/`transform`.

##### OM1-09 — `FCKPLS(backend='jax')` ignores `mode='valid'` and applies correlation instead of convolution

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Same-mode JAX derivative block equals negative numpy block within3e-16; JAX fixed padding emits120features where NumPy valid gives92 for2filters,p60,k15. Source disregards mode and omits convolution kernel reversal.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/sklearn/fckpls.py:446-465` (`convolve_1d_jax`, padding always `(pad, pad)`, no kernel flip) vs `:408` (`fftconvolve(x, h, mode=self.mode)`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: The JAX filter bank always produces `n_features` outputs per kernel (documented `mode='valid'` -> `n_features - kernel_size + 1`) and uses `lax.conv_general_dilated`, which is cross-correlation, whereas the NumPy path is a true convolution. For the antisymmetric (derivative-like, alpha > 0) kernels the JAX features are sign-flipped.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
mode='valid': n_features_out_ numpy 230   jax 300
mode='same' per-alpha blocks: alpha 0.0 identical; alpha 0.5/1.0/1.5/2.0: max|a+d| = 0.0, max|a-d| = 2.9
```

- Impact: the two backends are different models; `mode='valid'` is silently ignored on JAX; `get_fractional_features` and `n_features_out_` disagree with the documentation. Models cannot be moved between backends.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): flip the kernel (`kernel[::-1]`) and honour `mode` (padding `(0, 0)` for `'valid'`) in `convolve_1d_jax`.

##### OM1-10 — TF `decon_Sep_classification` has an inverted class-count condition

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: TensorFlow decon_Sep_classification with3classes has output(None,1), confirming >=2 condition incorrectly selects sigmoid branch.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/tensorflow/nicon.py:686-689`
- Category: correctness
- Description: `if num_classes >= 2: Dense(1, sigmoid) else: Dense(num_classes, softmax)`. Every sibling builder uses `== 2` for the single sigmoid output and softmax otherwise. For 3+ classes the model has one sigmoid unit; for `num_classes=1` it builds a one-unit softmax.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `decon_Sep_classification((256, 1), num_classes=3).output_shape` -> `(None, 1)`, last activation `sigmoid` (`nicon_classification(..., num_classes=3)` -> `(None, 3)`, `softmax`).
- Impact: multi-class training with this architecture fails (sparse-categorical loss on a 1-unit output) or learns nonsense.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): `if num_classes == 2: sigmoid(1) else: softmax(num_classes)`.

##### OM1-12 — TF `inception1D` reads the sequence length from the channel axis and crashes

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: inception1D((256,1),{}) raises negative output-size error on effective length1; input_shape[1] uses channel count for sequence length.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/tensorflow/generic.py:746` (`length = input_shape[1]`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: The TF controller passes `input_shape = (features, channels)` (see `get_preferred_layout`, and `UNet_NIRS`/`SEResNet_model` use `input_shape[0]` for the length). `inception1D` uses `input_shape[1]` (= channels, usually 1) as the length and takes the channel count from `params['num_channel']`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `inception1D((256, 1), {})` -> `ValueError: Computed output size would be zero or negative. Received inputs shape=(None, 1, 1), kernel shape=(3, 1, 16)`.
- Impact: the model cannot be built for any single-channel spectrum; with several channels it builds with a bogus length.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): `length, num_channel = input_shape[0], input_shape[-1]`.

##### OM1-14 — JAX classification builders emit probabilities while the JAX controller treats outputs as logits (NaN loss for binary)

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Actual Flax binary output has one probability; controller loss expression yields 0/NaN alternating labels, multiclass rows already sum to 1 before controller softmax. Full JaxModelController training for one epoch was replayed with BINARY_CLASSIFICATION and returned finite outputs despite NaN objective. Parameters changed, so no claim of always-NaN predictions or zero gradients is justified; invalid objective and double-squashed probabilities are confirmed.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/jax/nicon.py:286-291` (also `:337-342`, `:372-377`, `:420-425`, `:520-529`, `:566-571`); `nirs4all/controllers/models/jax_model.py:243,246,273` and `:399-403`
- Category: correctness
- Description: Actual Flax binary output has one probability; controller loss expression yields 0/NaN alternating labels, multiclass rows already sum to 1 before controller softmax. Full JaxModelController training for one epoch was replayed with BINARY_CLASSIFICATION and returned finite outputs despite NaN objective. Parameters changed, so no claim of always-NaN predictions or zero gradients is justified; invalid objective and double-squashed probabilities are confirmed.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`nicon_classification((300, 1), num_classes=nc)` + the controller loss):
```
num_classes=2: output (8, 1) in (0.44, 0.49); loss per sample [0., nan, 0., nan, nan, 0., nan, 0.]
num_classes=3: rows already sum to 1 -> softmax applied twice (loss ~1.04-1.16, flat gradients)
```

- Impact: Binary training uses an invalid objective and prediction probabilities are activated twice. A fresh controller run returned finite predictions and changed parameters, so universally NaN predictions or zero gradients are not claimed.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): make the JAX classification builders return logits (no final activation) and use sigmoid-BCE for the 1-unit binary case in the controller.

##### OM1-15 — `DiversitySelector` ranks "best" models with an ascending sort regardless of the metric direction

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: DiversitySelector chooses r2=.5 over.9 within same estimator class; unconditional ascending ordering selects worst for higher-is-better metrics.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/selection.py:488` and `:493` (`group.sort(key=lambda c: c.val_score or float('inf'))`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Candidates carry `metric` and `TopKByMetricSelector` infers the direction from it, but `DiversitySelector` always sorts ascending and takes `group[:max_per_class]` as "best first". For higher-is-better metrics (r2, accuracy, balanced_accuracy: the default classification score) it keeps the worst models of each class.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (metric `r2`, two PLS candidates 0.90 and 0.50, two RF candidates 0.0 and -0.3, `max_per_class=1`):
```
Diversity -> [('PLS_b', 0.5), ('RF_b', -0.3)]     # worst of each class
```

- Impact: stacking with `selector=DiversitySelector(...)` on classification (or r2-scored regression) feeds the meta-model the worst fold model per class.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): infer `ascending` from `candidate.metric` like `TopKByMetricSelector` (or accept a `metric`/`ascending` argument).

##### OM1-16 — `SpectralTransformer` ignores `pool` (CLS pooling is hard-wired; the mean-pooling branch sits in a comment)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Two SpectralTransformer models with identical weights, cls/mean pooling, produce exactly identical outputs; mean conditional is inside comment.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/pytorch/spectral_transformer.py:431` (`x = x[:, 0]  # Use CLS token if self.pool == 'cls' else x[:, 1:].mean(dim=1)`); builder default `:470` (`pool=params.get('pool', 'mean')`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: The conditional expression is part of the trailing comment, so the forward pass always returns the CLS token. The builder's default (`'mean'`, commented "works better for spectral data") and the documented `pool` option have no effect.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): two models with identical weights and `pool='cls'` vs `pool='mean'`: `max |out_cls - out_mean| = 0.0`.
- Impact: every `spectral_transformer*` factory trains CLS-pooled models irrespective of `params['pool']`.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): `x = x[:, 0] if self.pool == 'cls' else x[:, 1:].mean(dim=1)`.

##### OM1-17 — Selectors treat a validation score of exactly 0.0 as `inf`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: TopK chooses0 over.8 for r2 and.8 overperfect0 for RMSE. Using score or inf misranks zero-valued scores.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/selection.py:397,401,405-406` (`TopKByMetricSelector`), `:488,493` (`DiversitySelector`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `c.val_score or float('inf')` maps a legitimate `0.0` to `inf`. With `ascending=True` (RMSE) a perfect 0.0 model ranks last; with higher-is-better metrics (R2, accuracy) a 0.0 model outranks every positive score.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
TopK k=1, metric r2, candidates 0.0 and 0.8   -> selects the 0.0 model
TopK k=1, metric rmse, candidates 0.0 and 5.0 -> selects the 5.0 model
```

- Impact: wrong source model chosen for stacking whenever a score is exactly zero (constant-mean predictor, perfect fit on a small fold, accuracy 0).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use `key=lambda c: c.val_score` (candidates with `None` are already filtered) with the explicit `reverse`.

##### OM1-18 — `IntervalPLS` (NumPy) swallows all CV errors and silently selects interval 0

##### OM1-22 — `OKLMPLS`: `random_state` unused; returned `B`/`F` are not re-solved for the final `W`

- Category: correctness
- Description: Random initialisation (used when `warm_start_pls=False`) ignores `random_state`. In each iteration `W` is updated by a gradient step and QR after `B`/`F` were solved for the previous `W`, and the function returns that mismatched triple.

- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use `random_state` for the init and recompute `B` (and `F`) from the final `W` before returning.
