#### AOM-NIRS package `_aom_nirs/` (AOM)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 17 AOM claims were reviewed with numerical/parameter/serialization probes. Copied EMSC is grouped under OPO-01 with both locations retained. Positive Ridge penalties and nonzero gamma effects disprove the original universal no-shrinkage wording. Unwired vendored GPU execution remains source-only.

##### AOM-01 — FastAOM PLS-Ridge selects lambda using training loss instead of the promised validation criterion

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Training-MSE lambda selection reproduced as 0 on the default grid and 1e-4 on a positive grid, contradicting promised LOO. 'Never ridge', 'smallest always', and 'gamma dead' are overstatements: positive grids apply shrinkage; a singular zero-penalty system may be skipped; ties/numerics can choose differently; gamma affects nonzero selected penalties.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/_aom_nirs/fast/models/_common.py:151-164` (`ridge_on_scores`); consumers `fast/models/single_chain_pls_ridge.py:175`, `fast/models/hard_aom_chain_pls_ridge.py:199`, `fast/models/soft_aom_chain_pls_ridge.py:216`
- Category: correctness
- Description: Training-MSE lambda selection reproduced as 0 on the default grid and 1e-4 on a positive grid, contradicting promised LOO. 'Never ridge', 'smallest always', and 'gamma dead' are overstatements: positive grids apply shrinkage; a singular zero-penalty system may be skipped; ties/numerics can choose differently; gamma affects nonzero selected penalties.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```python
  for lam in lambdas:
      ...
      c = np.linalg.solve(TtT + reg, Tty)
      yhat = T @ c
      loss = float(np.mean((y_centred - yhat) ** 2))   # training loss
      if loss < best[2]:
          best = (c, float(lam), loss)
  ```
  `t_fast.py`: `FastAOMPLSRidge(FastAOMConfig(model=m))` on a noisy synthetic set -> `lambda_` = 0.0 for `single_chain`, `hard_aom_chain`, `soft_aom_chain` (all 15 components, no shrinkage); `sparse_mkr` (GCV) picks 0.001. `t_fast_lambda.py`: `SingleChainPLSRidge(lambdas=(1e-4,1e-2,1,1e2,1e4,1e6))` -> `lambda_ = 0.0001` (the smallest); `ridge_on_scores(T, yc, [1e-6,1e-2,1,100,1e4])` -> 1e-06.
- Impact: Training-loss selection favors insufficient regularization on the tested default grid. Positive-only grids do apply shrinkage, and component_shrinkage_gamma remains active for nonzero selected penalties.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Choose lambda with a genuine held-out/LOO/GCV criterion. Positive penalties already perform shrinkage and gamma is active for them.

##### AOM-02 — `ExtendedMSC` (default `scale=True`) builds its reference spectrum from mean-centred data, so the reference is ~0 and the output is ~1e19-scale garbage

- Re-audit verdict: **DUPLICATE — canonical item below**
- Re-audit result: Canonical: `OPO-01`. Same centered-reference EMSC defect as OPO-01, independently reproduced in distinct vendored file (ref2e-15/output6.8e22). Treat as second repair location, not same physical implementation.
- Fix disposition: Track and fix only under OPO-01; retain this location/reference.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: underlying behavior reviewed; not an independent finding
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/_aom_nirs/pls/preprocessing.py:218-223` (`ExtendedMSC.partial_fit`), `:242` (`transform`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: with `scale=True` the training data are centred (`tmp_x = scaler.transform(tmp_x)`) and then `reference_ = np.mean(tmp_x, axis=0)` is taken, i.e. the mean of centred data (zero up to round-off). The EMSC design matrix's first column is then ~0, `coeffs[0]` is round-off noise and `X_transformed[i] = (... ) / coeffs[0]` blows up (random per-row scale ~1e15-1e20). The same bug exists in the production transform it was vendored from; `fast/bases.py::EMSCBase` is a separate (correct) implementation.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```python
  scaler.fit(X); self.scaler_ = scaler
  tmp_x = scaler.transform(tmp_x)                 # centred
  self.reference_ = np.mean(tmp_x, axis=0)        # ~0
  ...
  X_transformed[i] = (X_transformed[i] - polynomial_part) / coeffs[0]
  ```
  `t_emsc.py`: `ExtendedMSC(degree=2, scale=True).fit(X).reference_` max|.| = 1.4e-15, `transform` max|.| = 1.5e+20; with `scale=False` reference max = 5.9, output max = 5.95. `t_mkl3.py`: `AOMMultiBranchMKL` default `emsc2` branch output max|Z| = 4.5e19 (input max 7.4). `t_auto.py`: the default `AOMRidgeAutoSelector` candidate "AOMRidgePLS-compact-Hmax-relative-emsc2" scores CV RMSE 1.39 vs 0.05 for the others.
- Impact: every consumer of `ExtendedMSC(degree=1|2)` is silently fed garbage: `branches.py:106-109` (`emsc1`/`emsc2` for `AOMRidgeRegressor(selection="branch_global")`), `multi_branch_mkl.py:90-91` (`emsc2` is in the *default* `branches` of `AOMMultiBranchMKL`), and the default candidate pools of `AOMRidgeAutoSelector`/`AOMRidgeBlender`. CV/KTA usually down-weights the branch (so the damage is wasted compute and a noisy kernel mixed in at a small weight), but a requested EMSC pre-treatment is never actually applied, and rows with an exactly-zero reference produce inf/NaN.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): compute `reference_` from the un-centred training matrix (or use the centred rows consistently, i.e. reference = mean of raw X and subtract the scaler mean from the rows only for the polynomial terms).

##### AOM-03 — `AOMLocalRidge` selects "nearest neighbours" by raw inner product, not by kernel-induced distance

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: For train[-6,-.9,-.1,.1,.9,5,6,7],query1,k2, helper picks[7,6] while distance-nearest[.9,.1]. Raw inner-product ranking omits train norm term; original negative-side example depends on centering, not universally that particular ordering.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/_aom_nirs/ridge/local_ridge.py:272-281` (`_topk_indices_per_row`), used at `:306` (`_batched_local_ridge_path`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: the module/class docs say the k nearest training samples in the superblock-AOM kernel-induced metric are used, but the neighbour set is `argpartition(-K_cross, k-1)`, i.e. the k training rows with the largest *inner product* `<phi(x), phi(x_j)>`. The squared kernel distance is `K(x,x) - 2 K(x,x_j) + K(x_j,x_j)`; dropping the training-norm term `K_jj` means large-norm (outlying, after centring) samples are preferred and, for a query on the negative side of the mean, the *farthest* samples on the opposite side are chosen.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t_local.py` (identity bank, 1-D line in feature space, train positions [-6,-0.9,-0.1,0.1,0.9,5,6,7], query at 1.0, k=2): local ridge picks `[-6.0, -0.9]`; true 2 nearest in the same metric are `[0.1, 0.9]`.
  ```python
  return np.argpartition(-K_cross, k - 1, axis=1)[:, :k]   # largest similarity, no K_jj term
  ```

- Impact: the "local" part of `AOMLocalRidge` fits on essentially arbitrary/high-leverage neighbours, so local predictions (and the CV-chosen blend `beta`) are degraded; CV can only fall back to `beta=0` (global ridge).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): rank by `2*K_cross - diag(K_tr)[None, :]` (negative squared distance up to a per-row constant).

##### AOM-04 — `AOMMultiBranchMKL(center=True)` never centres X: kernels are built from the uncentred branch output (== no-intercept ridge on raw X)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: centerTrue branch-none identity MKL matches raw-X no-intercept Ridge on centered y within7e-15, differs from centered Ridge by.643. xmean stored unused in kernels.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/_aom_nirs/ridge/multi_branch_mkl.py:139-147` (`_branch_kernel`), `:335-348` (`fit_branches_and_kernels`), `:545-554` and `:633` (`fit`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `fit` computes `x_mean` and stores `x_mean_` but never subtracts it; `Z = _apply_branch(transformer, X_tr)` goes straight into `K_b = Z A^T A Z^T` and the cross kernels. Only `y` is centred. The comment at `:552` says the branch transformer must see raw X (true), but the centring after the branch is missing, contradicting the "consistent with the existing AOM-Ridge estimator" claim. Because the ops are linear, the kernel equals that of an un-centred ridge: the (large) mean-spectrum direction is penalised like any other feature and acts as an implicit, shrunken intercept.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t_mkl2.py` (branches=("none",), identity bank, same effective alpha via the kernel's trace normalisation): `max|MKL - Ridge(fit_intercept=False on raw X, centred y)| = 9.4e-14` while `max|MKL - Ridge(fit_intercept=True)| = 0.68`; test RMSE 0.263 (MKL) vs 0.064 (centred Ridge / `AOMRidgeRegressor`, which matches sklearn to 1e-15).
- Impact: predictions of `AOMMultiBranchMKL` depend on the spectral offset (e.g. +3.0 baseline) and differ from every other AOM-Ridge estimator for the same alpha; the alpha-CV partly compensates by moving to tiny alphas, but the branch KTA scores/weights are also computed on uncentred kernels.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): centre `Z` (train mean, replayed on cross rows) after the branch transform, per fold, as `AOMRidgeRegressor` does.

- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/_aom_nirs/pls/classification.py:182` (`T_train = res.T`) vs `:229-233` (`transform` = `Xc @ x_effective_weights_`); engines in `pls/nipals.py:197-231,380-414`, `pls/selection.py:59-65,706-717`
- Category: correctness
- Description: for `engine in {"nipals_adjoint", "nipals_materialized", "pls_standard"}` (and `selection="soft"`), `res.Z` holds weights relative to the *deflated* matrix and `res.T` the deflated scores (`T = Xres @ w`), so `T != Xc @ Z` from the second component on (correct rotation is `Z (P^T Z)^-1`). The logistic calibrator is trained on `res.T` but `predict_proba` feeds it `Xc @ Z`, a different feature space. Only the SIMPLS engines (default) satisfy `T == X Z`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t_plsda2.py` (3-class, n=300, 6 comps, identity bank): `max|X@Z - x_scores_|` = 1.1e-16 for `simpls_covariance` but 5.4 for `nipals_adjoint` / `nipals_materialized`, while `max|X@R - x_scores_| = 7e-15`; test balanced accuracy 0.537 as shipped vs 0.613 when the same fitted calibrator is fed `X@R`.
- Impact: `AOMPLSDAClassifier/POPPLSDAClassifier(engine="nipals_adjoint"|"nipals_materialized"|"pls_standard")` give wrong probabilities/classes, and `transform`/`fit_transform` return wrong latent scores.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): store/transform with the rotation `R = Z (P^T Z)^+` (or train the calibrator on `Xc @ Z`).

##### AOM-06 — `select_soft` builds the component direction from the first Y column only (PLS2/PLS-DA)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Soft selection with noise first-target/signal second-target yields signal R2.010 and permuting targets changes coefficient direction by.634. Code uses only covariance column0.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/_aom_nirs/pls/selection.py:697-701` (`select_soft`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: operator scores use the Frobenius norm of the whole `A S`, but the direction fed to the adjoint is `r = op.apply_cov(S)[:, 0]` — the other Y columns are ignored (every other engine uses the dominant left singular vector for q>1). For PLS-DA (q = n_classes) the soft-mixture components are driven by class 0 only.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```python
  r = op.apply_cov(S) if S.ndim == 1 else op.apply_cov(S)
  r = r if r.ndim == 1 else r[:, 0]
  ```
  `t_soft.py` (Y = [noise column, signal column], 1 component): R^2 on the signal column = 0.008 for `select_soft` vs 0.863 for `select(..., "per_component", covariance)`.
- Impact: `AOMPLSDAClassifier/POPPLSDAClassifier(selection="soft")` (and any multi-target use) yields components uninformative for the non-first targets.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use `_dominant_direction(op.apply_cov(S))` per operator.

##### AOM-07 — Approximate PRESS uses component-insensitive full-SVD leverage and can favor excessive components

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: PRESS/SSE ratio constant~1e18 on centered20x40data. Full SVD U includes null singular directions and clips leverage1; even rank-aware centered full-row-rank hat would give common leverage and fail component-aware validation. 'Always maximum' only when training SSE decreases strictly; ties/early termination can differ.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/_aom_nirs/pls/scorers.py:192-202` (`approx_press_regression`), used by `pls/selection.py:128-145,591-600`
- Category: numerical
- Description: PRESS/SSE ratio constant~1e18 on centered20x40data. Full SVD U includes null singular directions and clips leverage1; even rank-aware centered full-row-rank hat would give common leverage and fail component-aware validation. 'Always maximum' only when training SSE decreases strictly; ties/early termination can differ.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t_press.py` (n=60, p=100, mostly-noise y): leverages min=max=1.0; PRESS/SSE ratio = 1.0e18 constant; `argmin PRESS -> k=15` of 15; `AOMPLSDAClassifier(criterion="approx_press")` -> `n_components_ = 15` vs `criterion="cv"` -> 1.
- Impact: The approximate PRESS criterion lacks component-aware leverage and can overselect when training error decreases; always selecting the maximum is not established.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): compute leverage from the PLS score space (hat of `T`) or fall back to CV when n <= p.

##### AOM-08 — `AOMMultiKernelRidge(alphas=<ndarray>)` crashes on `if self.alphas == "auto"`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Documented ndarray alphas raises ambiguous truth value in fit at self.alphas==auto; list accepted.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/_aom_nirs/ridge/mkr_estimator.py:250` (`fit`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: the docstring accepts "auto or sequence of positive floats", but a NumPy array makes `self.alphas == "auto"` an array comparison whose truth value is ambiguous. Lists work; arrays (e.g. `np.logspace`, the common way to build a grid, and what `clone`/Optuna-grid code produces) do not.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t_misc.py`: `AOMMultiKernelRidge(alphas=np.logspace(-3,1,5)).fit(X, y)` -> `ValueError: The truth value of an array with more than one element is ambiguous`; `alphas=[0.001,0.01,0.1]` works.
- Impact: valid input crashes at fit.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): `isinstance(self.alphas, str) and self.alphas == "auto"` (as `AOMRidgeRegressor._resolve_alpha_grid` does).

##### AOM-09 — `AOMMultiKernelRidge(one_se_rule=True)` uses std across the alpha grid, not a standard error across CV folds

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Toy alpha curve selects index5 although minindex2, using std of all alpha scores divided sqrt(nalphas). This is not fold-wise one-standard-error and depends on grid span.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/operators/models/_aom_nirs/ridge/mkr_estimator.py:117-134` (`_select_alpha_with_one_se`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `se = std(rmse_per_alpha) / sqrt(n_alphas)` is the dispersion of the CV curve over the (log-spaced) alpha values, which grows with the grid span, not the fold-to-fold SE of the minimum that the docstring promises ("largest alpha within one-standard-error of minimum CV RMSE"). The rest of the package (`select_alpha_with_rule`) uses the true fold SE.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t_1se.py`: toy curve `[.30,.10,.05,.05,.06,.10,.30,.9,1,1]`: argmin idx 2 (0.05), 1-SE pick idx 5 (0.10, threshold 0.174); on a synthetic dataset `one_se_rule=True` moves alpha index 18 -> 24 and test RMSE 0.112 -> 0.132.
- Impact: over-regularised model when `one_se_rule=True`.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): compute the SE from per-fold RMSEs at the argmin (reuse `select_alpha_with_rule`).

##### AOM-12 — PLS-DA classifiers: `scale` is ignored and a NumPy-integer `n_components` is treated as "auto"

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**

- Category: api-contract
- Description: `scale=True` has no effect on the fit; `n_components=np.int64(3)` fails the `isinstance(..., int)` test and goes down the auto path (with `criterion="covariance"` that means `max_components`, up to 25).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t_misc.py`: `n_components=3` -> `n_components_=3`, `np.int64(3)` -> 25; `scale=True` and `scale=False` give `np.allclose(coef_)` True on unequally-scaled features.

##### AOM-15 — Adaptive alpha-grid expansion re-runs identical CV for explicit `alphas` and over-reports `grid_expansions`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**

- Category: other
- Description: with an explicit `alphas` sequence the grid does not depend on `low`/`high`, but when the optimum sits within 2 of an edge the loop still "expands" (changing only `low`/`high`) and repeats the full CV up to `max_grid_expansions+1` times with the same result; `info["expansions"]` is also incremented on the last iteration where the expansion is never used.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `t_grid.py`: `alphas=[1e3..1e8]`, identity bank -> `select_alpha_superblock` called 3 times, `diagnostics_["grid_expansions"] == 3` with `max_grid_expansions=2`, grid unchanged.
- Impact: up to 3x CV cost and misleading diagnostics.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): skip expansion when `alphas` is not `"auto"`; increment the counter only when a new grid is actually evaluated.

### 5.7 Synthesis — `nirs4all/synthesis/`

#### Synthetic data generation (SYN)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 29 SYN claims were checked against builder, public generation, fitted parameters and export callers. Targeted fitting code review covers RNG and parameter propagation; physical interpretation and multi-sensor noise claims are explicitly bounded. Tables/constants were consulted for affected parameters, not independently scientifically validated. 2256 data tests passed.

##### SYN-01 — `regime_method="spectral"` always crashes with RecursionError (spectra never passed; fallback recurses into itself)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: First build with spectral regime recursively calls same assignment withspectraNone; builder assignsstate._Xonlyaftertargets. Public fluent call raisesRecursionError. Always applies normal first build, not arbitrary manual injected state.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/builder.py:958-960` (`_generate_data`), `nirs4all/synthesis/builder.py:1129` (`_apply_nonlinear_complexity`), `nirs4all/synthesis/targets.py:1020-1023` (`NonLinearTargetProcessor._assign_regimes`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `_generate_data` calls `_process_targets` (which reads `self.state._X` for "spectral" regimes) before it assigns `self.state._X = X`, so `spectra` is always `None` on the first build. `_assign_regimes` then "falls back" with `return self._assign_regimes(C, None)` but `regime_method` is still `"spectral"`, so it recurses forever.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```python
  # builder.py
  958  y = self._process_targets(C, generator)     # reads self.state._X (still None)
  960  self.state._X = X
  # targets.py
  1021 if spectra is None:
  1023     return self._assign_regimes(C, None)    # same method, same config -> infinite recursion
  ```
  Repro (`t4b.py`): `SyntheticDatasetBuilder(60, random_state=1).with_complex_target_landscape(n_regimes=3, regime_method="spectral").build_arrays()` -> `RecursionError: maximum recursion depth exceeded`; also `NonLinearTargetProcessor(...regime_method="spectral").process(C, y, spectra=None)` -> RecursionError.
- Impact: the documented `"spectral"` option of `with_complex_target_landscape` can never be used; even with the recursion fixed it would silently never use the spectra because of the ordering bug.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): set `self.state._X = X` before `_process_targets` (or pass `X` explicitly), and make the `spectra is None` fallback use the concentration branch directly instead of re-entering `_assign_regimes` with the same method.

##### SYN-02 — `nirs4all.generate.from_template(<path>)` throws away the real wavelength grid (generates on 0..N-1 "nm")

- Re-audit verdict: **DUPLICATE — canonical item below**
- Re-audit result: Canonical: `API-09`. Fresh path-template CSV with 30 wavelengths spanning 1100–2400 nm returns headers [0, 1, 2, ... 29]. This is the same bug as API-09: missing SpectroDataset.wavelengths attribute loses the axis. Calling wavelengths_nm on the generated dataset additionally rejects the zero nm header.
- Fix disposition: Track and fix only under API-09; retain this location/reference.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: underlying behavior reviewed; not an independent finding
- Location: `nirs4all/api/generate.py:907-911` (`from_template`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: for a path template the wavelengths are read with `getattr(template_ds, 'wavelengths', None)`, but `SpectroDataset` has no `wavelengths` attribute (it exposes `float_headers()` / `wavelengths_nm()`); the `AttributeError` is suppressed, `wavelengths` stays `None`, and `RealDataFitter.fit` falls back to `np.arange(n_wavelengths)`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
  ```python
  909  with contextlib.suppress(AttributeError, TypeError):
  910      wavelengths = getattr(template_ds, 'wavelengths', None)
  911  builder.fit_to(template_array, wavelengths=wavelengths)
  ```
  Repro (`t23.py`): folder exported with wavelengths 1100-2498 nm -> `DatasetConfigs(out)` reads `float_headers = [1100, 2498]`, `hasattr(ds, "wavelengths") == False`; `nirs4all.generate.from_template(out, n_samples=30)` returns a dataset with wavelengths `[0., 699.]` (700 points). `SyntheticDatasetBuilder(30).fit_to(real_dataset)` (passing the dataset object) correctly gives `[1100, 2498]`.
- Impact: the first documented example of `from_template` ("From a dataset path") produces spectra on an index axis; all NIR bands (1000-2500 nm) fall outside the grid, so the synthetic data neither resembles the template nor carries correct wavelength headers.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): pass the dataset object to `builder.fit_to(template_ds)` (the fitter already extracts `float_headers()`), or use `template_ds.float_headers(0)`.

##### SYN-03 — `with_metadata(...)` / `with_partitions(stratify=...)` are accepted but never reach the built dataset

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: with_metadata(n_groups3,n_repetitions2..3).build yieldsmetadata_columns[]. Generated sample metadata is assigned but never attached; stratify state is stored/serialized without use in split.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/builder.py:520-569` (`with_metadata`), `nirs4all/synthesis/builder.py:963-973` (`_generate_sample_metadata`), `nirs4all/synthesis/builder.py:1137-1189` (`_build_dataset`), `nirs4all/synthesis/builder.py:692`
- Category: api-contract
- Description: `_generate_data` builds `MetadataGenerationResult` (sample ids, bio sample ids, repetitions, groups) into `state._sample_metadata`, but `_build_dataset`/`_build_arrays` never read it; `state.stratify` and `state.include_metadata` are also write-only. The `nirs4all.generate.builder(...).with_metadata(n_groups=3)` example in `api/generate.py:434` therefore does nothing visible.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): repro `t4c.py`: `SyntheticDatasetBuilder(60, random_state=1).with_metadata(n_groups=3, n_repetitions=(2,3), sample_id_prefix="S").build()` -> `b.state._sample_metadata is not None` (60 rows generated) but `dataset.metadata_columns == []`; `grep _sample_metadata` shows only the assignment at builder.py:973.
- Impact: users asking for grouped/repeated samples (for group-aware CV) get a dataset with no group column and no repetition structure, silently.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): attach `_sample_metadata.to_dict()` to the dataset (honouring the train/test permutation) and drop or implement `stratify`/`include_metadata`.

##### SYN-04 — Multi-source datasets are built as ONE concatenated source with a header list of the wrong length

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh create_dataset with two 51-channel NIR sources returns one source, X shape (20, 102), and only 51 wavelength headers. The builder delegates to this concatenating implementation.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/sources.py:452-472` (`MultiSourceGenerator.create_dataset`), `nirs4all/synthesis/builder.py:1236-1252`
- Category: correctness
- Description: `create_dataset` documents "SpectroDataset with multiple sources configured" but hstacks all sources into `X_combined` and adds it as a single source, with `headers` taken only from the first NIR source.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): repro `t3.py`: sources = NIR 1000-1700 (351 pts) + NIR 1700-2500 (401 pts) + 15 aux markers -> dataset reports `Features (samples=50, sources=1): Source 0: (50, 1, 767)` while `len(ds.headers(0)) == 351`.
- Impact: `with_sources([...])` / `nirs4all.generate.multi_source(...)` never yields a multi-source dataset (multi-source controllers/branches cannot be exercised), and the header vector does not match the 767 feature columns, so wavelength-aware operators/plots mis-align or fail.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): add one source per entry (per-source headers/units) instead of a stacked matrix.

##### SYN-05 — Synthesis operators are used without `fit()`, so their RNG is re-created from the seed on every call (repeated `generate()` reuses the same noise / path-length draws)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: A spy over two consecutive generate() calls records identical standardized path-length draws and identical standardized noise matrices. Unfitted synthesis operators recreate default_rng(seed) on every transform.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:584-604` (operators built, never fitted), `generator.py:729`, `generator.py:865`; `nirs4all/operators/augmentation/synthesis.py:79` and `:289` (`rng = getattr(self, '_rng', np.random.default_rng(self.random_state))`); `nirs4all/operators/augmentation/environmental.py:175`
- Category: correctness
- Description: `PathLengthAugmenter`/`HeteroscedasticNoiseAugmenter` only create their persistent `_rng` in `fit()`. The generator calls `.transform()` directly, so `getattr(..., default_rng(seed))` returns a freshly seeded generator on every call; `TemperatureAugmenter` does `default_rng(self.random_state)` inside every transform. With a fixed `random_state`, every `generate()` call on the same generator re-applies the identical standard-normal noise matrix and path-length factors.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t2.py`): a spy on `_noise_op.transform` shows `z = (out-in)/sigma` identical between two consecutive `gen.generate(20)` calls (`np.allclose(rec[0], rec[1]) == True`) and identical `path_length` factors, although concentrations differ between the calls.
- Impact: datasets generated in several calls from one generator (e.g. separate train/test via `generate()`/`generate_from_concentrations`, `ProductGenerator`/`CategoryGenerator` reuse) share exactly the same noise realisation, artificially correlating "independent" samples; only reproducibility of a single call is intended.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): call `fit()` once in `_init_synthesis_operators` (and for the environmental/scattering/edge operators) or draw from the generator's own `self.rng`.

##### SYN-06 — Every sub-component is seeded with the same integer, so independent random effects are identical streams (noise == path length; hidden factor == path length; equal sources identical)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: In the same spy, correlation between standardized path-length draws and the first N flattened noise draws is exactly 1.0. Child operators receive the same seed. Not every effect is universally identical if draw order or distributions differ.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:267,584-604`; `nirs4all/synthesis/builder.py:927,1123-1126`; `nirs4all/synthesis/sources.py:301-308`; `nirs4all/synthesis/products.py:661-676,860-869`
- Category: correctness
- Description: generator RNG, path-length op, noise op, `NonLinearTargetProcessor`, `TargetGenerator`, `MetadataGenerator`, `ProductGenerator.rng` and each multi-source sub-generator are all `default_rng(random_state)` with the same seed, so they draw the same underlying normal stream.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): (`t2.py`) path-length `z` equals the first `n` noise `z` values (`corr == 1.0`); (`t26.py`) with `with_nonlinear_targets(hidden_factors=1)` (interactions "none"), the "no spectral signature" hidden-factor effect on y has `corr == 1.0` with the path-length multiplier applied to the spectra; (`t12.py`) `ProductGenerator("corn_grain", random_state=3)`: `corr(starch concentration, path-length factor) = 0.986`; (`t3.py`) two NIR sources with the same wavelength range and `MultiSourceGenerator(random_state=5)` are bit-identical (`np.allclose(A, B) == True`, noise included).
- Impact: spurious dependence between concentrations/targets and nuisance effects (a target "hidden factor" is recoverable from spectral scale), and multi-source datasets whose sources share identical noise/baselines. Only visible with a fixed `random_state` (the default for reproducible research).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): derive child seeds from a single `SeedSequence(random_state).spawn(k)` (or `rng.integers`) for each component/source.

##### SYN-07 — `generate(temperatures=...)` and `TemperatureConfig.sample_temperature` are ignored; recorded `metadata["temperatures"]` is not what was applied

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Explicit temperatures are copied into metadata, but temperature_op.transform receives only X and wavelengths. When temperature_variation is positive, the uniform range [-variation, +variation] discards the configured mean temperature delta.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:1405-1424` (`generate`), `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:1577-1590` (`generate_from_concentrations`), `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:427-444` (`_init_environmental_operators`)
- Category: api-contract
- Description: the `temperatures` argument ("Optional array of temperatures (°C) for each sample") is only copied into the metadata; `_temperature_op.transform` never receives it. The operator is configured once with either a fixed `delta_temperature` or, when `temperature_variation > 0`, `temperature_range=(-variation, +variation)` — which overrides the delta entirely, so `sample_temperature - reference_temperature` is dropped. Metadata temperatures are drawn from N(sample_temperature, variation) with the generator RNG, unrelated to the uniform deltas actually used.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t1.py`): `generate(50, temperatures=np.full(50, 25.))` vs `temperatures=np.full(50, 60.)` with the same seed -> `identical X: True`. With `TemperatureConfig(sample_temperature=45, temperature_variation=2)` the operator is built with `temperature_range=(-2.0, 2.0)` (delta 20 °C ignored).
- Impact: any temperature-controlled experiment (e.g. calibration-transfer across temperatures) silently uses the wrong effect size, and the metadata labels do not describe the spectra.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): apply per-sample deltas `temperatures - reference_temperature` (extend the operator to accept a vector) and use `delta ± variation` for the range.

##### SYN-08 — `measurement_mode` has no effect on generated spectra

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Seeded transmittance, reflectance and ATR generators produce identical spectra. The simulator is always constructed as transmittance and never applied.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:338-347` (`__init__`), builder `nirs4all/synthesis/builder.py:344-345,926`
- Category: api-contract
- Description: `SyntheticNIRSGenerator(measurement_mode=...)` stores the mode but always builds `create_transmittance_simulator(...)`, and `generate()`/`generate_from_concentrations()` never use `measurement_mode_simulator` (nor `instrument_simulator`, created at l.310).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t15.py`): `generate(20)` with `measurement_mode in (None, "transmittance", "reflectance", "atr", "transflectance")` and the same seed -> `all modes give identical X: True`; `SyntheticDatasetBuilder(30, random_state=1).with_features(measurement_mode="atr").build_arrays()` equals the default build.
- Impact: documented/advertised reflectance, ATR and transflectance simulation (docstring example `instrument="foss_xds", measurement_mode="reflectance"`) is a no-op.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): apply `MeasurementModeSimulator` with the requested mode, or reject/warn for unsupported modes.

##### SYN-09 — Product templates: `CORRELATED` components do not reproduce the requested correlation and are clipped to their bounds for most samples

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: CORRELATED composition generation normalizes its source to [0, 1], mixes it with a unit-variance Gaussian, shifts by 0.5, and clips to the target bounds. This does not preserve the requested Pearson correlation; the initial precise clipping percentages were not freshly measured.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/products.py:759-793` (`ProductGenerator._sample_compositions`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: the source is normalised to [0, 1] (std ≈ 0.29) but mixed with a unit-variance noise term (`correlation*source_normalized + sqrt(1-corr²)*noise`), and the result is mapped with `(correlated - 0.5) * target_range` around the bound midpoint. The effective correlation is weak, and the spread (≈0.6·range) far exceeds the allowed range so `np.clip` pins most samples to min/max.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t12.py`, 20 000 samples): `wheat_variable_protein` starch vs protein requested -0.85 -> achieved -0.29, 78 % of starch values sit exactly on a bound; `meat_variable_fat` water vs lipid requested -0.8 -> -0.27 (75 % clipped); `cheese_variable_moisture` lipid vs moisture requested -0.6 -> -0.16 (70 % clipped).
- Impact: the "correlation preservation between components" advertised for NN-training datasets is not delivered; component distributions are degenerate (point masses at the bounds).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): standardise the source (z-score), mix `rho*z + sqrt(1-rho²)*eps`, then map via mean/std (or the normal CDF onto the range) rather than a linear map plus clip.

##### SYN-10 — `generate_concentrations(method="correlated")` ignores the requested correlation (sign flipped; simplex normalisation adds spurious negative correlation)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Fresh 20,000-sample proof: requested +0.8 gives +0.1865, requested -0.8 gives +0.1970, requested 0 gives -0.2501. abs() loses the sign and simplex normalization induces dependencies. Arbitrary Pearson correlation matrices cannot all be realized on a simplex; any fix needs clear constraint semantics.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:690-703` (`_generate_correlated_concentrations`), reachable through `SyntheticDatasetBuilder.with_targets(distribution="correlated")`
- Category: numerical
- Description: after the Cholesky draw the code takes `np.abs(C)` (destroying negative correlations) and divides by the row sum (projecting to the simplex, which induces negative correlation between components).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t25.py`, 20 000 samples, requested `corr(c0,c1)=r`): r=+0.8 -> +0.19; r=-0.8 -> +0.20; r=0.0 -> -0.25.
- Impact: documented "Multivariate with specified correlations" is not honoured; users supplying `correlation_matrix` get unrelated structure.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): sample in a space that keeps the correlation (e.g. correlated Gaussians through a monotone map such as `exp`/CDF) and document that normalisation changes it, or normalise via a method that preserves rank correlation.

##### SYN-11 — `regime_method="concentration"` (the default) partitions samples on floating-point rounding noise for compositional data

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Default Dirichlet concentrations sum to one. The concentration-regime projection sums centered columns, giving approximately zero and quantile assignment based on roundoff instead of the stated principal direction.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/targets.py:1003-1018` (`NonLinearTargetProcessor._assign_regimes`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: with more than one component the "projection onto the first principal direction" is `C_centered.sum(axis=1)`. Concentrations from the default `dirichlet` (and `lognormal`/`correlated`) rows sum to 1, so this projection is identically ~0 and the quantile cuts are made on round-off.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t5.py`): Dirichlet concentrations -> `abs(projection).max() = 4.7e-16`; regime counts `[90 62 48]` but `max |corr(regime, C_j)| = 0.017` (i.e. unrelated to any component); with uniform concentrations the same code yields 0.45.
- Impact: `with_complex_target_landscape(n_regimes=3)` (default method) produces regimes that are effectively random and not "concentration-space clustering"; results depend on rounding.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): project on a real principal direction (e.g. first PC via SVD) or on a specific component.

##### SYN-12 — Threshold classification crashes for `separation > 3` ("scale < 0")

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**

- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/targets.py:466,471` (`TargetGenerator._classify_by_threshold`)
- Category: crash
- Description: `threshold_noise = (1 - separation / 3) * std * 0.5` becomes negative for `separation > 3`; `rng.normal(0, negative)` raises. The docs describe "2.5+" as "well-separated (easy)", so larger values are natural.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t5.py`): `TargetGenerator(0).classification(200, C, n_classes=3, separation=3.5, separation_method="threshold")` -> `ValueError: scale < 0`; `SyntheticDatasetBuilder(200, random_state=0).with_classification(n_classes=3, separation=4.0, separation_method="threshold").build_arrays()` -> same error.
- Impact: valid, documented input range crashes with a misleading message.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): `max(0.0, 1 - separation/3)`.

##### SYN-14 — `PriorSampler` domain names never match the domain registry; fallback components include the non-existent `"carbohydrate"`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Configured prior domain keys have an empty intersection with list_domains(). Seed-zero grain sampling falls back to the research category and selects the nonexistent carbohydrate component. Explicit canonical registry names are a different case.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/prior.py:95-115` (domain keys), `prior.py:442-455` (`sample_components`), `prior.py:514-520` (`_get_domain_category`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `NIRSPriorConfig` domains (`"grain"`, `"dairy"`, `"tablets"`, ...) are not keys of `APPLICATION_DOMAINS` (`"agriculture_grain"`, `"food_dairy"`, ...), so `get_domain_config` raises and the bare `except Exception` always selects the generic fallback list, whose `"carbohydrate"` is not a predefined component.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t18.py`): `[d for d in cfg.domain_weights if d in list_domains()] == []`; `PriorSampler(random_state=0).sample_for_domain("grain")` -> `domain_category: 'research'`, `components: ['lipid','cellulose','carbohydrate']`; `ComponentLibrary.from_predefined(s["components"])` -> `ValueError: Unknown predefined component: 'carbohydrate'`. With 5 fallback components and 3-5 drawn, ~80 % of sampled configs include it.
- Impact: domain-conditioned sampling (the purpose of the module) is never active and most sampled configs cannot be fed to the library/generator.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use registry domain names (or map them) and a valid fallback list; do not swallow the lookup error.

##### SYN-17 — Multi-scan `discard_outliers=True` overrides the configured averaging method with a plain mean

- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t7.py`, threshold=10 so nothing is an outlier): for `median`, `savgol`, `weighted` the output with `discard_outliers=True` equals the `mean`+discard output (`True`) while differing from the same method without discard; Savitzky-Golay roughness 0.2400 (no discard) vs 1.9508 (discard) = no smoothing.
- Impact: `MultiScanConfig(averaging_method="median"|"savgol"|"weighted", discard_outliers=True)` silently behaves as "mean".

##### SYN-18 — `AcceleratedGenerator` (NumPy backend) ignores `random_state`, and its noise level is `noise_level/sqrt(n_wavelengths)` of the signal RMS

- Category: numerical

- Description: `SyntheticDatasetConfig` carries `nonlinear`, `confounders`, `multi_regime` (and sub-fields such as `MetadataConfig.group_names`, `TargetConfig.component_indices`, `BatchEffectConfig.offset_std/gain_std`), but `from_config` copies only features/targets/metadata(part)/partitions/batch(enabled, n_batches)/output, and `get_config` never emits them; `generate_batch_effects` hard-codes 0.02/0.03.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t8.py`): `SyntheticDatasetConfig(nonlinear=NonLinearConfig("polynomial", hidden_factors=2), multi_regime=MultiRegimeConfig(n_regimes=3), confounders=ConfounderConfig(n_confounders=2, signal_to_confound_ratio=0.5))` -> `from_config(cfg).state`: `nonlinear_interactions none`, `hidden_factors 0`, `n_regimes 1`, `n_confounders 0`, `signal_to_confound_ratio 1.0`.
- Impact: config objects (the declarative route) give simpler targets than specified without any warning.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): map all config sections in `from_config`/`get_config`, or remove the unused fields.

##### SYN-21 — `get_zone_wavelength_range`, `get_all_zones_wavelength`, `get_all_zones_extended` return `(max, min)` instead of the documented `(min, max)`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: get_zone_wavelength_range(1st_overtones_CH) returns (1818.18, 1600), opposite to the documented ascending order. The visible-zone precedence subclaim was not freshly exercised.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/wavenumber.py:276-279`, `:295-299`, `:371-374`
- Category: api-contract
- Description: the zone tables store `(low_cm, high_cm, ...)` but these functions unpack them as `nu_max, nu_min` and return `(1e7/low_cm, 1e7/high_cm)` = `(large_nm, small_nm)`. `classify_wavelength_zone` (l.318) unpacks correctly. Additionally `classify_wavelength_extended(450/660)` returns `visible_electronic` (the docstring promises `blue_absorption`/`red_absorption`), because `VISIBLE_ZONES_WAVENUMBER` are fully shadowed by `EXTENDED_SPECTRAL_ZONES`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t10.py`): `get_zone_wavelength_range('1st_overtones_CH') -> (1818.18, 1600.0)` (docstring: `(1600.0, 1818.18...)`); `get_all_zones_extended()[0] -> (700.03, 350.005, 'visible_electronic', ...)`. The unit tests tolerate either order ("order may vary"), so the contract is unenforced.
- Impact: callers using `lo, hi = ...` get an inverted window (empty masks, negative widths).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): unpack as `(nu_min, nu_max, ...)` and return `(1e7/nu_max, 1e7/nu_min)`.

##### SYN-22 — `create_synthetic_matching_benchmark` does not match the benchmark's wavelength count; docstring example fails

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: create_synthetic_matching_benchmark(corn, n_samples=20) returns shape (20, 702), while the registry declares 700 channels. The step=range/n and end+step grid cause the mismatch.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/benchmarks.py:510` (`wavelength_step = range / n_wavelengths`), `benchmarks.py:548-554`, docstring `benchmarks.py:495`
- Category: correctness
- Description: step = range/n (should be range/(n-1)), then the generator builds `np.arange(start, end + step, step)`, adding extra points; measurement mode from the registry is never forwarded. The docstring example `SyntheticNIRSGenerator(**props)` raises `TypeError` (props contain `typical_components`, `n_samples`, ...).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t11.py`): `corn` -> `(20, 702)` vs `n_wavelengths=700`; `tecator` -> 101 vs 100; `milk` 1051 vs 1050; `SyntheticNIRSGenerator(**get_benchmark_spectral_properties("corn"))` -> `TypeError: ... unexpected keyword argument 'typical_components'`.
- Impact: "synthetic data matching benchmark" has a different spectral dimensionality than the benchmark it is compared against (e.g. in `validate_against_benchmark`, which raises on width mismatch).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use `wavelengths=np.linspace(start, end, n_wavelengths)`; fix the docstring.

##### SYN-23 — Wavelength headers are `str(int(wl))` (truncation, duplicates) and the default grid can overshoot `wavelength_end`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: A 0.5 nm custom grid yields duplicated integer headers. A 1000–1010 grid with step=0.1 ends at 1010.1000000000023. The cited authoring/exporter paths truncate wavelengths with int().
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:1668`, `builder.py:1162`, `sources.py:459`, `products.py:889`, `exporter.py:197,268,337,467`; grid at `generator.py:328-330`
- Category: correctness
- Description: headers are built with `int()` truncation (not rounding) so sub-nm grids and non-integer instrument grids lose information; and `np.arange(start, end + step, step)` with non-representable steps (0.1, 0.3, 0.7) can overshoot `wavelength_end`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t8.py`): `with_wavelengths(np.arange(1000.0, 1100.0, 0.5))` -> dataset headers `['1000','1000','1001','1001',...]`: 200 headers, 100 unique; MicroNIR-like `linspace(908.1, 1676.2, 125)` -> header error up to 0.987 nm; `SyntheticNIRSGenerator(wavelength_start=1000, wavelength_end=1010, wavelength_step=0.1)` -> last wavelength 1010.1 (> end), 102 points; (1100,1200,0.3) -> 1200.2.
- Impact: duplicate/shifted wavelength headers feed wavelength-aware operators and exports (CSV column names collide).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): format headers from the real values (`f"{wl:g}"`) and build the grid with `np.linspace`/`arange` on an integer count.

##### SYN-24 — Artifact injection crashes for custom wavelength grids of ≤20 points (realistic/complex presets)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: A seeded realistic generator with 16 channels and 500 samples raises high <= 0 in dead-band injection. This is probability dependent; not every small batch fails.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:905` (`dead_band`), `generator.py:896` (`spike`, needs ≥3 points)
- Category: crash
- Description: `self.rng.integers(0, self.n_wavelengths - 20)` requires `n_wavelengths > 20`; with `artifact_prob` of 0.02 (realistic) / 0.05 (complex) any run with enough samples eventually hits a `dead_band` draw.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t21.py`): `SyntheticNIRSGenerator(wavelengths=np.linspace(1100, 2400, 16), complexity="realistic", random_state=0).generate(500)` -> `ValueError: high <= 0` ("simple" works).
- Impact: filter-photometer style grids with few channels crash randomly.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): clamp the band start/width to the available points or skip band artifacts for tiny grids.

##### SYN-25 — Target noise can exceed the requested base range; correlation depends on the separate noise setting

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: With requested range=(5, 50), default noise yields a minimum of 4.108 because noise is added after scaling. The correlation formula also multiplies by the separate noise parameter. Distinguish a base-target range from an asserted bound on final noisy targets; a strict final bound requires final scaling/clipping.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/targets.py:164-169`, `targets.py:354-369` (`_add_controlled_noise`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: With requested range=(5, 50), default noise yields a minimum of 4.108 because noise is added after scaling. The correlation formula also multiplies by the separate noise parameter. Distinguish a base-target range from an asserted bound on final noisy targets; a strict final bound requires final scaling/clipping.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t5.py`): `regression(500, C, component=0, range=(0, 100))` -> min -0.87, max 90.63; `range=(5, 50)` -> min 4.11, max 45.28; `correlation=0.5, noise=0.5` -> achieved correlation 0.78.
- Impact: The final noisy targets need not satisfy a strict bound on the base range; requested correlation also depends on noise, requiring a clarified contract.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Clarify base range versus final noisy bounds; offer final scaling/clipping only if a strict final-bound contract is desired.

##### SYN-26 — Single-detector simulation over a multi-sensor grid may inflate out-of-band noise

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: One detector simulator is constructed from the instrument primary detector and applied to the full grid. Out-of-band zero response inflates the noise multiplier, capped at ten. Initial measured noise ratios were not freshly repeated; the multi-sensor physical interpretation deserves review.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/generator.py:351-359`, `nirs4all/synthesis/detectors.py:71-78,415-424`
- Category: numerical
- Description: One detector simulator is constructed from the instrument primary detector and applied to the full grid. Out-of-band zero response inflates the noise multiplier, capped at ten. Initial measured noise ratios were not freshly repeated; the multi-sensor physical interpretation deserves review.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above) (`t14.py`, detector applied to a zero spectrum): `foss_xds` (SI): 65 % of the grid has zero response, noise std 0.0059 there vs 0.0014 in-band (x4.1); `metrohm_ds2500` (PBS) 29 % of the grid, x4.7; `asd_fieldspec` 23 %, x4.7.
- Impact: A multi-sensor spectrum may receive a single-detector out-of-band noise multiplier; numerical noise ratios and physical interpretation are not freshly validated here.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): evaluate the response per sensor range (use the best-covering detector per wavelength).

##### SYN-27 — Realism scorecard: failed metrics are dropped from `overall_pass`; benchmark TSTR/TRTS errors are swallowed

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Metric exceptions append warnings without a failed MetricResult. all(r.passed) excludes unevaluated gates and can pass vacuously. Benchmark TSTR/TRTS catches exceptions without surfacing a diagnostic.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/validation.py:800-947` (per-metric `try/except`), `validation.py:951`, `validation.py:920`, `validation.py:1073-1074`
- Category: error-handling
- Description: when a metric computation raises, only a warning string is appended and no `MetricResult` is added; `overall_pass = all(r.passed for r in metric_results)` then ignores it (SNR even sets `snr_match = True`). `validate_against_benchmark` ends with `except Exception: pass`, so e.g. multi-output synthetic targets (5 columns) vs a 1-column benchmark (`r2_score` shape error) silently yield `tstr_r2=None`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): reading — e.g. real data with fewer than 5 samples makes `compute_adversarial_validation_auc` raise in `cross_val_score`, the scorecard returns `overall_pass=True` with only a warning.
- Impact: "PASS" can be reported although a quality gate could not be evaluated.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): add a failed `MetricResult(passed=False)` in each `except`, and surface TSTR/TRTS errors in `warnings`.

Export include_headers/compression and compute_snr noise_region_fraction have no behavioral reads; procedural library generation randomizes fixed configuration fields. These independent subclaims should be separated when fixed.
