##### SYN-29 — Fitting helpers consume global NumPy random state without a local seed API

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: Two fit paths use global np.random.choice/normal, consuming global state and limiting reproducibility. No random_state contract exists, and output differences between identical fits were not freshly demonstrated; do not claim every fit necessarily changes.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/synthesis/fitter.py:1039` (`_compute_km_linearity`), `nirs4all/synthesis/fitter.py:4279` (`RealBandFitter.fit` restarts)
- Category: concurrency
- Description: Two fit paths use global np.random.choice/normal, consuming global state and limiting reproducibility. No random_state contract exists, and output differences between identical fits were not freshly demonstrated; do not claim every fit necessarily changes.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): reading of the two lines; `RealDataFitter.fit(X)` for X with >100 rows and reflectance-like values can change `kubelka_munk_linearity` between calls.
- Impact: Fitting consumes global RNG state and lacks a local seed contract; output nondeterminism for every identical fit is not established.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Introduce a local seed/RNG contract if reproducible fitting is required; do not infer nondeterminism for every input from global draws alone.

### 5.8 Visualization, analysis, CLI

#### Visualization, analysis and CLI (VIZ)

**Re-audit coverage (targeted, 2026-10-05)**

All 25 original VIZ claims were reviewed. PipelineDiagram parsing, dimensions and edge endpoints received targeted inspection and numeric/text-only witnesses; VIZ-26 through VIZ-28 are new. CLI shared-artifact purge was reviewed without deleting any real workspace. Preset precedence (VIZ-21) is documented and is rejected as a functional bug.

##### VIZ-01 — Top-K charts pick/order models by the DISPLAY (test) partition, not the ranking partition

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: TopK and confusion-matrix charts truncate [:k] after Predictions.top unconditionally resorts by display metric(test). Data auditor independently checked Phase9, including aggregate_partitions=True. Distinct from other Predictions.top defects.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/visualization/charts/top_k_comparison.py:162` (`TopKComparisonChart.render`), `nirs4all/visualization/charts/confusion_matrix.py:160` (`ConfusionMatrixChart.render`); root cause `nirs4all/data/predictions.py:1295` + `:1584-1609` (`Predictions._resort_by_display_metric`)
- Category: data-leakage
- Description: The charts request `top(n=1, group_by=['model_name'], rank_partition='val', display_partition='test')` (best-val entry per model) and then keep `[:k]`. `Predictions.top` finally re-sorts the returned list by the rank metric *as computed on the display partition* (test), so `[:k]` keeps the k models with the best TEST score, while the figure title says "Ranked by best rmse [val]". With k smaller than the number of models the test set decides which models are shown (and k=1 shows the wrong "best" model).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
# data/predictions.py:1594-1609 (_resort_by_display_metric): enriched_results.sort(key=r.get(effective_metric) [display-partition value])
# top_k_comparison.py:154-162: group_by=['model_name'] ...; top_predictions = top_predictions[:k]
$ viz4.py (5 PLS models, 3-fold CV):  best VAL rmse = {'PLS12': 0.506, 'PLS8': 0.56, 'PLS4': 1.208, ...}
plot_top_k(k=1, rank_metric='rmse', rank_partition='val', score_scope='cv') -> models plotted ['PLS8']
   suptitle: "Top 1 Models Comparison - Ranked by best rmse [val]"      # PLS12 has the best val rmse
top(...group_by=['model_name']) order = PLS8 (val 0.560, test 0.679), PLS12 (val 0.506, test 0.753)  # sorted by test rmse
```

- Impact: `analyzer.plot_top_k(k=N)` / `plot_confusion_matrix(k=N)` with more than N models silently selects the N best-on-test models (test leakage into model selection) and mislabels them as val-ranked; also the aggregated heatmap path (VIZ-02) keeps the "first occurrence" of the same re-sorted list.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): in the charts sort `top_predictions` by `rank_score` (the rank-partition score) before `[:k]`, or make `top()` keep rank order when `rank_partition != display_partition`.

##### VIZ-02 — Aggregated heatmap (`aggregate=...`) fills only ONE cell per row

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Aggregated Heatmap groups by y_var only, keeps first prediction for each y value, and writes its own x cell; other(x,y) combinations remain NaN. Query/group/matrix construction reviewed.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/visualization/charts/heatmap.py:1104` (`group_by_cols = [y_var]`), `:1136-1141` (keeps only the first prediction per y label), `:1199-1208` (`HeatmapChart._render_with_aggregation`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: With `aggregate` set, the heatmap queries `top(group_by=[y_var])`, keeps a single prediction per y-label (`y_var_to_best_pred[y_val_str] = pred`) and writes it into the single column given by that prediction's own `x_var`. All other (y, x) combinations stay NaN. (It also keeps the "first" after the test-order re-sort of VIZ-01.)
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ hm1.py   # 2 datasets (A,B) x 2 models, plot_heatmap("dataset_name","model_name", aggregate="y", score_scope="all")
fast path     : cells=['1.843 (n=5)','2.659 (n=5)','0.591 (n=5)','2.740 (n=5)']   # 4 cells
aggregated    : x=['A','B'] y=['pls8','pls2'] cells=['0.523 (n=30)','1.843 (n=30)']  # dataset B column completely empty
```

- Impact: every documented use `analyzer.plot_heatmap('model_name','preprocessings', aggregate='ID')` (or `aggregate=True`) with `x_var != 'partition'` produces a mostly empty heatmap in which each row shows only its single best (model/dataset/...) combination.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): do not collapse by y_var only; group by `[x_var, y_var]` (or `[y_var, x_var]`) so each cell gets its best aggregated prediction.

##### VIZ-03 — Polars score regex drops negative values and truncates scientific notation (heatmap / candlestick / histogram)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Polars metric regex reads -0.2 and1.5e-06 as null and1.5 instead of -0.2 and.0000015. Same pattern exists in three charts. Applies to secondary metrics; primary metrics use direct score columns correctly.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/visualization/charts/heatmap.py:538`, `nirs4all/visualization/charts/candlestick.py:191`, `nirs4all/visualization/charts/histogram.py:195`
- Category: correctness
- Description: Non-primary metrics are extracted from the `scores` JSON string with the regex `..."metric"\s*:\s*([\d\.]+)`. The class `[\d\.]` cannot match a leading `-`, an exponent (`e-06`, `e+03`) or `NaN`. Negative values (R² < 0, bias, negative MSLE...) become `null` and are filtered out; `1.5e-06` is read as `1.5`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
regex = '"test"\\s*:\\s*\\{[^}]*"mse"\\s*:\\s*([\\d\\.]+)'  on '{"test": {"mse": 1.5e-06,...}}','{"test":{"mse":0.25..}}','{"test":{...,"mse": 3.0e+03}}'
-> [1.5, 0.25, 3.0]          (true: 1.5e-06, 0.25, 3000.0)
r2 regex on '"r2": -0.2' -> [None, None, None]
$ hm3.py (pure-noise target, true test R2 = {'PLS6': -2.374, 'PLS10': -6.593})
analyzer.plot_heatmap("dataset_name","model_name",rank_metric="rmse",display_metric="r2")
-> ValueError: No valid scores found for dataset_name vs model_name        (heatmap.py:605)
```

- Impact: `plot_heatmap/plot_candlestick/plot_histogram(display_metric='r2')` (the documented example) on a regression run whose models are poor on some partitions silently omits those models/predictions (histogram/candlestick: biased distribution; heatmap: cell missing or crash when all negative). Small MSE/MAE values show wrong magnitudes.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use a numeric pattern `(-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?(?:[eE][-+]?\d+)?|NaN)` or, better, `pl.col('scores').str.json_path_match`/decode the JSON instead of regex.

##### VIZ-04 — `nirs4all workspace query-best` ranks ascending for the default `test_score` column → returns the WORST models for higher-is-better metrics

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: query-best derives ascending from the score-column name test_score, yieldingTrue, and has no descending flag. It uses column name rather than stored metric direction. Command and infer_ascending source checked.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/cli/commands/workspace.py:85` (`workspace_query_best`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `--metric` is a score COLUMN name (`test_score`, `val_score`, ...; default `test_score`), but the direction is derived with `infer_ascending(args.metric)`, which only knows metric names (`rmse`, `r2`, `balanced_accuracy`...) and returns `True` (ascending) for any unknown string. The `--ascending` flag can only force `True`, so a descending order is impossible.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
ascending = True if args.ascending else infer_ascending(args.metric)      # workspace.py:85
infer_ascending('test_score') -> True
$ qb2.py (classification workspace, metric balanced_accuracy)
CLI default order : LogisticRegression test_score 0.525, 0.55, 0.6, 0.625   # "Top 4" = worst
descending order  : RandomForestClassifier 0.75, 0.70, 0.70, 0.70           # true best
```

- Impact: for every classification workspace (and any r2/accuracy-ranked regression run) "Top N predictions by test_score" lists the worst predictions. The command also never passes `partition` (default `val`) so it mixes per-fold/avg rows of the val partition.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): derive direction from the row's `metric` column (or add `--descending`/`--partition`), not from the column name.

##### VIZ-05 — Transfer metrics: `centroid_distance` is always ~1e-16 (each dataset is centered and PCA-projected separately)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: For identical clouds with target=X+1000, centroid_distance is9.1855e-17 despite raw centroid offset norm3464.1. Independently centered PCA coordinates force this. Do not claim every other transfer metric has an identical mathematical defect; strongest evidence concerns centroid and coordinate comparisons in different bases.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/analysis/transfer_metrics.py:171` (`TransferMetricsComputer._pca`), `:185-189` (`_centroid_distance`), `:98-99` (`compute`); consumers `nirs4all/analysis/selector.py:386-390` (`improvement_pct`), `nirs4all/analysis/transfer_metrics.py:422-428` (`compute_transfer_score`); same defect in `nirs4all/visualization/analysis/transfer.py:34` + `:211`/`:231` (`PreprocPCAEvaluator`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: Each of source/target is mean-centered and projected on its OWN PCA basis (`Z = pca.fit_transform(X - X.mean)`), so both score clouds have zero mean by construction. "Euclidean distance between dataset centroids in PCA space" is therefore ~0 for any pair of datasets, whatever their offset. `improvement_pct`, the 0.40-weight centroid term of `compute_transfer_score` (always takes the `raw_centroid < eps` branch → 0.0), and `PreprocPCAEvaluator.cross_dataset_df_["centroid_*"]` are noise. The other cross-dataset metrics (CKA/RV/spread on separate bases, Procrustes on position-paired rows) have the same "different basis" problem.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ tm.py   Xs=randn(60,100); Xt=randn(50,100)+shift
shift 0.0 centroid_distance 9.75e-16 | shift 10.0 5.64e-16 | shift 1000.0 5.60e-16
$ impr.py  selector.fit(Xs, Xt+5.0) preset 'fast'
raw centroid_distance: 2.3e-16 ; area_norm improvement_pct 0.00023 ; savgol_d2 0.00022
$ trust3.py  PreprocPCAEvaluator on {"A": randn, "B": randn+5.0}: centroid_dist_raw 2.2e-16, centroid_dist_pp 1.2e-16
```

- Impact: `TransferPreprocessingSelector` reports meaningless `improvement_pct`, and 40 % of its default composite score is a constant 0, so ranking is decided only by the weaker covariance-spectrum metrics; users selecting preprocessings for instrument transfer get misleading "improvements"/plots ("Distance Reduction (%)").
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): fit ONE PCA basis (e.g. on source, or on the stacked data) and project both datasets onto it before computing centroid/spread/Procrustes metrics; do not re-center each dataset separately.

##### VIZ-06 — `compute_transfer_score`: operator-precedence bug makes the "spread improvement" ignore the raw baseline

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: Composite score with only spread weight, raw spread2 and preprocessed.5, returns-.5 instead of+.75. Conditional-expression precedence makes relative-improvement branch unreachable in the usual case.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/analysis/transfer_metrics.py:436` (`compute_transfer_score`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `spread_improv = -abs(spread) if spread > eps else 0.0 if raw_spread < eps else (raw_spread - spread)/(raw_spread + eps)` parses as `A if c1 else (B if c2 else C)`. Whenever the preprocessed spread is > 1e-10 (always in practice), the result is `-abs(spread)`; the relative-improvement branch C is unreachable. The term becomes an absolute penalty clipped to [-1, 0].
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ score.py  raw spread = 2.0, weights = spread only
pp spread 0.5 (75 % better) -> -0.5 ; 1.0 -> -1.0 ; 2.0 (unchanged) -> -1.0 ; 4.0 (2x worse) -> -1.0 ; 8.0 -> -1.0
```

- Impact: the transfer score never rewards a spread reduction, and any preprocessing with spread >= 1 gets the same minimum contribution as one that doubled it, so ranking among preprocessings is wrong/tied for the spread component.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): mirror the centroid logic (`if raw_spread < eps: ... else (raw_spread - spread)/(raw_spread + eps)`), parenthesised explicitly.

##### VIZ-07 — Stage-3 augmented results store `None` transforms; `to_preprocessing_list()` returns pipelines containing `None`

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Stage3 resolves a composite name with preprocessings.get, producingNone; TransferResult.get_transforms deepcopies it. Stage4 rebuild drops transforms. Producers and consumers confirmed by source; no fresh stage3 runtime run.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/analysis/selector.py:327-330` (`_parallel_evaluate_preprocessings.evaluate_single`, augmented branch); consumers `nirs4all/analysis/results.py:94-95` (`TransferResult.get_transforms`), `:151-200` (`to_preprocessing_list`); also `selector.py:1116-1124` (stage 4 rebuilds results without `transforms`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Stage 3 builds augmentation names from the top-K of ALL previous results, including stacked names such as `"d1>detrend"`. `actual_transforms` resolves each component with `self.preprocessings.get(c)`, which returns `None` for a stacked name, and the list is stored on the result. `get_transforms()` returns the stored list as-is (`[deepcopy(None), ...]`), so the stacked component is lost. After stage 4 `transforms` is dropped, and resolving `"d1>detrend"` by name raises `ValueError`, which `to_preprocessing_list` swallows, silently removing the result.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ stage3.py  preset=None, run_stage2=True, run_stage3=True (5 base preprocessings)
augmented results with None transform: [('d1>detrend+d1', ['d1>detrend','d1'], [None, FirstDerivative()]),
                                        ('d1>detrend+detrend>d1', [...], [None, None]), ...]
to_preprocessing_list(top_k=39): 27 of 39 pipelines contain None
```

- Impact: `results.to_preprocessing_list()` — the advertised hand-off into `feature_augmentation` — yields invalid pipelines (`[None, MSC()]`, `[None, None]`) for most stage-3 winners, and drops others after stage 4.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): resolve stacked component names (split on `>`) into transform lists when building `actual_transforms`, and keep `transforms` when stage 4 re-creates results.

##### VIZ-08 — Trustworthiness implementation disagrees with the standard definition (neighbour off-by-one + factor 2)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced**
- Re-audit result: For n60,k5, custom trustworthiness=.388974 versus sklearn=.709679. kneighbors without X already excludes self; slice1:k+1 skips nearest neighbor, and /2 normalization doubles penalty. No universal exact-equivalence assertion for tied-distance datasets.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/analysis/transfer_metrics.py:293-298` (`TransferMetricsComputer._trustworthiness`); duplicate in `nirs4all/visualization/analysis/transfer.py:75-79` (`PreprocPCAEvaluator._trust`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: `NearestNeighbors.kneighbors(return_distance=False)` without `X` already excludes the query point, yet the code slices `idx[i, 1:1+k]`, skipping the true nearest neighbour; and the normaliser `Z = n*k*(2n-3k-1)/2` carries an extra `/2`, doubling the penalty (`2/Z` instead of `2/(n k (2n-3k-1))`).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ trust2.py / trust3.py  n=60, k=5, X random, E = X[:, :3] + noise
noise 0.3: sklearn.trustworthiness 0.6923  vs nirs4all 0.4200
noise 1.0: 0.6465 vs 0.2595 ; noise 5.0: 0.5315 vs 0.0460
(re-implementation with slice [0:k] and without the "/2" reproduces sklearn exactly: 0.6923 / 0.6465 / 0.5315)
```

- Impact: the `trustworthiness` column of `TransferSelectionResults.raw_metrics`/`metrics` and `PreprocPCAEvaluator.df_` (also fed into the "quality convergence" and "preservation summary" plots) is systematically too low and can go negative.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): use `idx[i, :k]` and `Z = n*k*(2n-3k-1)` (or call `sklearn.manifold.trustworthiness`).

##### VIZ-09 — Artifact CLI uses the wrong layout; a guarded purge can delete every dataset’s shared artifacts

- Re-audit verdict: **QUALIFIED — initial scope or interpretation corrected**
- Re-audit result: stats/cleanup/list guards use binaries whereas registry uses shared sharded artifacts. Purge ignores dataset and recursively deletes the shared artifact tree, but starts only if a top-level artifact file exists. Data-loss branch merits high severity instead of medium. Destructive source reviewed; no real workspace purged.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: source reviewed with qualifications; fresh runtime evidence where explicitly stated
- Location: `nirs4all/cli/commands/artifacts.py:33-40`, `:47-51`, `:98-102`, `:156-158` (guard on `workspace/binaries`), `:229-246` (`artifacts_purge`); `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/artifacts/utils.py:221` (`get_binaries_path` → `workspace/artifacts`), `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/artifacts/artifact_registry.py:1037-1040` (`find_orphaned_artifacts` lists only top-level files, `:1065` reads legacy `runs/*/manifest.yaml`)
- Category: other
- Description: stats/cleanup/list guards use binaries whereas registry uses shared sharded artifacts. Purge ignores dataset and recursively deletes the shared artifact tree, but starts only if a top-level artifact file exists. Data-loss branch merits high severity instead of medium. Destructive source reviewed; no real workspace purged.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ art_repro.py  ws/artifacts/ab/dsA_model.joblib, ws/artifacts/cd/dsB_model.joblib
--- stats / list-orphaned / cleanup --force: [info] No artifacts found (binaries/ directory does not exist)
--- purge dsA: [info] No artifacts to purge for dataset 'dsA'         (files untouched)
# add ws/binaries/dsA and a stray ws/artifacts/stray3.joblib, then: purge -d dsA --force --yes
[success] Purged 3 artifacts for dataset 'dsA'   # includes dsB_model.joblib (other dataset)
```

- Impact: Maintenance guards miss shared sharded artifacts; when the purge branch is reached, a dataset-scoped request can delete other datasets’ model files.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Base dataset-scoped maintenance on WorkspaceStore artifact references and protect artifacts belonging to other datasets.

##### VIZ-10 — `nirs4all --test-integration` exits 0 ("Basic pipeline functionality is working") when the real pipeline tests fail

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: TF/Optuna skips returnTrue; success_count>0 makes integration returnTrue even if core sklearn pipeline fails, and main exits0. Current control flow checked.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/cli/installation_test.py:324` and `:369` (skipped optional tests `return True`), `:457-467` (`test_integration`)
- Category: error-handling
- Description: A skipped TensorFlow/Optuna test counts as a pass in `success_count`, and any `success_count > 0` returns `True` ("Return True for partial success"). So with TensorFlow missing, a failing sklearn pipeline (the only mandatory one) plus a failing Optuna pipeline still yield exit code 0.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ integ_fp.py   (PipelineRunner.run patched to raise; tensorflow import blocked)
FAIL Sklearn Extended Pipeline ... Error: pipeline execution is broken
PASS TensorFlow Pipeline (NICON): 0.00s          # skipped
FAIL Optuna Extended Pipeline ...
[warning] Partial success: 1/3 tests passed
[success] Basic pipeline functionality is working
RETURN VALUE: True   ->  main(): sys.exit(0)
```

- Impact: CI/installers using `nirs4all --test-integration` as a smoke test get false positives.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): do not count skips as passes; return `False` unless the core sklearn pipeline test passed.

##### VIZ-11 — `nirs4all dataset validate` reports every validator warning (e.g. missing data files) as "[E204] File encoding issue" and then says "Configuration is valid"

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Warning loop discards original code/message and emits E204 encoding with empty encoding. Confirmed source defect. The Configuration valid result reflects validator warning policy and is not independently proof that validation is incorrect.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/cli/commands/dataset.py:69-75` (`dataset_validate`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: `for _ in result.warnings:` discards each warning's code/message and records a hard-coded `ErrorRegistry.E204` (encoding fallback) with `encoding=""`. `--check-files` (default True) therefore surfaces "file not found" as an encoding message of the config file itself; MIXED_FORMAT, UNKNOWN_SIGNAL_TYPE, UNUSED_AGGREGATE_METHOD are mislabelled the same way.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ cfg.yaml: train_x: does_not_exist_Xtrain.csv ; train_y: does_not_exist_Ytrain.csv
nirs4all dataset validate cfg.yaml
Warnings (2):
  ⚠ At cfg.yaml: [E204] File encoding issue: cfg.yaml. Using fallback encoding:
✓ Configuration is valid        (exit 0)
```

- Impact: users get a misleading diagnosis (an encoding problem on the config) and no hint which referenced file is missing.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): emit a diagnostic that carries the `ValidationWarning.code/message/field` (e.g. a dedicated warning code) instead of E204.

##### VIZ-14 — Heatmap model-name filters fail for mixed or upper case values

- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ hm2.py  plot_heatmap("dataset_name","model_name", ..., model_name="PLS8")
-> figure text: 'No predictions found for x=dataset_name, y=model_name, metric=rmse'
```

##### VIZ-15 — `score_scope` (default `'refit'`) is ignored by the non-aggregated heatmap / candlestick / histogram paths

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**

- Category: api-contract
- Description: The Polars fast paths take every stored row (fold, `avg`, `w_avg`, `final`) and never look at `score_scope`; only the `aggregate=` path forwards it to `top()`. The analyzer documents/defaults `score_scope='refit'`.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ hist.py   (entries by scope -> refit: 0, cv: 25)
plot_histogram(score_scope='refit') title: "... 30 predictions"
plot_histogram(score_scope='cv')    title: "... 30 predictions"      # identical, includes folds+avg+w_avg+final
heatmap cells: '(n=5)' per model x dataset (3 folds + avg + w_avg), refit rows excluded only because their val_score is NULL
```

- Impact: the same `PredictionAnalyzer` call gives different populations depending on whether `aggregate` is set; "refit only" distributions/heatmaps actually mix CV folds and ensembles (avg/w_avg double-count the same predictions).

##### VIZ-16 — Confusion-matrix tick labels are matrix indices, not class labels

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: sklearn confusion_matrix uses sorted actual class labels, while chart ticks use arange(matrix_size). Missing/string/noncontiguous classes are mislabelled. Current rendering path checked.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/visualization/charts/confusion_matrix.py:213` (`sk_confusion_matrix(y_true, y_pred)` without `labels=`), `:225-228` (`ax.set_xticklabels(tick_marks)`) (`ConfusionMatrixChart.render`)
- Category: correctness
- Description: Axis ticks are `range(n_classes)` of the *present* labels. If a class is absent from both `y_true` and `y_pred` of the displayed partition (typical for a small multiclass test set) the matrix is smaller and every label after the gap is shifted.
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ cm.py  3 classes {0,1,2}, test partition contains only classes 0 and 2
classes in test y_true: [0, 2]
x tick labels: ['0', '1']  y tick labels: ['0', '1']      # class 2 is labelled "1"; matrix shape (2, 2)
```

- Impact: multi-class confusion matrices mislabel classes whenever a class is missing from the displayed partition (and ordering is wrong for string/non-0..K-1 labels).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): compute `labels = np.union1d(y_true, y_pred)`, pass `labels=labels` and use them as tick labels.

##### VIZ-17 — `BranchAnalyzer.summary/compare/rank_branches` count every partition row (3x inflated n, inflated significance)

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: BranchAnalyzer gets predictions without partition, then _collect_scores accepts test_score from train/val/test rows. This repeats fold values three times; avg/w_avg add correlated observations. Collection source checked.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/visualization/analysis/branch.py:606` (`_get_predictions` → `filter_predictions()` returns one row per fold AND partition), `:625-693` (`_collect_scores` falls back to the denormalised `{partition}_score` / `scores` of every row, regardless of the row's own partition); used by `:346`, `:425-436`, `:440`
- Category: correctness
- Description: Every stored fold has a train, val and test row, and each row carries the same `test_score`/`scores`. Requesting `partition='test'` still collects the test score from the train and val rows too (Try 2/3 do not check `pred['partition']`; only Try 4 does), so each fold's test score is counted ~3x (final: 2x) and the avg/w_avg ensembles are added as extra "samples".
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ (5 models, 3-fold CV + refit)   rows=85, test-partition rows=30
scores collected for partition='test': rmse -> 85, r2 -> 85
```

- Impact: `count`, mean/std, and especially `compare()`'s `n1/n2`, t-test p-values and Cohen's d use inflated sample sizes (p-values too small, "significant" too often).
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): restrict to rows whose `partition` equals the requested one (and to one fold level, e.g. folds only or final only).

##### VIZ-18 — `BranchAnalyzer.compare` Cohen's d uses population variance with (n-1) weights

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Cohen's d pooling multiplies(n-1)*var(ddof0) instead of sample variance. Arithmetic source confirmed, overestimating effects for small groups.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/visualization/analysis/branch.py:454-461`
- Category: numerical
- Description: `(n-1) * np.var(x)` uses `np.var` with `ddof=0`, so the pooled numerator is `(n-1)/n` of the correct `(n-1)*s²`. The pooled std is underestimated and the effect size inflated by `sqrt(n/(n-1))`-ish factors (22 % at n=3).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ br.py  n=3 per branch:  effect_size reported -0.1369, correct pooled Cohen d -0.1117
```

- Impact: effect sizes are overstated for small samples.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): `np.var(x, ddof=1)`.

##### VIZ-19 — Branch-comparison confidence intervals use population std with a t critical value

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: branch_summary uses std(ddof0), then plotting CI uses t(n-1)*std/sqrt(n). That interval estimator needs ddof1. Current source confirmed.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/visualization/predictions.py:1213-1214` (`branch_summary`: `np.std(scores)` → `_std`), `:1321-1322` (`plot_branch_comparison`: `t_crit * std / np.sqrt(n)`)
- Category: numerical
- Description: The half-width `t_{n-1} * s / sqrt(n)` needs the sample std (`ddof=1`); the code feeds `np.std` (`ddof=0`), so CIs are too narrow by `sqrt((n-1)/n)` (18 % at n=3).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above): `row[f'{metric}_std'] = np.std(scores)` (:1214) ... `errors.append(t_crit * std / np.sqrt(n))` (:1322).
- Impact: error bars in `plot_branch_comparison(show_ci=True)` are under-estimated.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): compute std with `ddof=1` for the CI.

##### VIZ-22 — Read-only CLI commands create a workspace store inside any existing directory

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — source-confirmed**
- Re-audit result: Workspace guard checks only path.exists then WorkspaceStore initializes store.sqlite/arrays/artifacts. Read commands can pollute an existing wrong directory. Current source confirmed; newer workspace inspect has a separate read-only path.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: confirmed by source/caller review; no fresh runtime proof claimed
- Location: `nirs4all/cli/commands/workspace.py:19-23` (`_validate_workspace_exists` only checks `exists()`), `:61`, `:85-92`, `:115`, `:141` and the tuning/conformal/robustness list/show commands (`WorkspaceStore(workspace_path)`)
- Category: other
- Description: `workspace stats|list-runs|filter|query-best|...` open a `WorkspaceStore`, which creates `store.sqlite`, `arrays/`, `artifacts/` when missing. A mistyped `--workspace` that points to an existing directory (e.g. the project root) is polluted instead of being rejected (legacy workspaces are protected by the conversion guard).
- Initial audit evidence (historical; fresh result is stated above):
```
$ mkdir emptydir; echo data > emptydir/notes.txt; nirs4all workspace stats --workspace emptydir
ls emptydir -> arrays/ artifacts/ notes.txt store.sqlite
```

- Impact: inspection commands have write side effects and report "0 predictions" for a wrong directory instead of failing.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): require an existing `store.sqlite` (or open read-only) for the non-`init` commands.

##### VIZ-28 — Transfer score gives full EVR credit when preprocessing preserves zero variance

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: The EVR ratio falls back to 1.0 when the preprocessed evr_source is below epsilon, even when raw EVR is positive. A zero value admitted by TransferMetrics is incorrectly scored as full preservation.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/analysis/transfer_metrics.py:441-442`
- Category: correctness
- Description: The EVR ratio falls back to 1.0 when the preprocessed evr_source is below epsilon, even when raw EVR is positive. A zero value admitted by TransferMetrics is incorrectly scored as full preservation.
- Evidence: Pure helper witness with finite metrics, raw evr_source=1, preprocessed evr_source=0 and only EVR weight=1 gives compute_transfer_score=1.0 instead of 0. Constant-matrix PCA may produce NaN earlier; this proof concerns the scoring helper.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Guard raw_evr first then ratio metrics.evr_source/(raw_evr+eps).

### 5.9 Previously missing execution, lifecycle and diagnostics audit

Targeted review covered PipelineRunner, orchestrator/executor/refit, step parser/router/runner, predictor/retrainer/resolver/engine and their public callers. EXE-01 through EXE-03 use the explicit legacy lane. EXE-04 through EXE-06 are shared analysis helpers used by Studio/workspace inspection; EXE-07 concerns topology inputs consumed by legacy refit. All five pipeline/analysis modules were additionally reviewed, including topology and splitter metadata callers. This fills the initially missing area without claiming every native or GPU workflow was executed.

##### EXE-01 — Legacy CV fits supervised feature selection on validation labels before splitting

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: The legacy executor fits a preprocessing step once on the full train partition, including all CV validation rows and their labels. The model controller later splits those transformed features. A supervised SelectKBest therefore sees validation labels before each fold score is computed. This is distinct from CTD-13 (held-out test rows in tag fitting) and CTD-08 (branch-group leakage).
- Fix disposition: Legacy major: eligible for a focused correction before deprecation; no implementation in this audit.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path.
- Severity: high
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/controllers/transforms/transformer.py:276,319,559-565`; `nirs4all/pipeline/execution/executor.py:644-723`
- Category: data-leakage
- Description: The legacy executor fits a preprocessing step once on the full train partition, including all CV validation rows and their labels. The model controller later splits those transformed features. A supervised SelectKBest therefore sees validation labels before each fold score is computed. This is distinct from CTD-13 (held-out test rows in tag fitting) and CTD-08 (branch-group leakage).
- Evidence: Fresh public legacy run on independent random targets: legacy CV RMSE 0.8888457746; manual global-selection CV 0.8888457589; sklearn fold-local preprocessing CV 1.3646315511. This demonstrates validation leakage on a noise-only fixture, without claiming native/dag-ml share it.

```python
from tempfile import TemporaryDirectory
import numpy as np
import nirs4all
from sklearn.feature_selection import SelectKBest, f_regression
from sklearn.linear_model import Ridge
from sklearn.model_selection import KFold, cross_val_predict
from sklearn.pipeline import make_pipeline

rng = np.random.default_rng(19)
X, y = rng.normal(size=(60, 1000)), rng.normal(size=60)
cv = KFold(3)
with TemporaryDirectory() as ws:
    with nirs4all.run([SelectKBest(f_regression, k=5), cv, Ridge(alpha=1)],
                      (X, y), engine="legacy", workspace_path=ws,
                      verbose=0, save_charts=False, refit=False) as result:
        print(result.cv_best_score)  # 0.8888457746
safe = cross_val_predict(make_pipeline(SelectKBest(f_regression, k=5),
                                      Ridge(alpha=1)), X, y, cv=cv)
leaked_X = SelectKBest(f_regression, k=5).fit_transform(X, y)
leaked = cross_val_predict(Ridge(alpha=1), leaked_X, y, cv=cv)
print(np.mean((safe-y)**2)**.5)    # 1.3646315511
print(np.mean((leaked-y)**2)**.5)  # 0.8888457589
```

- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Fit every learned preprocessing step inside the training subset of each CV fold; preserve a separate full-train fit for final refit. This is a major legacy issue, not a minor compatibility refactor.

##### EXE-02 — Legacy batched datasets are reported completed even when every dataset fails

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: For a DatasetConfigs batch with two or more datasets, per-dataset exceptions are always swallowed even when continue_on_error=False. The managed run is then marked completed, including when all datasets failed. Scope: direct legacy PipelineRunner / a preloaded batch; ordinary public run iterating single datasets is not established by this witness.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/execution/orchestrator.py:284-293,314-321`
- Category: error-handling
- Description: For a DatasetConfigs batch with two or more datasets, per-dataset exceptions are always swallowed even when continue_on_error=False. The managed run is then marked completed, including when all datasets failed. Scope: direct legacy PipelineRunner / a preloaded batch; ordinary public run iterating single datasets is not established by this witness.
- Evidence: Two valid 20×4 datasets with an intentionally invalid Ridge(alpha=-1) trigger model errors. PipelineRunner(continue_on_error=False) returns zero predictions, no exception and run status completed. The deliberate invalid estimator tests failure reporting, not acceptance of negative alpha.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Honor continue_on_error and persist a failed/partial status with dataset failures rather than reporting an all-failed run completed.

##### EXE-03 — Legacy transfer/finetune retraining leaves persisted run status running after completion

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: The transfer/finetune execution path begins a WorkspaceStore run but returns without complete_run; its exception path also never fails the store run. Full retraining through the orchestrator is a separate path.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/retrainer.py:622-627,692-717`
- Category: lifecycle
- Description: The transfer/finetune execution path begins a WorkspaceStore run but returns without complete_run; its exception path also never fails the store run. Full retraining through the orchestrator is a separate path.
- Evidence: Fresh StandardScaler/KFold(3)/Ridge legacy bundle retrained in transfer mode through a target-workspace session returns two prediction entries. Reopening the target store after session close still reports the retrain run running.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Finish or fail the owned WorkspaceStore run on both normal and exceptional exit. A lone success-status call does not fix exception handling.

##### EXE-04 — Learning-curve diagnostics underestimate K-fold training size

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: estimate_train_size treats a validation-fold size as if it were the training size. For K balanced folds, training size is val_size × (K−1); the implemented division instead returns val_size/(K−1). This shared helper is used by Studio when exact train arrays are absent and does not change model fitting itself.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/analysis/model_diagnostics.py:175-192`; `tests/unit/pipeline/analysis/test_model_diagnostics.py:82-84`
- Category: diagnostics
- Description: estimate_train_size treats a validation-fold size as if it were the training size. For K balanced folds, training size is val_size × (K−1); the implemented division instead returns val_size/(K−1). This shared helper is used by Studio when exact train arrays are absent and does not change model fitting itself.
- Evidence: Fresh helper witness: estimate_train_size(20, 5) returns 5, whereas a 100-sample five-fold split trains on 80 samples per fold. The existing unit test asserts the wrong result 5, so the passing unit gate does not validate the formula.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Use the K-fold relationship train_size = val_size × (K−1), update its docstring and replace the wrong unit expectation with an independent split-size witness.

##### EXE-05 — Static shape diagnostics mishandle valid PCA, cropping and resampling parameters

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: The pre-fit UI helper assumes integer PCA component counts, integer crop endpoints and resampler parameter names that differ from the actual operator. It raises on valid configurations or returns impossible/wrong feature counts. This is a shared editor/diagnostic contract; the actual operators work on the same fixtures.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/analysis/shape_inference.py:43-55,127-130`
- Category: diagnostics
- Description: The pre-fit UI helper assumes integer PCA component counts, integer crop endpoints and resampler parameter names that differ from the actual operator. It raises on valid configurations or returns impossible/wrong feature counts. This is a shared editor/diagnostic contract; the actual operators work on the same fixtures.
- Evidence: On 100×10 input, PCA(None) and PCA(mle) inference raises although the fitted operators produce 10 and 1 features; PCA(0.95) reports 0 versus actual 10. Crop(start=0,end=None) raises versus actual 10; Crop(start=3,end=100) reports 97 versus actual 7. ResampleTransformer(num_samples=4) reports 10 versus actual 4. Studio editor calls the helper without catching these exceptions.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Handle None and real slicing bounds, use the actual num_samples parameter, and return an explicitly unknown shape for data-dependent PCA modes rather than inventing an exact pre-fit count. Track these operator-rule subclaims separately when implementing.

##### EXE-06 — Workspace CV metadata extraction ignores canonical split-wrapper steps

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: PipelineConfigs legitimately serializes a split wrapper as {split: {class: ..., params: ...}}, but the CV metadata extractor only accepts top-level class references. The shared workspace read contract and Studio adapter therefore miss an authored CV setup that the runtime accepts.
- Fix disposition: Shared/current path: retain for normal triage; no implementation in this audit.
- Severity: low
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/analysis/splitter_config.py:114-126,151-155`; `nirs4all/pipeline/storage/workspace_read_contract.py:192-194`
- Category: diagnostics
- Description: PipelineConfigs legitimately serializes a split wrapper as {split: {class: ..., params: ...}}, but the CV metadata extractor only accepts top-level class references. The shared workspace read contract and Studio adapter therefore miss an authored CV setup that the runtime accepts.
- Evidence: Fresh PipelineConfigs([KFold(3), Ridge()]) extraction returns KFold/n_splits=3; PipelineConfigs([{split: KFold(3)}, Ridge()]) yields a nested canonical step and extraction returns None. Wrapped split syntax is used by both legacy and DAG controllers/tests.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Unwrap the canonical split step before extracting its class and parameters, while preserving predict-only and nonreconstructable-refit exclusions.

##### EXE-07 — Legacy refit topology omits bare estimators serialized as default class strings

- Re-audit verdict: **VALIDATED — freshly reproduced; new finding**
- Re-audit result: Bare estimators with all-default parameters become dotted class strings in canonical PipelineConfigs and stored expanded_config. The topology walker tests only live model instances in its non-dict branch, so these strings disappear from model_nodes. Legacy per-model refit selection consumes that topology. This witness establishes incomplete topology/selection inputs, not an end-to-end omitted-final-model result.
- Fix disposition: Legacy minor: mention only, no fix planned unless a verified local correction really fits in 1–2 lines without a refactor.
- Execution scope: explicit legacy/controller path.
- Severity: medium
- Confidence: reproduced in this re-audit
- Location: `nirs4all/pipeline/analysis/topology.py:166-190`; `nirs4all/pipeline/execution/refit/config_extractor.py:740-750`
- Category: topology
- Description: Bare estimators with all-default parameters become dotted class strings in canonical PipelineConfigs and stored expanded_config. The topology walker tests only live model instances in its non-dict branch, so these strings disappear from model_nodes. Legacy per-model refit selection consumes that topology. This witness establishes incomplete topology/selection inputs, not an end-to-end omitted-final-model result.
- Evidence: Fresh PipelineConfigs([KFold(3), Ridge()]) yields a dotted Ridge string and model_nodes=[]; Ridge(alpha=2) and {model: Ridge()} each yield one model node. Existing real legacy workspace configurations with bare Ridge also have zero model nodes. Current in-library callers are legacy orchestrator, per-model selection and stacking refit.
- Possible correction (reference; fix disposition takes precedence): Normalize supported canonical string references before topology inspection, preserving model/splitter distinction and the same metadata used by runtime parsing. Do not add a second unrelated class-name heuristic.

## 6. Fix policy for the next phase

No implementation was authorized for this audit phase. For the subsequent fixes, prioritize current native/dag-ml/shared paths with demonstrated corruption, leakage or data loss, then common valid-input failures. Use the revised per-item scope and canonical IDs.

Legacy is planned for deprecation. Keep every verified legacy mention for users and migration decisions, but do not schedule minor legacy refactors or compatibility work. Correct a legacy issue only when it is major, or when the whole verified repair genuinely fits in one or two local lines. A partial two-line patch that leaves the same failure class unresolved does not meet that exception. Validate whichever targeted fix is chosen; do not broaden legacy architecture or introduce implicit fallback.

Reject DGB-04 and VIZ-21 as repair tasks; consolidate the six duplicates; decide the qualified contract/statistical items before changing semantics. Add regression coverage with the later fixes for demonstrated scientific/data-integrity defects. Review QA-01’s public API snapshot expectations explicitly rather than blindly regenerating goldens. Dependency installation, unrelated working-tree failures and untested optional backends remain separate from this repair queue.
